#5060. How to Buy Bitcoin With a Credit Card Safely: Step-by-Step Guide

We review everything we recommend independently. This article contains affiliate links from which we may earn commission.

Home » Cryptopedia » How to Buy Bitcoin With a Credit Card Safely: Step-by-Step Guide

Purchase your first BTC in under 10 minutes while avoiding bank blocks and fees.

Buying Bitcoin with a credit card is the fastest way to go from zero to owning crypto—often taking less than ten minutes from account creation to holding BTC in your wallet. While bank transfers can drag on for days, swiping your Visa or Mastercard gives you instant market exposure.

However, convenience comes with a catch. Not all crypto exchanges accept credit cards, some banks block crypto transactions outright, and high “cash advance” fees can silently eat into your investment if you don’t know what to look for.

Whether you are making your very first crypto purchase or simply want the quickest checkout method available, this step-by-step guide walks you through the exact process, breaks down the real costs, and shows you how to avoid rookie mistakes.

To keep the process smooth and avoid getting stuck mid-transaction, gather these four essentials before opening an account:

Follow these five straightforward steps to complete your purchase safely.

You cannot buy Bitcoin directly through your bank’s app. You need a regulated crypto exchange that acts as a bridge between your credit card and the Bitcoin network. Look for platforms with strong security track records, transparent fee structures, and instant card processing.

Top beginner-friendly platforms that support credit cards include:

Once you pick a platform, sign up using your email address and create a strong, unique password. Immediately enable Two-Factor Authentication using an authenticator app (like Google Authenticator) rather than SMS text messages, which are vulnerable to SIM-swap attacks.

Next, complete the identity verification process:

Most major exchanges automate this process, approving your account within 2 to 5 minutes.

Navigate to your account’s Payment Methods or Billing settings and select Add a Credit/Debit Card. Enter your 16-digit card number, expiration date, CVV security code, and billing address.

Pro tip: Make sure the billing address on your exchange account matches your credit card statement word-for-word. Even a minor mismatch will trigger an automatic fraud decline.

Head to the Buy Crypto section, select Bitcoin (BTC) as the asset you want to receive, and choose your linked credit card as the payment method.

Enter the amount you want to spend in your local currency (e.g., $100 or €100). Before hitting confirm, pause on the order preview screen and check two numbers:

Once you confirm the order, the exchange charges your card and credits your Bitcoin balance immediately.

Congratulations—you officially own Bitcoin! Right now, your BTC is sitting in your exchange’s custodial wallet. If you only bought a small amount (50–200) to get started, leaving it on a major, regulated exchange is fine while you learn the ropes.

However, if you plan to hold significant value long-term, transfer your Bitcoin to a self-custody wallet (such as a Ledger or Trezor hardware wallet, or a reputable mobile wallet like BlueWallet).

In crypto, holding your own private keys ensures that nobody—not even the exchange—can freeze or lose your funds.

While credit cards offer unmatched speed, they are the most expensive way to buy Bitcoin. Keep these three financial realities in mind so you aren’t surprised by your next bank statement:

The Golden Rule: Never buy Bitcoin on credit if you cannot pay off the card balance in full before your next billing cycle. Paying 25% interest on a volatile asset is a quick way to lose money even if Bitcoin’s price goes up.

Buying Bitcoin with a credit card has its advantages and drawbacks. Let’s take a look at them:

It’s essential to consider these factors before deciding to buy Bitcoin with a credit card. First, understand the fees, risks, and financial situation.

Understanding how to buy Bitcoin with a credit card gives you an easy, fast, and convenient way to get started in cryptocurrency.

It’s a crucial step towards participating in the digital economy, diversifying your investment portfolio, and potentially reaping significant gains from crypto market movements.

Plus, with the information you’ve gained today, you can navigate this process on popular platforms like Kraken, eToro, and Uphold, making your journey into crypto investing smoother and more secure.

Usually, no. Most regulated exchanges reject prepaid Visa or Mastercard gift cards because they cannot verify the cardholder’s identity and billing address.

Yes. You do not need tens of thousands of dollars to buy Bitcoin. BTC is divisible into 100 million smaller units called satoshis, allowing you to buy as little as $5 or $10 worth.

Yes. Many crypto exchanges support debit card purchases, and the process is generally similar to buying Bitcoin with a credit card. Availability and fees depend on the exchange and your country.

Your card may be declined because the exchange does not support your card or country, your bank blocks cryptocurrency transactions, additional verification is required, or the transaction exceeds your card’s limits.

Buying Bitcoin with a credit card does not normally affect your credit score by itself. However, using a large portion of your available credit or missing card payments can affect your credit profile.

#5059. Binance to Temporarily Suspend All Crypto Withdrawals on November 25: Reason

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

In a new announcement, Binance crypto exchange said it will perform wallet maintenance on November 25, 2026 at 06:00 (UTC). To support the wallet maintenance, Binance stated that withdrawals for all networks will be suspended beginning on November 25, 2026 at 06:00 (UTC). The recovery is estimated to take about 60 minutes.

The trading of tokens on Binance will not be impacted during the wallet maintenance. The exchange did not announce a corresponding suspension of deposits in the notice. Thus, the temporary suspension specifically concerns withdrawals across all networks.

In its notice, Binance said that withdrawal services will be resumed when the network is deemed to be stable and that no further announcement may be made.

Those looking to transfer funds should make their withdrawals before the wallet maintenance is set to begin to avoid delays and possible loss of funds.

Wallet maintenance refers to a standard operational process that supports blockchain updates, system improvements, and fund security.

This comes weeks after Binance suffered an historic hack with $6.3 billion in assets affected. Binance has since completed coverage of assets impacted in the security incident and published a new Proof of Reserves snapshot.

In a recent development, Binance stated it has engaged leading security firms Mandiant, SlowMist and Verichains for independent reviews of its security procedures.

These reviews, the exchange said, will validate its current security infrastructure, assess its security posture going forward, and help strengthen the protections expected from the crypto platform.

Binance also stated that it was coordinating closely with the U.S. Department of Justice, as well as with industry partners who have assisted in freezing assets impacted by the security incident in late October 2026.  The collaboration is necessary throughout the asset forfeiture process, with the goal of supporting the return of affected funds as efficiently as possible.

In separate news, Binance said it has integrated the Cboe Global Indices Feed into its platform, app and Binance AI, giving users access to real-time traditional market data. The initial rollout covers three of the world's most widely followed U.S. equity benchmarks, with more benchmark data to follow: S&P 500 Index, Dow Jones Industrial Average, Nasdaq-100.

#5058. Ripple’s $2.8 Billion Stablecoin Flips Telegram’s Gram After $1 Breakdown

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ripple's U.S. dollar stablecoin Ripple USD (RLUSD) has overtaken Gram (GRAM), the token previously known as Toncoin, in the CoinMarketCap ranking of cryptocurrencies by market capitalization.

According to CoinMarketCap data, RLUSD had a market capitalization of $2.82 billion on Tuesday and ranked No. 26, and Gram dropped to No. 27 with a market capitalization of $2.79 billion.

Gram fell below $1 and was trading at around $0.97 on Tuesday, down about 6% over the past seven days, according to CoinMarketCap. The 24-hour trading volume of Gram stood at around $47 million, and RLUSD had about $368 million in trading volume over the same period.

World Liberty Financial USD (USD1) also had a market capitalization of $2.82 billion on Tuesday and was one spot higher, at No. 25. Among stablecoins, Tether (USDT) and USDC still lead the list with $174.64 billion and $69.22 billion, respectively.

In late July, the market value of RLUSD was about $1.5 billion, according to RWA.xyz data. On Aug. 25, Ripple said that RLUSD had crossed $2 billion in market capitalization, with close to $1 billion of the supply issued on the XRP Ledger. By mid-September, RLUSD supply stood at $2.44 billion.

In late October, the U.S. Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) gave Ripple approval for Ripple National Trust Bank. A day later, on Oct. 27, Moody's Ratings assigned RLUSD an A2 long-term deposit rating and a P-1 short-term deposit rating. RLUSD is issued by Ripple subsidiary Standard Custody & Trust Company. Also in late October, RLUSD became available on Coinbase and Robinhood.

The Open Network renamed its token from Toncoin to Gram in June 2026. On July 21, Telegram founder Pavel Durov announced a native non-custodial Gram wallet for every Telegram app. "This summer will see the largest rollout of a non-custodial crypto wallet in human history. Instant zero-fee crypto transactions for over a billion users are about to become reality," Durov wrote.

This week, Telegram published the terms of service for Gram Wallet. According to the terms, the wallet is operated by RAZOR UNIVERSAL CORP., a Panamanian company, and Telegram provides the apps through which users access it. "Telegram does not operate Gram Wallet, does not hold your keys or assets, and is not a party to these Terms," the document says.

The terms also say that "neither we nor Telegram can freeze, seize, move, recover or reverse your assets." At the same time, according to the terms, "anyone who visits your Telegram profile may be able to see your associated Gram Wallet address," as well as the total balance of the wallet. Access to the wallet "may be restricted or unavailable in certain regions or to certain users," the document says.

#5056. $600K stolen from Trezor and Exodus wallets – How did it happen?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Onchain Lens reported over $600,000 lost from one user’s Trezor and Exodus wallets on the 23rd of November. The Trezor wallet theft report raised a harder question: how did transfers use the victim’s signing authority without their claimed approval?

The blockchain investigator listed approximately $580,000 in Tether [USDT] losses on TRON and $23,400 in USD Coin [USDC] on Solana. Another $9,500 reportedly disappeared from an Exodus wallet on the same iPhone 17 Pro Max.

Reportedly, the victim generated the recovery phrase on Trezor and kept it on paper and refused to allow anyone physical access to the device.

A 49,999 USDT transfer on the 21st of November used the TRON account’s default owner permission. It was a direct transfer, with no transfer from operation drawing on an existing token allowance.

On Solana [SOL], the examined instruction identified the victim’s wallet as the transfer authority, signer, and fee payer. Notably, no separate delegate appeared.

These transfers used the wallet’s signing authority. However, it is unclear how someone gained that access.

A valid signature authorizes a transaction on the network. So, it cannot establish whether the owner knowingly approved it. The Trezor accounts on both chains shared one recovery phrase, making that backup a possible link between the losses.

In an X reply, Trezor said similar cases usually involved “a compromised wallet backup.”

It’s hard to say what happened here without more details.

The company asked the affected user to contact official support. Its reply offered a possible explanation, without establishing this incident’s cause.

Trezor says its Bluetooth chip cannot access private keys. On the other hand, Exodus says its Trezor integration also leaves those keys inside the hardware. Whether the user enabled that integration remains unclear.

Apple’s 9th of November update patched Bluetooth issues, but Onchain Lens found no evidence linking those vulnerabilities to these transfers.

Earlier incidents show why identifying the route matters.

As AMBCrypto reported, Trezor confirmed implants in three CryptoBilis-sold Safe 5 devices on the 12th of November. Those implants captured recovery words from device screens.

Separately, Confiant’s MoldyRoost research documented another route; malicious ads delivering exploits to vulnerable iPhones. Furthermore, Chainalysis counted roughly 158,000 personal-wallet compromise incidents affecting 80,000 victims in 2025.

For this user, the unanswered question is specific: how did someone obtain signing access across the affected wallets?

#5055. HBAR Price Hits a Double-Bottom ‘Discount Zone’

Home » Altcoins » HBAR Price Hits a Double-Bottom ‘Discount Zone’

Big-timers are hedging the long-term accumulation zone after Hedera dropped below confluent resistance.

Hedera Hashgraph’s native HBAR coin is coiling within a macro ‘discount zone’, analyst Karl Moon notes. In his latest HBAR analysis based on the weekly crypto charts, HBAR has hit the major demand territory between $0.03525 to $0.0516, bouncing off to approximately $0.0555 very briefly.

$HBAR is entering the deep discount zone.Macro range lows are getting very close.This is where bulls need to start showing up. pic.twitter.com/zOfRD6DjuX

Next up, a confluent resistance area at $0.07 comes into play. From here, the OG altcoin may retrieve the $0.075 resistance before claiming the quarterly lows above $0.10. The support from big-time crypto investors looking for the bottom will be key: a positive CMF record is driving that trend switch.

As we can see in the HBAR price chart above, the altcoin is still trading inside the Bollinger Band envelope, slightly below the mid-tier Bollinger Band (BOLL) of $0.05166. Meanwhile, the Chaikin Money Flow (CMF) flipped to 0.07, hinting at crypto currency whales buying on the 4-hour time-frame.

Measured by the Bollinger Bands, the $0.0535 level described by Karl Moon is as the upper boundary of Hedera’s (HBAR) ‘discount zone’ perfectly coincides with the Red-Label Bollinger Band, typically intended to mark a bullish trend switch.

However, the Elliott Wave theory presents a dimmer picture. More Crypto Online’s trading team has established that a rebound above roughly $0.12 has been labeled as a Wave 4. The seasoned traders even expect HBAR to break below the 2024 lows.

hedera-hashgraph:native is approaching the 61.8% retracement at $0.046 and the 2024 low. There is no sign of a low yet, and more downside is likely, possibly including a break below the 2024 low. pic.twitter.com/rU6ZKIUURk

Price is now viewed as being in a Wave 5 (final downward impulse leg) of a dipping sequence.

The count anticipates more downside. There are no clear reversal signs yet. The first major test is the 61.8% Fibonacci level near $0.046. Given that Hedera’s native digital currency jumps back above the $0.0535 for good, the bearish thesis would be invalidated.

The technical view anticipates more downside, including a possible break below the 2024 low, based on wave patterns and lack of reversal evidence in the HBAR/USDT 4-day timeframe. Meanwhile, Futures participants are betting on an HBAR price bounce back.

The 24-hour liquidation stats also point to short-sellers taking in a higher deficit than HBAR’s bulls, accounting for $145.12K and $87.69K in excessively-leveraged position liquidations, per CoinGlass’ real-time data. While some long positions are unwinding, the scene’s still crowded.

Top trader accounts on major exchanges are mostly bullish, eager for this trend to continue.

Overall HBAR Futures volume grew by 83% since the day before, whilst the Open Interest (OI) slid by 4% to drop below $100 million. Bitcoin’s (BTC) ability to stay above $60K will contribute towards setting the near-term tone.

XRP has completed a short-term golden cross against Bitcoin, giving bulls a fresh technical signal as the cryptocurrency eyes a retest of $1.

The 50 MA rose above the 200 MA on the 3-hour chart, completing a golden cross, as XRP saw relative outperformance against Bitcoin. The timing of the golden cross remains surprising given the current selling in the market.

At the time of writing, XRP was down 5.83% in the last 24 hours to $0.923, giving back most of a rally that sharply took it past $1 after Jensen Huang returned as CEO of Nvidia.

On Monday, Nvidia shocked the markets with the surprise announcement of Jensen Huang returning as the CEO of the popular tech and AI company. For crypto traders, the initial response looked extremely encouraging as most tokens sharply rose. #5053. No CLARITY Act? How CFTC moves ahead with new crypto guidance

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Although the CLARTIY Act failed to pass the Senate this year, the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) is working hard to develop new crypto market advisories.

On the 23rd of November, the agency issued a new advisory allowing certain crypto assets to be counted towards the financial resource requirements of regulated derivatives trading and clearing platforms. This includes Bitcoin [BTC], Ethereum [ETH], and payment stablecoins.

So now, Swap Execution Facilities (SEFs), Designated Contract Markets (DCMs), and Derivatives Clearing Organizations (DCOs) can use these digital assets to confirm that they have sufficient financial resources to cover at least 12 months of operating expenses.

These requirements are designed to ensure that such platforms have enough funds to continue operating and meet their financial obligations, even during times of financial stress.

However, the approval comes with an important limitation. While eligible crypto assets can contribute towards the 12-month financial resources requirement, they cannot be counted towards the separate liquidity requirement. This is because, under Generally Accepted Accounting Principles (GAAP), cryptocurrencies are not classified as cash or highly liquid securities.

In simple terms, holding Bitcoin or Ethereum may help a platform prove its financial strength, but it cannot replace the cash or highly liquid assets it needs to meet immediate payment obligations.

The advisory also requires appropriate haircuts. In other words, the platforms must discount the value of crypto assets when calculating how much they can count towards their financial resources. This would be done to account for the price volatility and risks associated with digital assets.

Expressing their opinion on the matter, CFTC’s Chairman Mike Selig said,

Pleased to see @CFTC staff continue to provide important clarity around crypto assets in US derivatives markets through today’s staff advisory.

On the same day, though, the CFTC also introduced a new reporting framework for certain fully collateralized event contracts. This was done to replace the reliance on temporary regulatory exemptions and no-action letters that have been in place since 2017.

In other words, the move aims to make reporting requirements for event contracts, including prediction-market products, clearer and more consistent.

Yet, despite all the efforts, Senator Adam Schiff criticized the Trump-appointed CFTC Chairman over his handling of the legal battle surrounding prediction markets. Schiff claims that the chairman had previously told the Senate during his confirmation process that he would allow courts to decide whether prediction markets fall under gambling laws.

However, according to Schiff, the chairman has since acted against that commitment. Schiff believes that Selig has allegedly disregarded court orders and intervened in ongoing litigation in ways that favor the prediction-market industry.

His criticism suggests that the CFTC leadership is taking an active role in protecting prediction-market platforms rather than maintaining regulatory neutrality.

All of this coincided with the agency’s plans to use its existing authority to introduce two initiatives: Regulation Crypto Asset Transactions (CTX) and Regulation Crypto Asset Markets (CAM).

Ishika Kumari is a Crypto Analyst at AMBCrypto, specializing in regulatory developments, market dynamics, and blockchain’s real-world impact. She breaks down complex protocols and legislation into practical, easy-to-understand insights.

#5052. Cross River Bank enters Bitcoin-backed lending with $250K loans

The US bank is offering Bitcoin-backed loans ranging from $10,000 to $250,000 through LendingUSA, allowing borrowers to access cash without selling their holdings.

Cross River Bank and consumer financing company LendingUSA have launched a Bitcoin-backed lending program offering loans of up to $250,000, allowing eligible borrowers to access funds without selling their Bitcoin holdings.

According to a Monday announcement, borrowers can take out loans ranging from $10,000 to $250,000, using their Bitcoin (BTC) as collateral. They can borrow 30%, 50% or 70% of the value of their coins, depending on the loan terms. Eligible borrowers can either make interest-only payments during the loan term or postpone payments until the loan is due.

However, the loans carry risks if Bitcoin’s price falls. Borrowers could be required to provide additional collateral or repay part of their loans, with pledged Bitcoin subject to liquidation if collateral requirements are not met.

Bitcoin traded at $62,788 as of 4:13 p.m. UTC on Monday, up 3.03% over the past 24 hours but down 28.20% year-to-date, according to TradingView data.

The partnership marks Cross River’s first Bitcoin-backed lending program as a loan originator. Bitcoin pledged as collateral will be held by a qualified third-party custodian throughout the loan term.

Other Bitcoin-backed lenders include Ledn, which has offered crypto-backed loans since 2018, and Coinbase, which introduced Bitcoin-backed loans through Morpho in January 2025. US banks are also involved, including Column N.A., which originates some loans offered through Strike, and Fortis Bank, which participates in commercial lending arrangements with Unchained.

#5050. Grayscale Confirms Bitcoin’s Record Bear Market

Although Bitcoin has recently reclaimed the $60,000 price mark after seeing a sudden shift in market sentiments that triggered increases of over 6% in its price during the last day, momentum has slowed again, further stretching the prolonged volatility seen over the past months.

With the Bitcoin bear market still persisting, Grayscale has sparked discussions across the crypto market after drawing attention toward a major bearish milestone for the asset.

Earlier today, Grayscale revealed that Bitcoin's current bear market has lasted more than 411 days and is becoming the longest on record.

While this marks an historic milestone in the Bitcoin bear market, Grayscale further disclosed an interesting twist that makes the current bear market pretty different from previous times.

The source confirmed that this bear market has been relatively mild in terms of price losses despite lasting longer than previous downturns.

All through the current bear market, Bitcoin has only posted a price decrease 56%, suggesting that it has suffered considerably smaller losses than several previous bear markets.

This means that even though Bitcoin has spent more than a year in a bear market, its decline has not been as severe as those seen in earlier cycles.

To back the data, Grayscale showed charts comparing Bitcoin's performance across historical bear markets that ended in June 2011, December 2013, December 2017, November 2021, and the ongoing October 2025 cycle.

While the 2011, 2017 and 2021 bear cycles suffered massive losses, especially during the former when its indexed performance fell below 10, the current 2025 bear market broke historical pattern by enduring a longer period yet a comparatively modest decline in Bitcoin’s price.

#5049. XRP Hit 29 Cents in Kraken’s Canadian Market Sell-Off

Home » Trendsetter News » XRP Hit 29 Cents in Kraken’s Canadian Market Sell-Off

XRP traded at just 29 cents in Kraken’s Canadian-dollar market during a localized sell-off, raising questions about thin liquidity.

XRP reportedly traded at just 29 cents in Kraken’s Canadian-dollar market during a sharp, localized sell-off discussed by crypto commentator Crypto Sensei.

His video examines whether the trades reflected an exchange-specific liquidity problem rather than a broader XRP collapse, alongside falling ledger activity and disputed price forecasts.

According to the account presented in the video, 11 Canadian-dollar markets fell by at least 65%, with bitcoin trading below 18,000 and ether around 560.

The host cites November 20, 2026 as the incident date and reports that Kraken considered the orders correctly processed and would let the trades stand.

The analyst didn’t include trade records or Kraken’s full statement. Those limits matter: the reported 29-cent execution should not be confused with XRP’s prevailing price across exchanges.

Crypto Sensei attributes the dislocation to heavy selling meeting insufficient liquidity. Buyers may have secured steep discounts, while sellers received prices far below those available elsewhere.

“Whoever sold and just got totally wiped out here, I feel for you,” he says. The video does not establish whether individual losses resulted from discretionary sales, automated orders or liquidations.

A separate headline claiming the XRP Ledger had lost 60% of its users also draws scrutiny.

The underlying figure, according to the clarification cited in the YouTube video, was roughly a 63% decline in daily activity, not evidence that a comparable share of holders permanently departed or sold their XRP.

The host challenges Jake Claver’s repeated $100 year-end XRP forecast, arguing that institutional adoption is a longer process than such targets suggest. He also distinguishes Ripple’s business expansion from confirmed institutional use of XRP itself.

Former Ripple executive and Evernorth CEO Ashish Birla offers a longer-term XRP development case in a clip: Evernorth intends to deploy XRP on-chain, pursue yield and support ecosystem features rather than simply hold the asset. The video leaves the execution details and eventual returns unresolved.

For HODLers, these are separate questions: whether an exchange has enough liquidity, whether ledger usage is growing, and whether adoption creates demand for XRP. A dramatic trade print, or an ambitious treasury strategy, does not answer all three.

#5048. Just 8% of tokenized assets enter DeFi – Can Solana capture the remaining $45.5B?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

No doubt, tokenized equities have become one of the fastest-growing segments of the RWA market in 2026.

Binance Research shows that the entire sector’s market cap increased to $3 billion. That’s a rise of in excess of 4x since the start of the year. Against this backdrop, Solana’s momentum in tokenized equities stands out.

As AMBCrypto reported, Solana has seen its tokenized equities weekly spot trading volumes reach an all-time high of $1.8 billion. This represents 43% of all on-chain equities, marking the highest proportion since June. Furthermore, tokenized stocks issued by Backpack Securities generated $1.39 billion in trading volume on Solana last week, marking their biggest week yet.

In essence, Solana [SOL] is directly benefiting from the growing interest in tokenized equities.

As an example, during the first 24 hours of listing, Anthropic’s tokenized stock raised $134.7 million on Solana, some 2.6x higher than the debut trading volume recorded by tokenized SpaceX shares. Furthermore, it attracted over 15,000 investors, underlining the growing interest in investing in both public and blue-chip listed businesses via blockchain. However, this momentum could extend beyond spot trading.

According to on-chain data, tokenized assets have become one of the fastest-growing DeFi categories in 2026. The key question is therefore whether Solana will be able to leverage its growing tokenized equity momentum to further develop its DeFi network and capture a larger share of this emerging market.

One of the biggest opportunities for tokenized assets lies in DeFi.

The logic is simple: Investors can use these assets as collateral. This could help them back loans and allow borrowing too, hence increasing the practical application of tokenized assets. And while tokenized equities on DeFi have seen an impressive increase in value, rising by almost 1,800% within just one year ($14.9 million to $274 million), their overall adoption remains relatively low.

According to Token Terminal, the tokenized assets market is valued at $49.6 billion, but just $4.1 billion (8.2%) is currently utilized on DeFi platforms. This means that a large proportion of $45.5 billion is unused, highlighting the gap between tokenized asset growth and actual DeFi adoption.

Notably, this is where Solana’s growing tokenized equity activity becomes important.

According to on-chain data, Solana has a 32% stake in total tokenized equity TVL valued at $87 million. Ethereum is next at 20%. Thus, Solana is well placed to continue to attract investment into tokenized assets. The next step is to build on this momentum by bringing more of these assets into DeFi through lending, borrowing and collateral markets.

With this in mind, Solana has the potential to enhance liquidity within its network. It can also enable tokenized assets to be used as collateral. It can then seek to claim more of the $45.5 billion of ‘missed’ DeFi value. This, in turn, makes Solana’s tokenized stocks momentum key to underpinning its DeFi position.

Solana’s tokenized equity activity is growing, with its market share reaching 32%.

Only 8.2% of tokenized assets are used in DeFi, leaving Solana room to attract more capital.

#5046. Saylor Spots New Role for Bitcoin

Strategy Executive Chairman Michael Saylor sees new ways to put Bitcoin (BTC) to work on Wall Street.

According to staff at the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC), Bitcoin can be used to meet certain financial resource requirements for U.S. derivatives exchanges and clearinghouses.

‘Next step for adoption is using Bitcoin to work,’ Saylor wrote on X in response to the CFTC advisory published on Nov. 23.

As per the guidance, these companies must maintain sufficient financial resources to cover their operating costs for at least a year.

As clarified by CFTC staff, Bitcoin and Ether can be used to meet this requirement (as well as payment stablecoins). The advisory also provides guidance on how staff interpret the current rules. It also applies to swap execution facilities (SEFs), which are platforms where counterparties trade swaps.

Bitcoin holdings worth $10 million could count for $8 million toward the annual requirement, based on the minimum 20% discount that CFTC staff said they would accept. This provides some flexibility in case Bitcoin's price falls. The discount is also called a haircut.

For Ether, the same minimum 20% haircut applies. For payment stablecoins, the haircut is 2%.

This provides some incentive for an exchange to actually hold BTC. Exchanges could hold Bitcoin on their balance sheets and use some of its value to satisfy this reserve requirement. There would be no need to sell it for USD first.

However, a futures exchange still needs to have sufficient unencumbered cash or highly liquid securities to cover its operating costs for at least six months. Bitcoin, Ether and even payment stablecoins do not fall into this category.

The same six-month liquidity requirement applies to clearinghouses. Additionally, they need to maintain sufficient financial resources to cover a default by a clearing member.

CFTC staff also noted a potential downside for exchanges holding crypto reserves. During a market slump, there's a risk that an exchange's operating losses might rise. At the same time, the value of the Bitcoin it holds to cover those losses might fall.

So this possibility should be considered before an exchange decides whether to use Bitcoin to meet its financial resource requirements.

#5042. Strategy sells $176M in MSTR shares for STRC buybacks – What’s behind the shift?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Michael Saylor’s Strategy is making headlines, but there is something under the wraps that is raising eyebrows.

In its 23rd November Form 8-K filing, the Bitcoin [BTC] DAT revealed that the company is becoming more aggressive with its capital management strategy, particularly when it comes to its preferred stock, STRC.

In fact, between 16th and 22nd November, the company sold 1,925,676 shares of its Class A common stock (MSTR), generating $176.7 million in net proceeds.

But here’s a catch: instead of directing the entire amount towards Bitcoin purchases, Strategy used just $29.6 million to acquire 457 BTC while allocating the remaining proceeds towards repurchasing STRC shares.

At the same time, the company bought back 2,183,747 STRC shares for approximately $211.8 million during the same period, using $147 million from MSTR share sales and another $64.8 million from its USD cash balance. Naturally, this has raised an important question: why is Strategy selling one security to buy back another?

So, the difference lies in the roles of MSTR and STRC. MSTR, as we all know now, is Strategy’s common stock, offering investors exposure to its Bitcoin holdings and business performance. STRC, meanwhile, is a variable-rate perpetual preferred stock with a $100 stated value that pays regular dividends.

Therefore, Strategy aims to keep STRC trading around 99-100. When it trades below this range, buybacks can help support its market price while reducing outstanding shares and future dividend obligations.

This also explains the company’s expanded buyback capacity. On 23rd November, Strategy increased its Digital Credit Securities repurchase authorization from $3 billion to $4 billion, with approximately $1.12 billion remaining. At the same time, it continues to use its ATM program to raise capital by issuing MSTR shares.

Given such a change in plans, Strategy still continues to be the largest BTC holder, with a total of 851,305 BTC, acquired at an aggregate cost of $64.23 billion and an average purchase price of $75,446 per BTC.

All in all, the filing shows that Strategy is balancing Bitcoin accumulation with preferred stock buybacks rather than directing all its capital towards BTC purchases.

This comes as the MSTR stock was trading at $84.40 at press time up by 8.48% in the past 24 hours. Meanwhile, STRC was trading at $98.26 at press time up by 5.01% in the past trading day.

Lastly, AMBCrypto’s recent report also highlighted this shift, as its MSTR shares were facing mounting pressure from Bitcoin’s decline, losing over 40% in Q4 2026.

However, one thing is clear, and that is continued MSTR weakness could make fresh capital raises more difficult, threatening Strategy’s future Bitcoin accumulation plans.

#5038. Meme Coins Dive To $19B While Cap Share Shrinks To 3%

With BTC dipping below $60K, the downward slope carries onto the most fragile section of the altcoin compartment.

Right now, the picture is a lot different: the 3% share in late November testifies to long-term investors leaving the meme coin sphere. Some major crypto platforms have seen a massive imbalance between buying and selling positions.

📉 Altcoin market loses $408B while memecoin dominance collapses from 11% to 2%During this corrective phase, the altcoin sector has been hit the hardest.Since the last market peak in September, when some altcoins delivered strong performances, TOTAL2 has plunged by 34%,… pic.twitter.com/vHUPPSAOUf

The last ones left are the big dogs or timeless symbols of internet culture. Dogecoin (DOGE), Shiba Inu coin (SHIB) & PEPE Token (PEPE) have been equipped with stronger long-term liquidity due to the spontaneous community burns & continuous developer work.

#5037. XRP Ledger Volume Per Account Up 437% as Trades Get Bigger

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Each account trading on the XRP Ledger's decentralized exchange moved an average of 4,328 XRP a day in the third quarter of 2026, up 437% from 805 XRP in the same quarter a year earlier, according to a report that XRP treasury company Evernorth released on Tuesday.

According to Evernorth, the average swap on the XRP Ledger reached 35 XRP in the third quarter, up from 19 XRP in the second quarter and under 6 XRP a year earlier. "The growth came from bigger trades, not more of them," the company wrote in the report.

At the same time, the number of accounts trading on the XRP Ledger's order book fell to 1,042 a day in the third quarter of 2026 from 2,027 a day in the third quarter of 2025. The number stood at 1,203 in the fourth quarter of 2025, 1,324 in the first quarter of 2026 and 1,125 in the second quarter, according to the report.

Order-book volume on the XRP Ledger rose to 4.51 million XRP a day in the third quarter from 1.63 million XRP a day a year earlier, Evernorth data shows.

In total, 5.16 million XRP a day was traded on the XRP Ledger's exchange in the third quarter, and the figure was up 96% year over year, according to Evernorth.

Measured in U.S. dollars, exchange volume on the XRP Ledger fell 15% year over year in the third quarter of 2026, according to the report.

Evernorth also compared the XRP Ledger with Ethereum, BNB Chain, Solana, Base, Arbitrum, Avalanche and Polygon. Across these seven networks, exchange volume in dollars fell 42% over the same period, the report shows.

XRP was trading at around $0.93 on Tuesday morning after reaching as high as $1.026 on Monday, according to CoinMarketCap.

The report also shows that the average balance of Ripple's RLUSD stablecoin on the XRP Ledger reached $937 million in the third quarter of 2026, up from $539 million in the second quarter. By the end of the quarter, the RLUSD balance on the XRP Ledger stood at $1.15 billion, compared with $670 million at the end of the second quarter, and the number of RLUSD holders on the network rose to 67,449 from 45,620.

According to Evernorth, the XRP Ledger's average share of RLUSD reached 48.5% in the third quarter, up from 34% in the second quarter. Still, Ethereum finished the quarter with more RLUSD than the XRP Ledger. Evernorth noted that on Ethereum, RLUSD "can already be lent," and that on the XRP Ledger, "native lending is in validator voting."

As of Nov. 20, the Lending Protocol amendment had 24 of 35 validator votes, according to XRPScan. The amendment needs 29 votes, held for 14 days, to activate.

Evernorth completed its business combination with Armada Acquisition Corp. II on Oct. 9, and its shares began trading on Nasdaq under the ticker XRPN on Oct. 12. The company entered the public markets holding approximately 473 million XRP, according to its press release.

Evernorth announced the deal in October 2025, with investments from SBI, Ripple, Pantera Capital, Kraken and GSR.

#5035. Bitcoin developers propose fix for transaction verification gap affecting Lightning wallets

The proposal would let lightweight wallets independently verify transaction signatures recorded in mined blocks, advancing efforts to standardize witness proofs across Bitcoin software.

Bitcoin developers have proposed a new verification system that would allow lightweight wallets to independently authenticate transaction signatures recorded on the blockchain.

The draft Bitcoin Improvement Proposal, submitted by developer Jean Pablo on Nov. 22, addresses a limitation in how wallets verify transaction information received from external servers.

The proposal could particularly benefit wallets such as Electrum, which supports Bitcoin's Lightning Network and relies on external servers for some blockchain information.

Currently, lightweight wallets can verify that a transaction was included in a Bitcoin block using a cryptographic proof tied to its transaction ID, or txid.

However, that identifier excludes witness data, which contains signatures and other information used to authorize spending under Bitcoin's Segregated Witness (SegWit) system.

Consequently, a wallet could successfully verify a transaction's inclusion while holding witness data that differs from what miners actually included in the block.

Pablo's proposal addresses this discrepancy by introducing standardized witness transaction inclusion proofs built around a second identifier, known as the witness transaction ID, or wtxid.

Unlike the conventional transaction ID, the wtxid incorporates witness data, allowing wallets to verify that the full transaction they received matches the version included in a mined block.

The proposed system connects that identifier to Bitcoin's existing witness commitment, which is recorded in the block's coinbase transaction. A second cryptographic proof then connects the coinbase transaction to the block header.

An ordinary proof checks the transaction ID. The draft adds a path from the witness data to the same block header.

A wallet can compare the witness data it received with what a valid block committed to.

The proposed path assumes a valid block with a SegWit witness commitment. Chain selection and confirmation depth are separate from inclusion verification.

The diagram follows the two proof paths described in the draft and the wallet verification flow in the Bitcoin Core discussion.

The proposed path assumes a valid block with a SegWit witness commitment. Chain selection and confirmation depth are separate from inclusion verification.

Together, these checks allow wallets to authenticate witness data without downloading and examining the entire block.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The development follows an earlier request from Electrum contributor SomberNight, who sought witness inclusion proofs to strengthen transaction verification for the wallet's Lightning Network functionality.

Lightning wallets interact with Bitcoin's underlying blockchain when opening, closing and monitoring payment channels, making reliable transaction information particularly important.

The proposed verification system would allow such wallets to independently confirm the witness data supplied by servers while continuing to operate without storing complete blockchain data.

The request previously prompted work on Bitcoin Core, where developers explored extending existing transaction proof commands to cover witness information.

That pull request closed in May without being merged, and developer Sjors Provoost later continued the effort through a separate implementation that introduced commands to generate and verify witness proofs.

Meanwhile, Bitcoin Knots introduced experimental witness-proof support in version 29.1, and Electrum developers have been working on related protocol changes.

However, these efforts use different proof formats, creating compatibility challenges between wallets and the servers providing blockchain information.

Pablo's proposal attempts to resolve those differences by specifying two binary formats and a JSON representation for exchanging and verifying proofs.

The first format provides a separate cryptographic proof for each transaction, while the second reduces duplicated information when verifying multiple transactions together.

In one example involving 10 transactions, the second format reduced proof data from 4,523 bytes to 3,325 bytes, approximately a 26% reduction.

The proposal also includes a Python reference implementation and 93 test cases designed to establish consistent verification behavior across software implementations.

Of those tests, 31 cover proofs that should be accepted, while 62 cover invalid proofs that verification software must reject.

The specification would give Bitcoin Core, Electrum and other wallet developers a common framework for implementing witness verification.

Pablo has requested feedback on the proposed formats and verification requirements, leaving developers to determine whether a shared standard can replace the experimental approaches already in use.

#5033. LINK, AAVE and UNI Now Supported by Fidelity

Fidelity Investments now supports three new cryptocurrencies on its retail crypto trading platform: Chainlink (LINK), Aave (AAVE) and Uniswap (UNI).

The firm expanded from five available cryptocurrencies to eight on Nov. 24. In just one day, Fidelity added the same number of digital assets that it had added in the four years since it first made Bitcoin and Ether available for customer trading.

Eligible U.S. customers can trade these three tokens on the Fidelity website and app. Some states may have limited access to Fidelity's services.

Fidelity's three new listings include tokens that represent different areas of decentralized finance. Aave is a lending protocol where users can deposit crypto assets to earn interest or use them as collateral for loans. AAVE token holders can vote on changes to the protocol's lending markets and other aspects of its operation.

Uniswap is a decentralized trading platform where users can trade different tokens using shared pools of assets provided by other users. UNI is the Uniswap governance token.

Chainlink provides the data that blockchain apps need to operate. For example, a lending protocol might use a Chainlink price feed to assess the value of collateral for a borrower's loan. Chainlink (LINK) tokens are used to pay for services provided on its network.

Fidelity Crypto started accepting customers from its waitlist in November 2022. At the time, the only two cryptocurrencies it offered were Bitcoin (BTC) and Ether (ETH). By March 2023, Fidelity was offering its service to more eligible U.S. investors, but these were still the only cryptocurrencies it allowed customers to trade.

On July 17, 2024, Fidelity added Litecoin (LTC) to its list of cryptocurrencies, bringing the total to three. Over a year later, on Oct. 23, 2025, the company introduced Solana (SOL). In addition to individual retail accounts, Solana became available for crypto IRAs, wealth managers and institutional clients.

Fidelity added its fifth cryptocurrency on Feb. 4, 2026, when it launched Fidelity Digital Dollar (FIDD) as its first stablecoin available to retail and institutional investors. Eligible users can exchange or redeem each FIDD token for $1. Fidelity waives its usual crypto trading fee when users exchange FIDD for dollars.

#5032. Cuomo says rivals urged companies to opt out of OKXICE tokenized stocks

Former New York Gov. Andrew Cuomo said competitors encouraged companies to opt out of OKXICE’s rollout, while some issuers simply wanted more time.

Former New York Gov. Andrew Cuomo accused competing trading platforms of encouraging companies to reject OKXICE’s tokenized stock plans, saying the rivals wanted more time to launch their own services.

OKXICE is a joint venture between crypto exchange OKX and Intercontinental Exchange (ICE), the US financial markets operator that owns the New York Stock Exchange. Its platform launched Monday, giving eligible US investors access to around-the-clock trading in tokenized stocks.

Speaking during a Monday press briefing, Cuomo, who co-chairs OKXICE, said competitors had urged companies to opt out of the rollout, which has faced objections from 67 companies.

“Remember, also, we had competitors urging companies to opt out,” he said. “Why? Because they want companies to wait for them to have a system.”

The comments underscore the growing competition between traditional exchanges and crypto companies over tokenized stocks, which use tokens on a blockchain to represent ownership of or rights to an asset, such as a stock, allowing them to be traded around the clock.

OKXICE announced an initial rollout of six securities, including Coinbase, Circle, Robinhood, Strategy, BitGo and Securitize. Another 107 assets have been confirmed for a phased rollout, according to the company.

Related: Public companies object to OKX-ICE’s tokenized stock plan

While Cuomo alleged that competitors encouraged companies to opt out, he said some issuers simply needed more time to understand the platform.

Under the US Securities and Exchange Commission’s (SEC) temporary Innovation Exemption, issuers can object to having their shares tokenized by an unaffiliated third party during a 30-day notice period.

Cuomo said the deadline gave some companies too little time to understand the technology and discuss the decision with their lawyers and boards. He added that OKXICE had received calls from companies planning to opt out for now but interested in learning more about the platform before making a final decision.

“The easiest decision was, no, but many were really saying, I need time, I want to talk about it,” he said.

JPMorgan Chase, McDonald’s, SpaceX and Coca-Cola were among the 67 companies that objected to having their shares tokenized on the platform.

Cuomo did not name the competitors he said were encouraging the objections or provide evidence for the claim during the briefing. Other companies developing tokenized stock trading services include Nasdaq, Kraken, Robinhood, Coinbase and Ondo Finance.

#5030. What the junk bond market can tell Bitcoin traders after Monday’s rebound

Risk premiums on the weakest U.S. corporate bonds widened last week, while U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $570 million in net outflows on Monday.

U.S. junk-bond yields rose to 8.46% at the Nov. 20 close as investment-grade yields fell, according to ICE BofA data published Nov. 23 by the Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis. Bond investors demanded a bigger premium to own the weakest corporate debt. That gives Bitcoin buyers a separate gauge of risk appetite after Monday's rebound lost momentum.

The yield on the U.S. high-yield index rose from 8.25% on Nov. 16 to 8.46% on Nov. 20. The biggest yield move among the three corporate indexes was in bonds rated CCC or lower. Its effective yield rose from 17.88% to 18.61%.

Over the same dates, the investment-grade corporate yield fell from 5.95% to 5.84%. The two-year Treasury yield fell from 4.83% to 4.63%.

The gap over the Treasury curve widened across all three ICE BofA corporate bond indexes between Nov. 16 and Nov. 20, 2026.

The option-adjusted spread for bonds rated CCC and lower widened 92 basis points, compared with 42 for high yield overall and 8 for investment grade.

CCC and lower is a subset of the high-yield index. Option-adjusted spreads measure the yield premium over the Treasury curve after accounting for embedded options.

ICE BofA daily closing option-adjusted spreads via FRED. Change equals the Nov. 20 close minus the Nov. 16 close. One percentage point equals 100 basis points. Bars share a 0–100 basis point scale.

CCC and lower is a subset of the high-yield index. Option-adjusted spreads measure the yield premium over the Treasury curve after accounting for embedded options.

Effective yields reflect the returns implied by the prices and payments of bonds in each index. The terms of any new financing would depend on the issuer and the deal. An option-adjusted spread measures the extra yield over the Treasury curve after accounting for options built into the bonds.

The high-yield spread widened from 3.34 to 3.76 percentage points between Nov. 16 and Nov. 20. The CCC-and-lower spread rose from 13.10 to 14.02 points. The investment-grade spread widened from 0.90 to 0.98 points. Even as investment-grade yields fell, investors demanded a bigger premium to hold those bonds over Treasuries.

U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $570 million in net outflows on Monday after $669 million on Friday, according to Farside Investors. On Monday, Bitcoin approached $64,000 after trading near $55,100 early Saturday. The Monday rally gave way to a pullback toward $62,500.

The authors of The Crypto Cycle and US Monetary Policy, a 2023 IMF working paper, found that U.S. monetary tightening weighed on a common factor in crypto prices through investors' willingness to take risk, a channel they also identified in equities. The latest bond and ETF readings are consistent with shared caution, though they cannot explain Monday's Bitcoin price moves on their own.

Further widening in high-yield spreads alongside continued spot Bitcoin ETF outflows would point to caution persisting in both markets. Narrower spreads alongside renewed ETF inflows would support the case for a broader recovery in risk appetite. Sustained ETF inflows while spreads stay wide would suggest demand through those funds can recover even as bond investors remain wary.

#5028. Bisq 2 desktop users must update to keep trading Bitcoin

Version 2.1.16 patches a flaw in the bundled Java runtime. A separate Nov. 25 network change requires nodes to run version 2.1.14 or later.

Bisq released a security update for Bisq 2, its peer-to-peer Bitcoin trading software, on Nov. 23 and urged desktop users to install version 2.1.16 as soon as possible, even if they have no trade in progress. Version 2.1.16 fixes a flaw in the app’s bundled Java software, and Bisq says desktop users need the update to keep trading. A separate network change on Nov. 25 requires nodes to run at least 2.1.14.

In the 2.1.16 release notes, Bisq identified the flaw as Java4Shell and said it could let an attacker run code on an affected system. The release also includes a Tor security update.

After the Nov. 25 change, Bisq 2 nodes running versions older than 2.1.14 can miss offers and trade messages. Bisq announced the change in October when it released version 2.1.14. Starting Nov. 25, updated nodes will require newer V2 signatures on the data they store and share. Bisq warns that versions below 2.1.14 will have trouble connecting and exchanging data, and the project will end support for them.

The project's headless node release notes warn that trades can stall if a node on 2.1.13 misses messages it needs to move a deal forward.

The official downloads page lists the desktop builds for Windows, macOS and Linux and provides PGP signatures and a public key for verifying the installers.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Version 2.1.16 also changes how the app handles a trade across a restart. It saves messages that arrive before the trade can process them, then applies them on startup if trading is enabled and the node meets the minimum trading version. In pre-release testing, a Bisq contributor reported completing test trades after upgrading Ubuntu nodes from 2.1.15 to a 2.1.16 release candidate. The test used nodes accessed through the API and covered trades that were already open during the upgrade.

For mobile traders, the version to check depends on the app. Bisq Easy Mobile runs its own node on Android. Its 0.15.0 release moved to the 2.1.14 core. Bisq says users still on 0.14.x can miss offers and messages or see trades stall after Nov. 25. Bisq urges users to install Bisq Easy 0.17.0, which uses the 2.1.15 core, to keep trading.

Bisq Connect on Android and iOS controls a separate desktop or headless Bisq 2 node. Users who rely on a node run by someone else need that operator to check the version and update it if it falls below the network minimum.

US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) have recorded their worst month since launching in January 2024, led by unprecedented withdrawals from BlackRock.

Meanwhile, US spot Ethereum ETFs recorded $84.92 million in withdrawals on Monday, bringing their monthly outflows to $1.15 billion.

#5026. Base makes quantum security a 2027 priority as regulators warn of risks

Base creator Jesse Pollak says the upcoming Everest upgrade will help prepare wallets for future quantum threats, although further changes will be needed.

Base, an Ethereum layer-2 blockchain developed by Coinbase, is making quantum security a priority for 2027 as it prepares to protect its network against future threats from quantum computers.

Base creator Jesse Pollak told Cointelegraph in an exclusive interview that the upcoming Everest upgrade will introduce a new type of transaction designed to lay the groundwork for quantum-secure wallets.

The network is the second-largest blockchain by decentralized finance (DeFi) total value locked (TVL), with approximately $5.1 billion in assets deposited across its protocols, according to DefiLlama.

The upgrade will not make wallets quantum-resistant right away, but Pollak said it is an early step toward introducing further protections in the future. “That doesn’t mean we’ll be able to solve all of it in 2027, but [it’s] something the team’s going to be working on,” Pollak said.

The plans come as global regulators warn that quantum computers could eventually break the security systems used across financial markets, including blockchain networks.

Quantum computers process information differently from regular computers and could one day allow hackers to access crypto wallets and steal funds. However, many experts have concluded that today’s quantum computers aren’t powerful enough to do this.

One of Base’s first steps will come through Everest, its upcoming fifth network upgrade. Pollak said the upgrade introduces a new transaction type called Base Transactions, which he expects to support future improvements to wallet security.

“That network upgrade doesn’t specifically solve quantum security for these wallets, but it does put in place the framework,” Pollak said.

The current technical specifications for Base Transactions, outlined in EIP-8430, still rely on existing cryptography rather than quantum-resistant signatures. However, the proposed transaction format separates how a transaction is signed from how its signature is verified, allowing the network to introduce new security methods in the future without requiring users to change their wallet addresses.

Base has not set a timeline for introducing quantum-resistant protections, beyond making the work a priority for 2027.

Related: Ethereum dev targets 2028 for quantum resistance, a year early

On Thursday, the International Organization of Securities Commissions (IOSCO) warned in a report that sufficiently powerful quantum computers could threaten the security of financial systems.

The international regulator said blockchain networks could be especially difficult to protect because making security changes would require cooperation from many participants. It also warned that these upgrades could create new security problems, raise costs and disrupt transactions.

Meanwhile, advances in artificial intelligence (AI) could make cyberattacks harder to detect and analyze, the report notes.

Governments are also preparing for the threat, with the US National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST) introducing its first three security standards designed to protect against quantum attacks in 2024, and releasing further guidance in October 2026. The agency has set 2035 as the primary target for completing the transition to quantum-resistant cryptography across US federal systems.

Beyond quantum security, Pollak outlined four priorities for Base in 2027: expanding trading, payments and lending, while preparing for a future in which AI agents carry out more financial transactions.

Pollak pointed to growing demand for tokenized stocks, saying weekly trading volume on Base had reached $1 billion. He said much of the interest came from investors outside the US seeking easier access to American stocks.

AI agents are also expected to play a bigger role in the network. “Agentic payments was a big trend in 2026. I think that agentic finance is going to be one of the biggest trends of 2027,” he said.

Base’s latest network upgrade, Denim, was rolled out on Nov. 5 and reduced block times from two seconds to 200 milliseconds, allowing the network to produce blocks 10 times faster.

The upgrade was followed by transaction delays on Base. Pollak said Base reviewed the incidents, made fixes and introduced additional testing to help prevent similar problems.

Looking ahead, Pollak said he wants future upgrades to happen without users noticing. “Our goal in 2027 is to make it so that upgrades are non-event. So no one’s even talking about them,” he said.

#5021. Is the AI Bubble Finally Popping? Nvidia Crashes 48% as Wall Street Experts Sound the Alarm

Is the AI Bubble Finally Popping? Nvidia Crashes 48% as Wall Street Experts Sound the Alarm

Wall Street's artificial intelligence boom is facing its biggest test yet.

Nvidia plunged 14% on Friday, the Nasdaq Composite lost 5.3%, and the broader S&P 500 entered correction territory as investors questioned whether the trillions of dollars being poured into AI infrastructure will ever produce the expected returns.

The Nasdaq closed at 22,617.73, approximately 18% below its October 6 record closing high of 27,599.79. Its 10% weekly decline was the worst since 2020, while the index has now erased its gains for 2026.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Nvidia with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

Nvidia, the company that powered much of the AI rally, finished Friday at $125.53, down 47.5% from its October closing record of $239.24.

The chipmaker lost nearly 23% during the week as the fallout from former CEO Jensen Huang's departure and reports of an SEC accounting investigation added to concerns about the AI business model.

Other AI-linked companies have suffered even steeper losses. CoreWeave, which rents computing capacity to AI developers, closed at $39.51, almost 80% below its 2025 peak, while Oracle has lost roughly 70% from its highs and finished the week at $101.19.

AMD shed 20.5% over the week, closing at $424.39, while Alphabet lost 10.3% to $265.29. The iShares Semiconductor ETF, SOXX, dropped 17.4% during the same period, showing that investors are selling across the companies supplying the AI industry, not merely its most speculative businesses.

The selloff accelerated just as Anthropic completed its historic $81 billion IPO. The Claude developer priced its shares at $83 and closed its first trading session 17% higher, but its stock fell another 9% on Friday as enthusiasm for the offering failed to restore confidence in the wider sector.

With so many stocks in the red, it’s logical that many AI fanboys are starting to get nervous.

Traders on Polymarket have become increasingly pessimistic. Contracts assessing whether the AI industry will experience a defined downturn showed approximately 54% odds by December 31, 2026, and 65% by June 30, 2027, according to Friday's market snapshot.

The market has attracted approximately $4.1 million in trading volume, but its definition of a bubble bursting is more demanding than a falling stock market. At least three specific events must occur within a single 90-day period, including Nvidia closing 50% below its all-time high, SOXX falling 40%, major AI suppliers experiencing similar collapses, or severe financial problems at AI developers.

SOXX has already fallen approximately 43% from its split-adjusted record closing price, satisfying one of the specified price thresholds on paper. Nvidia is approaching another, although crossing either threshold alone would not settle the contract.

The damage has also spread into cryptocurrencies, undermining expectations that 2026 would bring a sustained recovery. Bitcoin finished the week near $58,895, down 9.8% over the period and more than 50% below its 2025 peak.

The total cryptocurrency market capitalization fell to approximately $2.01 trillion, down from more than $4 trillion at its peak. As Decrypt reported Friday, the Crypto Fear and Greed Index dropped to 9, signaling extreme fear and its weakest reading since February.

This was supposed to be the year of the usual pre-halving moon. Instead, for the first time in the history of Bitcoin, the coin is on its way to have two consecutive red years.

Bitcoin has increasingly behaved like other speculative assets sensitive to liquidity conditions, particularly when investors expect borrowing costs to remain elevated.

The pressure is also visible in investment flows. U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $582.2 million in net withdrawals on Thursday, while forced liquidations added to the selling as Bitcoin broke below the psychologically important $60,000 level.

Owen Lamont, a portfolio manager at Acadian Asset Management and former University of Chicago finance professor, believes the warning signs have become difficult to ignore. After arguing in January that AI stocks were not yet in a bubble, he told Fortune this week that he now assigns a 70% probability to the U.S. stock market already being in one.

Lamont tracks four warning signs: excessive valuations, widespread belief in a speculative boom, new stock issuance, and investors pouring money into the market. His latest assessment suggests all four are now present, although his issuance figures are heavily influenced by SpaceX's massive IPO earlier this year.

Bank of America's November fund manager survey offers another indication of deteriorating confidence. Investors reduced their technology exposure by the most since 2006, and 52% expect at least one major AI hyperscaler to announce a capital spending cut within six months, Fortune reported.

The European Central Bank has also raised concerns about how AI development is being financed. Its November review estimates that capital expenditure by major American technology companies could reach $5.5 trillion cumulatively by 2030, with growing reliance on debt as infrastructure spending outpaces internally generated cash.

The similarities to the dot-com era are difficult to dismiss: investors are assigning enormous valuations to companies based on projected demand, businesses are racing to build infrastructure ahead of proven profitability, and financial markets are increasingly dependent on a small group of technology companies.

During the original dot-com collapse, the Nasdaq lost approximately 78% between March 2000 and October 2002. The present decline is considerably smaller, but history also offers a reminder that a technology can succeed commercially while the companies financing its expansion lose enormous amounts of market value.

There are important differences, however. Many of today's largest AI beneficiaries, including Nvidia, Alphabet, and Microsoft, operate profitable businesses with substantial existing revenues, unlike numerous internet startups whose valuations depended almost entirely on future growth.

Nvidia's latest quarterly results illustrate the contradiction. The company beat earnings estimates, reported record revenue and net income, and issued stronger-than-expected revenue guidance, yet its shares continued collapsing.

Richard Saperstein, chief investment officer at Treasury Partners, described the downturn as an "AI repricing" in comments to Reuters, citing slowing data center construction and tighter funding conditions. Lawrence Gillum, chief fixed income strategist at LPL Financial, offered a less alarming interpretation, saying the decline was "not a bubble bursting" and arguing that the AI investment cycle still has room to run.

Higher interest rates complicate both arguments. Investors are increasingly expecting the Federal Reserve to raise rates again in December, increasing financing costs for data centers while reducing the appeal of stocks whose valuations depend on profits years into the future.

The balance of evidence points to more than a routine bout of nerves, but less than a confirmed systemic bust. Stocks have repriced faster than the underlying technology has changed.

What happens next depends on whether actual demand for AI can justify the data-center spending spree—and whether the companies financing it can withstand tighter credit. The real test isn't simply whether Nvidia can keep selling chips. It's whether its customers can turn all that computing power into profits.

Right now much of the AI hype is just… hype. Markets are moving more by the perceived potential of AI in the future and not so much by what AI is actually doing right now.

Crypto investors have seen this before with ICOs, the TPS wars, the metaverse, the NFT fever and more. For many of them, the distinction may offer little comfort.

Bitcoin's slump shows how quickly markets can stop distinguishing between promising technologies and overpriced bets when confidence disappears. The dot-com crash didn't kill the internet; it killed the assumption that every internet company would make its investors rich.

AI may be the next world-changing technology to prove that being right about the future is not the same as making money from it.

#5020. US moves to seize $1.1 billion from Binance hackers without accessing their wallets

Circle has agreed to issue replacement USDC if a court approves forfeiture, allowing authorities to recover stolen funds while the original tokens remain frozen.

US prosecutors are seeking more than $1.1 billion in USDC stolen during the October Binance hack without accessing the attackers' wallets.

The Justice Department filed a civil forfeiture complaint in Washington, D.C., on Nov. 23, targeting frozen USDC associated with 36 wallet and smart-contract addresses across 18 blockchains.

Under the proposed recovery arrangement, stablecoin issuer Circle would create an equivalent amount of new USDC and transfer it to the US government if the court approves the forfeiture.

The original tokens would remain frozen in addresses allegedly controlled by the hackers.

Circle had already blocked approximately $1.1 billion in USDC following the Oct. 29 attack, which US authorities attributed to North Korea's Lazarus Group.

However, the company cannot directly transfer the frozen tokens because it does not control the private keys associated with the attackers' wallets.

Instead, its ability to block transfers and issue replacement tokens provides prosecutors with a mechanism to recover the value of stolen assets without obtaining the underlying wallets.

The forfeiture complaint describes a substitute payment to the U.S. government.

The proposed transfer would use new USDC equal to the blocked amount.

The complaint leaves the eventual treatment of the original tokens unspecified.

The two tracks distinguish Circle’s existing transfer restriction from the conditional substitute issuance described in paragraph 34 of the federal complaint. No figures are calculated.

The complaint leaves the eventual treatment of the original tokens unspecified.

Meanwhile, prosecutors are seeking a further restraining order to keep the stolen USDC immobilized while the forfeiture case proceeds.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The proposed arrangement highlights the recovery powers available through centrally issued stablecoins, unlike native Bitcoin and Ethereum, which a token issuer cannot freeze.

The Justice Department said US authorities have helped freeze, seize, or return more than $2.9 billion in cryptocurrencies since the attack, which resulted in over $6 billion in losses.

Binance welcomed the recovery effort, confirming that it was working with U.S. authorities and industry partners to retrieve stolen assets.

“We are coordinating closely with the US Department of Justice, as well as with industry partners who have assisted in freezing assets impacted by the incident.”

Binance added that it would continue cooperating throughout the forfeiture proceedings to support the return of affected funds.

The exchange previously said it had replaced all affected customer token assets with its own funds by Nov. 13, drawing on its corporate treasury, futures insurance fund and emergency reserves.

Meanwhile, Binance announced the appointment of Mandiant, SlowMist and Verichains to conduct independent reviews of its security procedures following the attack.

The firms will examine its existing security infrastructure, identify potential weaknesses and assess its defenses against future cyberattacks.

Mandiant specializes in incident response and nation-state cyber threats, while SlowMist and Verichains provide blockchain security research and threat intelligence.

Binance Chief Security Officer Jimmy Su said the company was committed to acting on the reviewers' findings.

The announcement follows court filings describing how attackers allegedly compromised a Binance employee through malicious software distributed during a fraudulent recruitment interview before gaining control of exchange wallets.

The security reviews have not yet produced findings, leaving Binance's next defensive changes dependent on their conclusions.

#5017. BTCS offloads $44M in ETH to reduce DeFi debt amid market downturn

BTCS sold more than half its Ether holdings to reduce leverage following the market sell-off, while BitMine added another 7,819 ETH to its treasury.

Nasdaq-listed blockchain company BTCS sold approximately 27,300 Ether on Friday to repay $43.8 million in loans on decentralized lending protocol Aave as cryptocurrency markets plunged.

According to a Monday filing with the US Securities and Exchange Commission, BTCS sold the Ether (ETH) on Nov. 20 and used the proceeds to reduce its outstanding Aave debt to approximately $10.5 million.

The transaction reduced BTCS’s Aave borrowings by approximately 81%, as the company sought to limit liquidation risk amid heightened market volatility. Its remaining Aave positions had a loan-to-value ratio of approximately 32% following the repayment.

BTCS reported approximately $17.9 million in outstanding convertible notes, in addition to its remaining Aave borrowings, according to the company’s announcement. The company also said it temporarily exited liquidity pool positions in its Imperium DeFi business as part of its efforts to reduce exposure to further market volatility.

The move follows a similar reduction in Ether exposure by digital asset manager CoinShares, which disclosed last week that it sold its entire ETH treasury position and half its Solana (SOL) holdings in October to fund share buybacks.

BitMine accounts for roughly 75% of the Ether held in corporate and government treasuries tracked by CoinGecko, which listed 35 entities holding a combined 8.16 million ETH at the time of reporting.

The contrasting strategies come as Ether continues to struggle, trading at approximately $1,743 as of 6:35 p.m. UTC on Monday, down 37.7% over the past year and 24.8% over the past 30 days, according to CoinGecko data.
