#5016. Signal Calls OS-Level AI an 'Existential Threat' and Blocks Screenshots to Fight It

Signal Calls OS-Level AI an 'Existential Threat' and Blocks Screenshots to Fight It

Signal is moving to wall off its messages from the AI assistants now baked into smartphones, restricting screenshots so that systems like Google’s Gemini and Apple’s Siri can’t quietly scan and store users’ private conversations.

The encrypted-messaging nonprofit said Thursday it will block the latest agentic AI features built into iOS 27 and, on Android, automatically enable a “screen security” setting whenever it detects an AI assistant trying to read a user’s Signal screen.

The move extends a tactic Signal first used last year against Microsoft’s Recall, the Windows feature that captured periodic screenshots; the company blocked it to keep Recall from storing images of Signal messages by default.

The concern, Signal says, is that operating-system AI agents work by “scanning” whatever is on a user’s screen, which means Signal data gets captured and handed to those systems to process.

Once that happens, the content lands in whatever local database or remote server the AI is using, outside Signal’s control and beyond the encryption guarantees that have defined the app for a decade.

Users who still want to take screenshots can disable the protection in Signal’s settings, though the company warns that doing so could expose their messages, and their contacts’ messages, to the AI agents.

Signal also conceded the fix is incomplete: It doesn’t stop sneakier methods of access, such as pulling data straight from a device’s filesystem, and it can’t yet be implemented on macOS, where Signal says Apple lacks the documented tools to block screenshots reliably.

Signal also flagged third-party agents like Meta’s Muse, warning they exploit accessibility permissions meant to help people, not to serve as “a skeleton key” for AI.

The worry isn’t hypothetical: A journalist found that Muse had accessed his private iMessages despite his settings and then gave a false explanation of how it knew their contents, an episode that crystallized fears about how much agents can reach once granted deep device permissions.

Signal’s move lands amid a broader reckoning over AI agents accessing data they shouldn’t, from rogue agents breaching systems to banks scrambling to keep autonomous software out of sensitive accounts.

#5012. Eric Trump’s Bitcoin miner surges 17% after $150 million deal to avoid selling BTC

The financing gives American Bitcoin another source of liquidity while competitors increasingly sell Bitcoin to finance data-center expansion.

American Bitcoin secured a $150 million Bitcoin-backed credit facility as other listed miners sell reserves to finance AI infrastructure.

The Eric Trump-linked company disclosed the revolving credit agreement with FalconX in a Nov. 23 SEC filing, giving it access to capital without selling Bitcoin or issuing additional shares.

Data from Yahoo Finance showed that American Bitcoin shares climbed as high as $4.38 during Monday's trading before closing 16.3% higher at $4.07. Despite the rebound, the stock remains approximately 84% below its level at the start of the year.

The financing comes as the company continues expanding its Bitcoin holdings, which recently surpassed 8,500 BTC.

American Bitcoin said the accumulation has moved it from 15th to 13th among publicly traded corporate Bitcoin holders worldwide.

The company combines Bitcoin mining with direct accumulation, making the preservation of its reserves central to its long-term strategy.

“This $150 million facility provides American Bitcoin with non-dilutive capital to support our continued operations and growth initiatives.”

The approach contrasts with a broader shift across the mining industry.

Blockchain analytics firm Glassnode reported that publicly listed miners in its tracked group had reduced their combined Bitcoin reserves by nearly half since January. The firm attributed the decline to miners increasingly using Bitcoin holdings to finance their expansion into artificial intelligence data centers.

For years, many publicly traded miners retained their mined Bitcoin and sometimes purchased additional coins. That strategy has increasingly given way to asset sales as operators redirect capital toward AI computing infrastructure.

American Bitcoin's arrangement offers another financing route, allowing it to retain ownership of its Bitcoin while pledging some holdings as collateral for dollar liquidity.

The agreement allows American Bitcoin's subsidiary to borrow up to $150 million from FalconX Charlie, secured by Bitcoin held at Anchorage Digital Bank.

According to the SEC filing, borrowings carry a 5.95% annual loan fee, alongside commitment fees on unused capacity. The facility matures in November 2028, with a possible three-year extension.

However, preserving Bitcoin through collateralized borrowing introduces exposure to forced asset sales during market downturns.

The agreement requires pledged Bitcoin to cover at least 143% of outstanding principal when funds are drawn. If that ratio falls below 130%, FalconX can demand additional Bitcoin or partial repayment to restore the collateral requirement.

American Bitcoin’s subsidiary had drawn nothing on the $150 million FalconX line as of the Nov. 23 filing. These thresholds govern draws and outstanding loans.

The 143% ratio serves as the draw minimum and the target after a margin-call top-up or partial collateral release.

The graphic shows the agreement’s thresholds for draws and outstanding principal. It does not show a reported collateral balance or forecast a sale.

Markers are spaced proportionately on each axis using the contractual ratio of pledged bitcoin value to total outstanding principal. Thresholds come from Item 1.01, the master agreement and the signed term sheet.

The graphic shows the agreement’s thresholds for draws and outstanding principal. It does not show a reported collateral balance or forecast a sale.

A decline below 105% can trigger default and potentially allow the lender to liquidate pledged assets under the agreement's conditions.

At the minimum initial collateral ratio, a Bitcoin price decline of about 9.1% would trigger a margin call if the company borrowed and neither added collateral nor repaid debt.

American Bitcoin already had 3,090 BTC pledged for mining equipment purchases as of Sept. 30, according to its quarterly filing.

The new facility could let it access more capital while maintaining its Bitcoin position, but borrowing would also put additional holdings behind collateral obligations.

Still, the arrangement leaves American Bitcoin on a different path from miners selling reserves to fund AI infrastructure, with its ability to retain those coins increasingly tied to BTC's market value and how much it ultimately borrows.

#5008. Fraudsters Used Fake Crypto Exchanges to Steal $37 Million, SEC Alleges

Fraudsters Used Fake Crypto Exchanges to Steal $37 Million, SEC Alleges

The Securities and Exchange Commission has charged eight entities with running a sprawling scheme that used fake crypto trading platforms and sham investment advisers to steal more than $37 million from U.S. investors.

The case targets eight Colorado-registered entities that the SEC says were likely operated by individuals overseas.

According to the complaint, filed in Colorado federal court, the defendants lured victims into believing they were working with a legitimate investment adviser and crypto trading platform, when both were entirely fake, “simply fronts to steal investor money.”

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Bitcoin with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

The mechanics will sound familiar to anyone who has followed the rise of “pig butchering” scams. The SEC said the fraudsters advertised on social media to funnel investors into group and private chats, where operators posing as advisers first coached them to trade stocks on real U.S. markets before steering them toward a bogus crypto platform and pressing them to send as much money as possible. Victims believed they were buying real crypto assets; in reality, the funds were stolen.

To fake legitimacy, the operators went to unusual lengths, the SEC said, fabricating phony SEC certificates and even making filings with the SEC, FINRA and FinCEN, then pointing skeptical investors to those documents as proof they were real.

The scheme ran from August 2024 through May 2026 across four adviser-and-platform pairs, with the perpetrators abandoning one pair and spinning up the next every few months.

“The SEC continues to seek out and expose fraudulent investment scams that prey on U.S. investors,” said Laura D’Allaird, chief of the agency’s Cyber and Emerging Technologies Unit, urging the public to keep reporting such schemes.

The charged entities, carrying officious-sounding names like Convergent Wealth Advisors and Essential Crypto Exchange, face allegations of violating federal antifraud provisions, with several also accused of breaching the Investment Advisers Act.

The SEC is seeking injunctions, disgorgement and civil penalties. It credited cooperation from regulators in Hong Kong, Singapore, China and Switzerland, underscoring the cross-border nature of the fraud.

The case reflects a persistent threat, as fake platforms and social-engineering scams continue to drain billions from victims worldwide.

Chainalysis and others have documented how such operations, often run by organized crime from Southeast Asia, have scaled with the help of AI-generated personas and scripts, turning romance and investment cons into one of crypto’s costliest categories of fraud.

#5007. Hong Kong wants banks to secure new approval before holding customers’ crypto

The proposed legislation would extend SFC oversight to crypto custodians as financial institutions grapple with security and regulatory challenges.

Hong Kong plans to require banks to register with its securities regulator before offering crypto custody services to customers.

The government published the Virtual Asset Services Legislation (Amendment) Bill 2026 on Nov. 20, expanding its regulatory framework to cover businesses safeguarding, trading, advising on or managing virtual assets.

Under the legislation, banks and stored-value facility operators already supervised by the Hong Kong Monetary Authority (HKMA) would need additional registration with the Securities and Futures Commission (SFC) to provide covered virtual asset services. Independent providers would generally need an SFC license.

Hong Kong’s bill would generally require a license for standalone custodians and registration for HKMA-supervised banks and stored value facility licensees.

Both forms of authorization would come from the Securities and Futures Commission.

The diagram covers the proposed authorization routes. Exceptions and implementation timing depend on the legislation.

The two routes follow the government’s gazettal announcement and the earlier custody consultation. Existing bank standards follow the HKMA’s May 2026 guidance.

The diagram covers the proposed authorization routes. Exceptions and implementation timing depend on the legislation.

The bill, scheduled for its first reading in the Legislative Council on Dec. 2, would amend Hong Kong's Anti-Money Laundering and Counter-Terrorist Financing Ordinance. Applicants would face fit-and-proper assessments and other regulatory requirements designed to strengthen investor protection.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The additional requirements build on existing regulatory obligations for banks entering the digital asset market.

In May, the HKMA updated its guidance requiring financial institutions offering digital asset custody to maintain appropriate security, governance and operational controls.

Banks intending to launch such services must consult the monetary authority beforehand and demonstrate compliance with its custody standards.

The regulatory expansion comes amid growing institutional interest in digital assets, although security concerns and regulatory uncertainty have slowed adoption.

A February report by Ripple and Quinlan & Associates found that 77% of 65 surveyed Hong Kong financial institutions had either adopted digital assets or planned to do so. However, only 25% had adopted them at the time of the survey.

The research found that 65% of respondents preferred outsourcing custody, rising to 80% among buy-side institutions.

Bank-affiliated custodians attracted the strongest institutional interest, with 64% of respondents expressing willingness to engage them. However, only 40% rated their readiness highly.

Hong Kong's proposed custody framework targets the security risks associated with controlling customers' digital assets.

In their December 2025 consultation conclusions, the SFC and the Financial Services and the Treasury Bureau identified the safeguarding of private keys and similar instruments that enable asset transfers as the central activity requiring authorization.

Regulators said businesses controlling these instruments face technological and operational risks that could result in the loss or unauthorized transfer of customers' assets.

Fund managers and other institutions that delegate custody to third parties without safeguarding private keys would generally fall outside the specific custody licensing requirement.

Similarly, noncustodial wallet providers that let customers retain independent control of their assets would generally not require authorization. Shared-key arrangements would be assessed based on the provider's ability to transfer assets and customers' ability to retain or recover access independently.

The proposed virtual asset custody regime would exclude tokenized securities, which remain subject to existing securities regulations.

Existing crypto custodians could face more immediate compliance pressures once the legislation takes effect.

During the consultation process, some industry participants requested transitional arrangements lasting between three and 18 months, citing potential operational disruptions and the time needed to meet regulatory requirements.

However, authorities rejected automatically granting existing operators temporary authorization, citing concerns about investor protection and confusion over their regulatory status.

Instead, regulators proposed expedited approvals for certain banks, bank subsidiaries and entities associated with licensed crypto trading platforms that had already undergone supervisory assessments.

Authorities also encouraged existing custodians to begin engaging with the SFC or HKMA ahead of the new regime.

While the bill is scheduled to reach the Legislative Council on Dec. 2, the government has yet to announce a commencement date. That decision will determine how much time existing custodians have to secure authorization and avoid disruptions to their businesses.

#5005. Kalshi secures temporary win in New York gambling law dispute

The ruling allows Kalshi to continue operating as its legal battle with New York over sports-related event contracts moves toward a January hearing.

A New York state court judge on Monday denied the state attorney general’s request for a temporary restraining order against prediction market operator Kalshi, allowing the company to continue offering event contracts in New York as a legal battle over its sports-related markets proceeds.

In a statement to Cointelegraph, Elisabeth Diana, Kalshi’s head of communications, confirmed the ruling, saying the judge recognized the complex legal questions surrounding derivatives and federal preemption.

“The judge denied the State’s request for a TRO, recognizing that there are complex questions about derivatives and preemption. We remain committed to working with the State on a path forward that works for our 1.5 million customers and for the state of New York,” Diana said.

The denial of the temporary restraining order does not resolve the broader dispute over whether Kalshi’s federally regulated event contracts are subject to New York’s gambling laws.

The case will now proceed toward a preliminary injunction hearing set for early January, when the court will consider whether to restrict Kalshi’s event-contract operations in New York. Before then, both parties are scheduled to return to court on Nov. 30 to discuss potential restrictions on users aged 18 to 20, following concerns raised by the judge over the state’s minimum legal age of 21 for sports betting.

Related: CFTC halts some Kalshi Trump mention markets over manipulation concerns

New York sued Kalshi in July, alleging the prediction market operator was running an illegal, unlicensed gambling business by offering event contracts tied to sports, elections and other outcomes. The lawsuit seeks to halt the company’s operations in the state, recover alleged illegal gains and impose civil penalties.

The case followed an October 2025 cease-and-desist order from the New York State Gaming Commission, which prompted Kalshi to challenge the state’s authority in federal court. Kalshi argues that its status as a federally regulated derivatives exchange places its event contracts under the jurisdiction of the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC).

In August, the CFTC warned that New York’s requested restraining order could prevent Kalshi from offering event contracts nationwide because the exchange is based in the state. The regulator also said New York was seeking more than $36 billion in damages.

In a separate federal court proceeding on Friday, US District Judge Lorna Schofield rejected the CFTC’s emergency request to block New York from pursuing its enforcement case against Kalshi, according to Reuters.

Kalshi isn’t the only prediction market operator facing legal action in New York. The state also sued Polymarket in September over allegedly offering illegal sports betting contracts, following similar actions against platforms operated by Coinbase and Gemini in April.

#5002. Prediction market bets placed just before big news could soon be easier to trace

New CFTC reporting rules could help investigators trace prediction market trades to people with early access to information.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) approved a rule on Nov. 23 requiring exchanges that list certain prediction market contracts to collect traders' names and employers.

When trading surges ahead of an announcement, those records could help investigators identify who traded and look for links to people or organizations with early access to the news.

The final rule also requires exchanges to disclose when covered trades occurred, at what price and size, and how the contracts settled. The CFTC says the reports will help it monitor trading across markets.

Under Section 16.03, exchanges must publish each covered trade’s execution time, ticker, size, and US dollar price as soon as technologically practicable.

The data must stay free for at least a year after it first appears, with instructions on how to download, save, and search it. Public trade disclosure is familiar to exchanges such as Kalshi.

A May 13 CFTC staff letter already made prompt publication of those four fields and reporting to the CFTC under Section 16.02 conditions of relief from certain swap-reporting rules. Its updated list includes KalshiEX and Kalshi Klear.

On Nov. 23, Kalshi’s public trade feed recorded a match for 981.95 contracts in KXHIGHNY-26NOV23-T64 at one cent for Yes and 99 cents for No. The $1 binary contract asked whether New York City’s high that day would fall below 64°F.

Kalshi’s product filing identifies the exchange as a registered designated contract market. Its clearing affiliate, Kalshi Klear, told the CFTC that it clears KalshiEX’s binary event-contract swaps on a fully cash-collateralized basis.

The reporting rule covers certain event contracts classified as swaps and listed on a designated contract market.

A derivatives clearing organization must clear the contract and hold enough collateral at all times to cover the maximum possible loss. A covered contract can pay a fixed amount to one side, or pay both sides based on the settlement price in a structure where only one side profits.

The contract named The Weather Company as the source for its outcome. Kalshi’s settlement record shows a high of 69°F and a final No, with settlement recorded at 12:07 p.m. UTC on Nov. 24. The rule requires exchanges to publish whether the event occurred and, if it did, what happened, when it happened, and the source used to determine the outcome.

A reader could line up the execution timestamps with an independently verified announcement time and see which trades came first. Kalshi’s trade ID identifies the execution, and the public feed shows the trades in sequence, while the accounts behind them remain private.

Exchanges will have to collect each trader’s name, physical address, email, phone number, occupation, and employer and associate that information with every covered order or trade.

The rule also calls for the names of account guarantors and people with a financial interest of 10% or more in the trader or account, where applicable. Exchanges must keep the data for the life of the contract and for at least five years after it ends.

The CFTC’s reporting guidebook shows how the agency can use a customer account number to match an exchange’s trade report with a separate file that lists the trader’s occupation and employer.

A link to a company involved in an announcement could help investigators decide where to look. They would still need to establish when the information became available, whether the trader had access to it, and whether their conduct violated the relevant rule.

Under Section 16.02, exchanges report identifying data they already maintain once the provision applies to covered trades. An employer match can guide an inquiry. A finding of misconduct requires separate evidence of conduct.

#5001. GTA 6 Leaker Dangles a PC Port Behind a $15 Million Crypto Pump

GTA 6 Leaker Dangles a PC Port Behind a $15 Million Crypto Pump

A notorious Grand Theft Auto 6 leaker is holding a potential PC version of the game hostage to a cryptocurrency payday, promising to release the final file needed to make a leaked build playable once its meme coin hits a $15 million market cap.

The leaker, who goes by Cyberleek, has released what it claims is a near-complete 209 GB Xbox build of GTA 6 from November 2026 via torrent, according to a report from PC Gamer.

The one piece withheld is the main executable, the file that would let modders begin emulating the game on PC, potentially bringing it to the platform long before Rockstar Games intends.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Bitcoin with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

Here’s the crypto catch. Cyberleek says it will release the executable on Nov. 23 regardless, but will drop it early if its token, called CYBERLEEK, reaches a $15 million market cap. That’s half the $30 million target the leaker floated in October.

The pressure tactic doesn’t appear to be working: the token is trading around $0.002 as of the weekend, with a market cap of roughly $1.3 million, more than $13 million short of the goal. The coin is down some 20% on the week, but the numbers will fluctuate wildly given the very thin liquidity on a small cap coin.

The setup has drawn open skepticism that it’s a cash grab, or worse. Observers have warned it could be an attempt to pump the coin before a rug pull, the crypto term for abandoning a token after luring buyers in. Cyberleek has also claimed to hold a PS5 build as a fallback if the Xbox files can’t be made to work.

The episode sits at the increasingly busy intersection of crypto, piracy, and AI. Modern Xbox games are architecturally closer to PC software than PlayStation titles, and AI coding tools have accelerated the kind of decompilation work that makes fast ports plausible, the same dynamic behind a wave of AI-assisted game ports this year.

It also caps a chaotic launch window for GTA that crypto has repeatedly crashed. One trader banked around $66,000 on a GTA 6 meme coin amid ticker confusion, and the game’s disc-free, digital-only release has fueled broader debate, with GameStop’s CEO arguing that digital-only games have hurt consumers.

None of the leaked files have been independently confirmed as genuine, and there is still no official GTA 6 PC version; Rockstar, famously litigious, could respond with legal action rather than a port.

#4996. US seeks to recover $1.1B in crypto stolen in North Korea-linked Binance hack

US prosecutors are seeking control of stolen USDC, while Elliptic says hackers have moved $247 million through Bitcoin mixers.

The US Department of Justice is seeking to recover more than $1.1 billion in crypto stolen from cryptocurrency exchange Binance in October by hackers linked to North Korea.

In a court filing Monday, prosecutors asked a federal court in Washington, DC, to permanently forfeit the funds, which are held in USDC – a stablecoin issued by Circle. The filing follows a court order obtained earlier this month allowing authorities to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion in assets linked to the hack. Since the attack, which occurred on Oct. 29, US authorities said they have helped to freeze, seize or return nearly $3 billion of the more than $6 billion taken from Binance.

“Following the Binance theft, the FBI and our partners moved quickly to trace and freeze stolen assets and share critical information with virtual asset service providers,” said in a statement Brett Leatherman, operations director of the Federal Bureau of Investigation’s (FBI) cyber branch.

Binance has also pursued legal action to recover the stolen assets. On Nov. 18, the exchange said it had secured a $6.5 billion worldwide freezing injunction from the Abu Dhabi Global Market Courts. The orders target unidentified attackers and allow legal notices to be delivered through blockchain transactions to wallets linked to the theft.

The filing comes as blockchain analytics firm Elliptic also reported an analysis updated Monday that hackers had moved at least $247 million in Bitcoin (BTC) through crypto mixers – tools that make stolen funds harder to track. The firm said another $2.22 billion remained in wallets linked to the theft, while $2.85 billion had been frozen or seized.

Elliptic noted that hackers had moved stolen funds through more than 27,000 wallets across at least 16 blockchains. However, most of the crypto had not yet been converted into cash, and the 18,270 BTC stolen directly from Binance had not moved since the attack.

US authorities have linked the attack to a North Korean hacking group called TraderTraitor. According to Binance, the hackers gained access to its systems after tricking an employee into downloading malicious software. Following the attack, Binance said it had committed more than $6.34 billion to cover the losses and reassured that customer funds were safe.

#4995. 9 Bodily Changes That Happen a Month After Taking Testosterone

TRT can bring big changes fast—and some might surprise you.

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

TESTOSTERONE GOES BEYOND building muscle. T helps you maintain muscle mass, facial and body hair, sex drive, and red blood cell production. So when your T levels start to decline—which happens naturally in your thirties—there’s a whole range of symptoms like low libido or just feeling blue.

Testosterone replacement therapy (TRT) can help raise T levels. After a couple of weeks to a month, you’ll start to see some changes, some big and small. Some pleasant and not so pleasant. Below, urologists explain what happens to your body a month after taking testosterone.

IF YOU HAVE low T, your doctor will conduct some tests to see if you’re a good candidate for the therapy. Ryan Smith, MD, urologist specializing in men’s health at the University of Virginia Health, says a doctor will perform two blood tests in the morning (on different days) when testosterone is at its highest.

Low is considered under 300 nanograms per deciliter (ng/dL) by some accounts and under 264 ng/dL by others. Doctors also factor in any symptoms you’re having, like trouble getting erections or orgasms, fatigue, or just feeling blah.

Nowadays, testosterone is more accessible without a prescription, with many people only having to complete an online assessment. Leaving out a doctor’s approval and supervision, however, can be harmful, says Justin Dubin, MD, urologist at Baptist Health.

In a 2022 study that Dr. Dubin co-authored, researchers found that many online platforms are not providing testosterone therapy in accordance with the American Urological Association and the Endocrine Society guidelines. Using a secret shopper, the researchers found that the platforms offered therapy to men who didn’t meet the guidelines and didn’t mention the risks or benefits of testosterone therapy.

Working with a doctor ensures that you’re prescribed testosterone only when you need it and that you’re taking the right dose. Then, they can work with you to make sure your testosterone levels respond appropriately. Doctors like to recheck everything every six months to make sure you’re not having any negative side effects.

A number of testosterone options are available. You can choose between injectables, topical gels, creams, under-the-skin pellet therapy, pills, and nasal sprays. You can learn more about them here.

Each comes with its own set of side effects, so it all comes down to your personal preference, health situation, and what your insurance covers. You can read about the real-life experiences of guys of different ages and body types and their experiences with taking testosterone.

IT CAN TAKE a few weeks to a month or two to see your symptoms improve after starting testosterone replacement therapy, says Dr. Smith. Here are some of the side effects—some obvious and some not—that come with testosterone therapy.

When you’re low on testosterone, you might notice your sex drive dip. But when you take TRT, the treatment reactivates the androgen receptors in the part of your brain that controls desire, says Abraham Morgentaler, MD, associate clinical professor of urology at Harvard Medical School and author of Testosterone for Life. Regaining a healthy sex drive is one of the biggest benefits of testosterone replacement therapy, he says.

TRT may also make your erections more satisfying, though this isn’t the only piece of the puzzle. Erections also rely on healthy nerves and blood flow. That means that testosterone therapy by itself won't always help erectile dysfunction.

Muscles respond to testosterone and starting TRT can increase muscle mass, says Dr. Morgentaler. That’s because testosterone activates the androgen receptors in muscle tissue to stimulate growth.

The effect of a testosterone replacement dose on your muscles, however, is fairly minimal. To make the most of this muscle-building benefit, you’ll need to be doing your part through strength training and possibly raising the amount of protein you consume (depending on your current intake). Don’t know where to start? Check out MH’s four-week muscle building guide.

Some men also report fat loss. While testosterone doesn’t directly incite fat loss itself, part of it may be thanks to the uptick in muscle mass. The more muscle you have, the higher your basal metabolic rate (BMR). That means your body will burn more calories at rest.

Another indirect way testosterone can help with fat loss is motivation to keep pushing once you see results. Jed Kaminetsky, MD, urologist at NYU Langone Medical Center, testosterone can increase your energy levels, making it easier to get up and sweat it out.

Fatigue is a common symptom of low T. “When we treat them, a lot of men will say that their energy has improved,” says Dr. Morgentaler.

Scientists studying testosterone aren't sure exactly how the hormone plays a role in energy—just that it does. One of the prevailing hypotheses Dr. Morgentaler can get behind is that testosterone somehow affects your mitochondria (that organelle you learned in high school is the powerhouse of your cells). The theory is that “testosterone turns them on, so they're more productive in terms of creating the energy that the cells need.”

Ronald Tamler, MD, PhD, associate professor of medicine at the Icahn School of Medicine at Mount Sinai, says changes in energy may also be tied back to androgen receptors. “If they don't get sufficient input, that can cause fatigue,” he says. Bringing testosterone levels back up can help reverse this.

Experts don't have a definitive answer to why testosterone impacts mood so deeply—after all, “the brain is a complicated thing,” says Dr. Tamler. But the positive effect of TRT on mood is a potential life-changing benefit.

An old but highly referenced clinical trial in 2003 looked at how testosterone gels affected middle-aged men with treatment-resistant depression and low T. After 8 weeks, the men showed greater improvements in the psychological aspects of depression (sadness, guilt, and anxiety) than those given a placebo. The men taking testosterone gels also showed nearly the same rate of improvement in somatic aspects of depression (improvements in sleep, appetite, and libido). A more recent review of 27 clinical trials involving 1,890 men with low T found that TRT provided a significant reduction in depressive symptoms, especially at higher doses.

One of the most important things to know before starting TRT is that the treatment decreases sperm production and may cause infertility.

Testosterone might affect the size of your testicles, too. "Most of the size of the testicle is dedicated to making sperm, so when you're making less sperm, the testicles get smaller," says Dr. Morgentaler.

Sperm production may or may not go back to normal even after stopping treatment, says Dr. Kaminetsky. One study found increasing age and longer TRT linked to lower chances of normal sperm recovery.

Some people notice a little swelling in their feet and ankles because testosterone can encourage your body to hold onto excess fluid, says Dr. Morgentaler.

This isn't a big deal for most people, he says, and swelling is more common if you're taking a non-daily treatment like an injection, when you're getting a higher dose of T in one sitting.

Testosterone replacement therapy may change your skin type—for better or for worse, says Dr. Morgentaler.

Testosterone can increase oil production, which isn't necessarily a bad thing. Oil production is important for healthy skin, so you may actually end up with a better complexion. However, too much oily buildup can lead to breakouts.

While Dr. Morgentaler says testosterone-induced breakouts aren’t common, they are more likely to occur in men with a history of acne. Skin changes may also be more common if you’re taking testosterone injections versus other modes of administration since you're getting a higher dose all at once.

In all men—whether you’re taking T or not—some testosterone is converted into the hormone estradiol, a form of estrogen. In men who naturally have more breast tissue, testosterone can be converted into estradiol, which may stimulate breast tissue to grow.

This is called gynecomastia, says Dr. Morgentaler, and it's not as common as other side effects. If gynecomastia does happen, your doctor will likely stop treatment for a month or two to allow your breast tissue to go back to normal. Then, you’ll resume T along with a drug that blocks the conversion of testosterone to estradiol.

If you’re going the topical testosterone therapy route, one issue to watch out for is transference with women and children.

Darshan Patel, MD, assistant professor of urology at the University of California San Diego’s Men’s Health Center, says testosterone from gels or creams can be transferred through skin-to-skin contact or from clothing and absorbed by someone else.

“You really have to be careful to wash your hands well and not expose others,” adds Dr. Smith. Contact with excess testosterone can affect children’s genitalia and cause premature pubic hair development and aggressive behavior.

Alexa is a Denver-based contributor who covers all things lifestyle, wellness, travel, home, and beauty. When she's not writing, you can find her sweating it out at boxing or Pilates, planning her next travel adventure, or drinking red wine.

Jesse N Mills, MD, is the director of The Men's Clinic at UCLA as well as a clinical professor of urology at the David Geffen School of Medicine at UCLA.

#4993. Google's Quantum Chip Beats Classical Limits in 'Impossible' Game

Google's Quantum Chip Beats Classical Limits in 'Impossible' Game

Google says its Willow quantum processor won a mathematical coordination game 98.3% of the time, above the 88.9% maximum attainable by classical strategies, in a Science study published Thursday.

The experiment tests quantum contextuality, so measurement outcomes depend on what else is measured alongside them. Google researchers explained that “you cannot assign fixed, pre-existing values to everything you might measure.”

Don’t panic. The result does not mean Google has a commercial quantum computer, nor that its largest experiment beat every classical machine. The 98.3% figure came from four qubits—the basic unit of information used to encode data in quantum computing—the 105-qubit run tested a different, more limited challenge.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Google with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

The first test used the Mermin-Peres magic square game, which asks two players to fill intersecting parts of a three-by-three grid without communicating. Their answers must obey conflicting row-and-column rules and match at the shared square.

A prearranged classical strategy can succeed on no more than eight of nine assignments, or 88.9%. Entangled qubits can theoretically win every round, while Google’s physical processor won 98.3% across 900,000 trials, according to the researchers.

The researchers wrote that “our qubits coordinated better than any pre-written table allows.” Unavoidable hardware noise prevented the result from reaching the quantum ideal.

Google also measured contextuality. Any model with predetermined outcomes scores at most 4; quantum theory allows 6. Willow registered 5.590, with a reported uncertainty of 0.008.

A separate GHZ parity game asked up to 71 entangled qubits to deliver coordinated binary answers. Success was measured against the strongest classical winning rate, which approaches 50% with more players; Google remained above that limit throughout its tests, despite accumulating hardware noise.

Researchers also verified genuine entanglement across 45 qubits. In the hidden linear function task, a shallow quantum circuit had to produce a binary string satisfying mathematical constraints. The processor reached roughly 60% success using 105 qubits.

Google says the tasks exposed small, systematic errors that conventional randomized tests can hide. Its corporate blog called contextuality testing “a sensitive new method to catch subtle hardware errors,” potentially helping engineers evaluate circuits used for chemistry and materials research.

The work follows Google's October report of a 40-fold error reduction in preparing magic states, a resource needed for fault-tolerant quantum computing.

For Bitcoin, the looming danger is that a future fault-tolerant quantum computer could break digital signatures, but that is still far from happening. Google’s 105-qubit task was instead a shallow mathematical circuit, and its largest instance still failed in about four out of 10 runs.

#4990. California court terminates lawsuit against Coinbase over TerraUSD purchases

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

A federal judge has granted Coinbase's motion to dismiss a lawsuit brought by Larry Pearl, who purchased TerraUSD on Coinbase in May 2022 shortly before the token collapsed.

A U.S. federal judge has granted Coinbase's Coinbase Global, Inc. (COIN) motion to dismiss a proposed class-action lawsuit brought by Larry Pearl, who purchased the TerraUSD (UST) stablecoin on the trading platform shortly before the token collapsed.

In a Nov. 20 order, U.S. District Judge Maxine M. Chesney granted Coinbase's motion to dismiss. Judgment was entered three days later, terminating the case.

The plaintiff's latest lawsuit, which was the fourth amended complaint, asserted claims under negligent misrepresentation and violations of California's unfair competition, false advertising, and securities regulations.

According to his original complaint in June 2022, Pearl held approximately $13,000 worth of UST on Coinbase at the time of the collapse, which wiped out over 90% of the value of his holdings.

Pearl alleged that Coinbase misrepresented UST by failing to adequately disclose that it was an algorithmic stablecoin linked to sister cryptocurrency LUNA, rather than a reserve-backed asset.

On the consumer-protection claims based on alleged omissions, the court found that Coinbase's website and linked materials had already disclosed that UST was not backed by U.S. dollars held in a bank account and that minting UST required burning LUNA. The court also stated that trading data for UST and LUNA was publicly available.

The court dismissed several claims because they exceeded the scope of the limited leave to amend granted in an earlier order. The remaining claims were dismissed on the merits.

Pearl also alleged that UST was an unregistered security and that Coinbase either sold it directly or materially aided its sale with misleading statements. The court dismissed the securities claims, finding that Pearl failed to show UST was a security under the Howey test. He did not adequately allege a common enterprise or an expectation of profit.

Terra's UST lost its peg amid large withdrawals from the high-yield Anchor Protocol. The failure of the algorithmic mechanism triggered a rapid collapse. UST fell well below its dollar peg while LUNA’s supply hyperinflated, and both tokens lost nearly all of their value within days, wiping out tens of billions of dollars in market value across the Terra ecosystem.

Following the collapse, Terraform Labs co-founder Do Kwon left South Korea. He was arrested in Montenegro in March 2023 while traveling on falsified documents and was extradited to the U.S. in late 2024. In December 2025, a federal judge in New York sentenced him to 15 years in prison after he pleaded guilty to fraud charges.

#4989. Bitcoin Rebounds to $61K After Weekend Plunge to Two-Year Low

Bitcoin Rebounds to $61K After Weekend Plunge to Two-Year Low

Bitcoin recovered to around $61,300 early on Monday after a turbulent weekend that saw it fall as low as $55,298, its lowest level since September 2024, as a slide in tech stocks and expectations of tighter Federal Reserve policy rattled risk assets.

The leading cryptocurrency was up 0.5% over 24 hours at time of publication, but down 4.5% over the past week, according to CoinGecko data. It remains about 51% below its all-time high of $126,080.

The weekend low came a day after Bitcoin broke below $60,000 on Friday, as AI stocks slumped following Anthropic's IPO.

"Bitcoin's sharp volatility over the weekend was primarily the result of a steep pullback in U.S. tech stocks, expectations of further monetary policy tightening by the Federal Reserve, and deleveraging across global risk assets," Tim Sun, senior researcher at HashKey Group, told Decrypt.

If growth expectations keep falling while inflation keeps the Fed hiking, "two key pillars that previously supported risk-asset rallies—ample liquidity and strong growth expectations—could be weakened simultaneously," Sun said. More than $2 billion in leveraged liquidations over the weekend had released some short-term selling pressure, he added, creating conditions for a possible rebound.

Spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $669.2 million in net outflows on Friday, their largest single-day outflow since June 25, according to SoSoValue data. That brought last week's net outflows to $1.79 billion, the funds' third consecutive week of withdrawals topping $1 billion, and November's total so far to $4.14 billion, by Decrypt's calculation. Total net assets fell to $71.3 billion, the lowest since June 30.

"The weekend correction looks less like a Bitcoin-specific event and more like part of a wider market correction," Neil Steinhardt, head of Nexo U.S., told Decrypt, noting that gold and equities also fell. ETF outflows had worsened the drawdown, he said, adding that equities appeared to have led the sell-off.

It is now below its median since launch, while outflows run near $260M per day.

The last stretch this quiet, in mid-2024, came before the run to $100K. Periods of apathy tend to be better times to accumulate than periods of hype. pic.twitter.com/xiJ3HF9cLj

— glassnode (@glassnode) November 22, 2026

Onchain analytics firm Glassnode took a longer view. ETF trading volume has fallen below its median since the funds launched, while outflows are running near $260 million a day, the firm tweeted on Sunday. The last stretch this quiet, in mid-2024, preceded Bitcoin's run to $100,000, it said.

BitMEX co-founder Arthur Hayes struck a more bearish note. Bitcoin had "decisively" broken below its previous local low, he tweeted on Saturday, adding that "$50,000 will be the next real test" if the Nasdaq kept falling. On Sunday evening, as Nasdaq futures weakened in Asian trading, Hayes tweeted that Bitcoin could retest $55,000, and noted that South Korea's Kospi index had fallen 5%.

$BTC just decisively broke below the previous local low. If Nasdaq keeps puking, $50,000 will be the next real test. pic.twitter.com/vn0VSpuWAz

— Arthur Hayes (@CryptoHayes) November 21, 2026

On Decrypt Predict, users put a 17% chance on Bitcoin falling to $55,000 before the end of November, as of 5:56 a.m. ET on Monday. The market settles on Binance's BTC/USDT price, which fell as low as $55,130 last week, according to the Binance chart Hayes shared. Users put a 1% chance on Bitcoin climbing back to $82,500 this month.

Traders now turn to October's personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation data, due on Wednesday, followed by the November jobs report on December 4 and the Fed's December 9 meeting.

The core PCE report "could help show whether the Fed is leaning toward 25 or 50 basis points," Steinhardt said, with markets then guided by the December meeting and comments from Fed Chair Kevin Warsh and other officials.

Traders are leaning toward the smaller move. As of 6:00 a.m. ET on Monday, CME FedWatch put the odds of a quarter-point hike on December 9 at 96.2%, up from 88.6% on Friday, with a 3.8% chance that rates stay unchanged. The odds of a half-point increase stood at zero, from 4.5% a week earlier.

U.S. spot selling remained heavy last week, according to a note from Bitfire Research shared with Decrypt. The Coinbase Bitcoin Premium Index, which tracks the gap between Bitcoin's price on Coinbase and on Binance, fell as low as negative 0.23%, the firm said.

Sun said he saw "no clear bullish catalyst" over the next one to two weeks, and expected Bitcoin to stay in a "high-volatility consolidation phase." Cooler inflation, steadier stocks and a return of ETF inflows could support a more sustainable recovery, he said, while hotter inflation and further tech losses could send Bitcoin back to its prior lows.

#4987. DOJ Moves to Forfeit $1.1 Billion in USDC Stolen in Binance Hack

The Justice Department filed to forfeit more than $1.1 billion in USDC stolen from Binance by North Korean hackers, and Circle agreed to issue replacement tokens.

The Justice Department filed a civil forfeiture complaint Monday to take ownership of more than $1.1 billion in USDC that North Korean hackers stole from Binance last month, tokens that Circle froze but says it cannot move.

The complaint, filed in U.S. District Court for the District of Columbia, targets all USDC held at 36 addresses across 18 blockchains. The department said the U.S. has now helped freeze, seize or return more than $2.9 billion in crypto from multiple sources since the Oct. 29 theft.

On Nov. 12, the department said it had court authority to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion, out of a joint government and industry freeze of more than $2.7 billion. A temporary restraining order issued that day was served on Circle the same day.

Monday’s filing is the step that would let the government take title to the funds.

Circle blocked access to the USDC on or about Oct. 29, the complaint says. But the stablecoin issuer told prosecutors it does not have the technical ability to transfer the tokens out of the blocklisted addresses, which remain under the hackers’ control.

Instead, Circle has agreed to issue new USDC equal to the frozen amount and send it to the U.S. once a court enters a forfeiture order. The complaint calls those tokens “Substitute USDC.”

Several of the addresses are smart contract and hot wallet addresses on Binance’s own platform. The hackers took them over in the attack and drained some, but not all, of the funds before Circle stepped in, prosecutors wrote. “It appears Binance no longer controlled them,” the complaint says.

The filing also lays out how investigators say the breach began. A Binance employee contacted by a purported recruiter agreed to a job interview and was told to download software that contained malware, which matches what the exchange said earlier this month.

The infected device let the attackers plant an EIP-7702 injection. That feature, added to Ethereum in a 2025 upgrade, lets ordinary wallets temporarily act like smart contracts. Prosecutors said the hackers used it to take control of Binance’s hot wallets and route funds to a “sweeper” contract that moved them out as soon as they arrived.

The first large transfer, about $780 million in USDC, left Binance on Ethereum at about 4:41 p.m. ET on Oct. 29, the complaint says. Another $43 million followed 18 minutes later. Investigators also traced AVAX that was split up, bridged and swapped until it became about $1.56 million in USDC.

U.S. agencies attribute the theft to North Korean military hackers known as the Lazarus Group, or APT38. Investigators said some wallets that collected Binance funds also received money from the roughly $387 million Bitget hack in September, which the exchange’s CEO tied to North Korea.

The complaint also lists earlier heists it attributes to the group, including about $308 million from Japan’s DMM Bitcoin in May 2024 and about $1.5 billion from Bybit in February 2025.

“North Korean cyber hackers stole more than $6 billion in cryptocurrency from Binance, but swift coordination between the government and private sector partners helped prevent a substantial portion of those assets from disappearing,” said Assistant Attorney General A. Tysen Duva of the Criminal Division.

FBI Cyber Branch Operations Director Brett Leatherman said North Korea “depends on sophisticated laundering techniques” to turn stolen crypto into money for the regime. The FBI, the Secret Service and IRS Criminal Investigation are handling the case.

“Secret Service investigators worked with precision to identify and freeze stolen funds within minutes of the hack,” said Special Agent in Charge Todd Bratz. “The criminals behind this intrusion believed they could outrun law enforcement, and they have been proven wrong.”

#4986. Kalshi halts operations in Utah while dodging immediate ban in New York

Kalshi said it 'made the difficult decision' to pause its operations in Utah after a federal judge allowed the state to enforce its gambling laws.

Kalshi has suspended operations in Utah after a federal judge allowed the state to enforce its gambling laws, while avoiding an immediate ban in New York as its court denied the state's request to shut down the prediction market platform.

A Kalshi spokesperson told The Block on Monday that the company "made the difficult decision" to pause its operations in Utah while it awaits a court decision on the Commodity Futures Trading Commission's request to block the state from enforcing against Kalshi.

"This is an unfortunate outcome for the more than 177,000 Utahns who use Kalshi," said McGavick. "We will continue to fight this in court, and we look forward to returning to Utah once our legal position is vindicated."

The suspension comes amid mounting nationwide legal pressure, as federal regulators and state authorities remained locked in escalating battles over who has the authority to regulate prediction market platforms. The CFTC has maintained that it has exclusive jurisdiction over such platforms under the Commodity Exchange Act (CEA), while several states have argued that certain event contracts violate state gambling laws.

Specifically in Utah, U.S. District Judge Robert J. Shelby ruled in August that the CEA does not preempt the state's anti-gambling laws against Kalshi's event contracts, rejecting the company's claim of federal preemption. Kalshi later appealed, but the federal court of appeals denied its request to block Utah's enforcement in September.

Last month, the CFTC filed a lawsuit against Utah and requested the court to temporarily block the state from enforcing its gambling laws against CFTC-regulated prediction markets, including Kalshi. Judge Shelby has not yet ruled.

Meanwhile in New York, a judge has denied Attorney General Letitia James' request to shut down Kalshi, allowing the prediction market platform to continue operating in the state for now.

During a hearing on Monday, New York Supreme Court Justice Melissa Crane rejected the state's bid for a temporary restraining order (TRO) that seeks to block Kalshi from operating. Kalshi has previously said the potential TRO could shut it down nationwide, though James said that was not her goal.

According to a series of X posts from gaming lawyer Daniel Wallach, the judge said she was uncomfortable granting relief this early in the case, particularly given the scope of the proposed injunction.

"The judge denied the state's request for a TRO, recognizing that there are complex questions about derivatives and preemption," said Kalshi spokesperson Elisabeth Diana. "We remain committed to working with the state on a path forward that works for our 1.5 million customers and for the state of New York."

The CFTC and the Department of Justice also sued New York in April, arguing state enforcement actions against prediction market operators are preempted by federal law.

In recent weeks, the CFTC and the DOJ have filed lawsuits against several states, including Utah, Arkansas, and Louisiana, seeking to block state enforcement against prediction market platforms.

#4984. CFTC staff say exchanges can count Bitcoin toward operating reserves

The staff guidance allows Bitcoin, Ether and payment stablecoins to count towards operating reserves.

US Commodity Futures Trading Commission staff said Monday that CFTC-regulated derivative exchanges and clearinghouses can count Bitcoin, Ether and payment stablecoins toward meeting their financial resource requirements.

CFTC rules require derivatives exchanges, swap trading venues and clearinghouses to maintain enough financial resources to cover at least 12 months of operating costs on a rolling basis.

The latest staff guidance clarifies that certain crypto assets can count toward part of that requirement, but with a discount to their market value. CFTC staff said they would accept a “haircut” of at least 20% for Bitcoin and Ether and 2% for qualifying payment stablecoins to account for the risk of holding those assets.

The guidance gives CFTC-regulated exchanges and entities more flexibility in how they meet their operating reserve requirements. CFTC-regulated entities include Coinbase Derivatives, Crypto.com’s North American Derivatives Exchange and Bitnomial Exchange, as well as prediction markets Kalshi and Polymarket US.

However, they will still need to meet a separate liquidity requirement with assets such as cash or highly liquid securities.

The advisory represents the views of staff at the Division of Market Oversight and Division of Clearing and Risk, and is not considered a formal rule change approved by the Commission.

#4983. Solana Meme Coin for Fiona the Hippo Raises $100K for Cincinnati Zoo Charity

Solana Meme Coin for Fiona the Hippo Raises $100K for Cincinnati Zoo Charity

Fiona the Hippo, a fan-favorite pygmy hippo at the Cincinnati Zoo, died last week after swallowing a hat that accidentally ended up in her enclosure. Crypto bros have created a meme coin in her honor—raising $100,000 for charity in the process.

The tragic death came after a visitor tossed their knitted hat in the air, which then got caught by a ceiling fan and flung it through a small window into Fiona's habitat. The pygmy hippo lost her appetite as the hat got stuck in her small intestine, and veterinary staff tried their best to help Fiona pass the hat naturally—as surgery was deemed too risky. Unfortunately, Fiona died on Thursday, November 19, at nine years old.

As is often the case, crypto bros rushed to create a Solana meme coin that surged to a $3.52 million market capitalization the day after Fiona's death. It has since dropped more than 50% to $1.5 million, per DEX Screener.

Fiona the hippo has died after she ate a visitor's hat that fell into her enclosure at the Cincinnati Zoo. pic.twitter.com/YHbjoEEDhb

The Pump.fun-created token is donating 100% of the generated creator rewards to the Zoological Society of Cincinnati, the non-profit that runs the zoo and its scientific programs. So far, according to Donate.gg, $103,600 has been donated.

But, of course, profits are at the forefront of crypto traders' minds. One of the top traders turned a $1,637 investment into $43,800 in a matter of days, according to DEX Screener.

This sparked global outrage, as the gorilla was killed through no fault of his own. In turn, Harambe became one of the most viral memes of the 2010s.

"I think stricter action needs to be taken against both visitors and zoo officials to make sure things like this don’t happen again," the trader said. "Honestly, I feel like a big part of the problem is the lack of accountability and consequences. Unless people are held responsible, nothing is really going to change."

#4982. South Korea Unveils Crypto Bill Capping Exchange Ownership at 20%

South Korea’s Financial Services Commission on Tuesday unveiled a government bill that would cap a major shareholder’s stake in a crypto exchange at 20%, open the door to domestic coin offerings and let bank-led groups issue won stablecoins.

The draft, titled the Act on the Innovation of the Digital Asset Industry and Protection of Users, was presented at a Virtual Asset Committee meeting chaired by FSC Chairman Lee Eog-weon. It would replace Korea’s two-track system, built on an anti-money-laundering law and a 2024 user protection act, with a single framework covering the industry, markets and users.

Lee said the law would raise trust and transparency in Korea’s digital asset market and help draw new money into the industry. He said the government would do everything it can to see the bill fully debated in the National Assembly and enacted quickly.

Under the bill, exchanges would become “digital asset platform operators,” a designation reserved for firms that hold separate licenses for dealing, brokerage and custody. The FSC modeled the ownership limit on the rules for alternative trading systems in the stock market.

Major shareholders could own up to 20%, or up to 34% with FSC approval under a presidential decree. Shares above the limit would lose their voting rights. Existing exchanges would get three years after designation to comply, and small and midsize exchanges could get up to three more.

The cap could reach some of Korea’s best-known exchanges. Mirae Asset Consulting owns 97.15% of Digital X, the exchange formerly known as Korbit, The Korea Herald reported. Naver Financial’s planned share-swap takeover of Dunamu, the operator of Upbit, would make the exchange a wholly owned subsidiary.

The FSC declined to comment on specific companies, the paper said.

“Ownership restrictions are necessary to strengthen exchanges’ public accountability and neutrality,” Yoo Young-jun, director general of the FSC’s Digital Financial Policy Bureau, said at a briefing, citing more than 10 million exchange accounts in Korea.

The bill would also move trade execution, listing reviews and market surveillance from self-regulation to formal rules. Exchanges would need outside directors to make up a majority of their boards, plus audit committees and compliance officers.

Domestic initial coin offerings would be allowed as long as issuers file a white paper with the FSC before marketing tokens to the public. The release pointed to a platform company that sidestepped Korea’s ICO ban by setting up a foundation abroad, then sold its token through local exchanges. Exchanges would have to file and publish a report on each token they list.

Stablecoins, called digital payment tokens in the bill, could only be issued by licensed firms with at least 5 billion won in capital. At first, the FSC plans to allow issuance only by consortiums in which banks hold a majority stake, though fintech and tech companies could be the largest single shareholder.

Issuers would have to hold reserves of at least 100% of tokens outstanding at banks or similar institutions, and they could not pay holders interest. Tokens with more than 1 trillion won outstanding could be designated as significant, and the Bank of Korea could request joint inspections of their issuers.

Licensed firms would carry no-fault liability to users for hacks and system outages. The FSC could impose punitive fines of up to 10% of revenue when a security lapse causes an incident. The release cited a November 2025 theft from one exchange’s wallet and an October 2026 hack at another, without naming either.

Insider-trading bans would extend to former employees for a year after they leave. The FSC could also freeze accounts when there is substantial suspicion of unfair trading. Regulators have already shown a willingness to punish exchanges, having fined Bithumb $24.6 million and suspended its new-user services for six months over anti-money-laundering violations in March.

Last week, the FSC was reported to be preparing guidelines that would let about 3,500 listed companies and professional-investor firms buy bitcoin. The new bill now heads to the National Assembly.

#4979. Bitcoin slips back toward $60,000 as Jensen Huang’s Nvidia return rally fizzles

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Analysts said Huang's return was a reflex rally that did not resolve the macro headwinds bitcoin is facing as a risk asset.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) fell back toward the $60,000 zone during late evening Monday after briefly surging above $64,000 on news of Jensen Huang's return as Nvidia CEO.

The world's largest cryptocurrency climbed as high as $64,500 shortly after Nvidia's board announced Huang's return, alongside a broader stock market jump and a 31.5% surge in NVDA stocks. This reversed a weekend decline near $55,000 that followed his scandal and resignation earlier this month.

By late Monday, however, bitcoin erased most of the gains from earlier in the day and traded as low as $59,603. It has since recovered partially to trade at $60,443.

Min Jung, associate researcher at Presto Research, described the spike as a reflex rally rather than a shift in fundamentals.

"It squeezed shorts for a few hours and then faded, because the news only fixed a leadership shock at Nvidia," Jung told The Block. “The things actually weighing on bitcoin — a strong dollar, hawkish rate pricing, and steady ETF outflows — haven’t changed.”

Zeus Research Analyst Dominick John similarly attributed the rally to "short covering" that ran into overhead supply once forced buying settled. "The failure to hold these levels suggests that sustained spot demand remains weak, leaving the market vulnerable to further downside," John said.

The analyst warned that a sustained break below $60,000 could open a path toward $58,000 and the recent low of $55,000, while reclaiming the $62,000 to $64,000 zone would improve the near-term outlook.

Analysts largely agreed that Huang's return addresses only a fraction of the headwinds facing bitcoin as a risk asset. Jung cited inflation data, Federal Reserve rate expectations, the dollar, and AI-stock volatility as the dominant drivers, with crypto ETF outflows as a main amplifier. Last week, U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs saw nearly $1.79 billion in net outflows, the largest weekly withdrawal since June.

Traders are now focused on Wednesday's core Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE) data, a key inflation indicator, and December's Federal Open Market Committee (FOMC) decision.

"With a 25bps hike in December almost fully priced in, I think the market will be particularly sensitive to anything that brings a 50bps hike back into the conversation," ViaBTC Chief Analyst Jeff Ko said. "Within crypto, I would watch whether spot Bitcoin ETF flows turn positive following Huang's return. I am also keeping an eye on USDT, which is still trading at around 0.9987, below the 0.999 level that has marked risk-off phases before."

#4978. UniCredit Opens Bitcoin to Italian Savers, Capping Gains at 65%

UniCredit began selling a six-year, capital-protected certificate linked to BlackRock's iShares Bitcoin ETP to retail clients in Italy, with gains capped at 65%.

UniCredit on Tuesday began selling a capital-protected certificate that lets retail clients in Italy follow the bitcoin price, a product the bank calls the first investment solution of its kind in the country.

The six-year, euro-denominated certificate will be listed on the Munich stock exchange and is linked to the iShares Bitcoin ETP managed by BlackRock, UniCredit said in a release. The placement period runs from Nov. 24 to Dec. 18, 2026.

The note is open only to UniCredit Italia retail clients with specific characteristics, and only through the bank’s advisory service. It can’t be bought independently or by investors who aren’t UniCredit clients, and the minimum investment is €1,000, Italian newswire Teleborsa reported.

A capital-protected certificate is a structured note. The issuing bank promises to repay the original investment at maturity and pays a return tied to an underlying asset, in this case bitcoin through the BlackRock product. Buyers hold a claim on the bank, not bitcoin itself.

If bitcoin is down at maturity, holders get 100% of their capital back. If it rises, they share in the gain up to a maximum return of 65% on the capital invested. Before maturity, the certificate’s price follows market conditions, so the protection applies only at the end of the six years.

That cap works out to roughly 8.7% a year at most, no matter how far bitcoin climbs. And because the certificate is UniCredit’s own debt, the protection depends on the bank’s ability to pay. Documents for a similar UniCredit note in Germany state that no third-party guarantee covers an issuer default.

“With the launch of this certificate, UniCredit is writing a new page of financial innovation in the country, confirming its market leadership,” said Chicco Di Stasi, Head of Group Investment Product Solutions at UniCredit.

“Diversification is no longer just a prudential rule, but the key to capturing the value of the future,” Di Stasi said, adding that the bank is responding to growing client demand and offering the product to Italian savers “for the very first time.”

Di Stasi said the bank is bringing to Italy “a pioneering model already proven by its success in Germany and Austria.” Those earlier notes are denominated in dollars and track BlackRock’s U.S.-listed iShares Bitcoin Trust instead.

HypoVereinsbank, UniCredit’s German unit, sold a version to private clients in March with a 160% cap, or 60% upside. UniCredit Bank Austria launched its own six-year note in April with a 175% cap, or 75% upside. Italy’s 65% falls between the two.

The bank has moved into bitcoin in steps. It first offered a capital-protected bitcoin product to professional clients only in 2025. In May, it placed a bitcoin and ether certificate for professional clients with a €100,000 minimum.

In September, UniCredit was reported to be weighing crypto custody, including the infrastructure to hold and trade digital assets. Elsewhere in Italy, Banca Sella became the first Italian bank licensed for crypto services under MiCA in May.

UniCredit is one of Europe’s largest banks. It reported €918.3 billion in total assets at Sept. 30 and a record €3.3 billion in third-quarter net profit.

Bitcoin traded near $60,400 on Tuesday, more than 50% below its record above $126,000.

#4977. Czech Authorities Charge 15 Over $12M Unlicensed Crypto Casino

Czech Authorities Charge 15 Over $12M Unlicensed Crypto Casino

Czech customs officers and police have charged 15 people over BETFIN, a crypto casino that investigators say operated without a gambling license while presenting itself as a decentralized project, authorities said on Thursday.

The group's unlawful gains so far total about 267 million koruna ($12.1 million), according to a joint statement from the Czech customs service and the National Centre Against Organised Crime (NCOZ). Officers detained 20 people and searched more than 40 homes and other premises in October, in an operation involving nearly 300 customs officers and police, as well as Europol staff.

Three alleged organizers are being held in custody. Those charged are accused of participating in an organized criminal group and operating gambling without a license, and the main organizers face up to 13.5 years in prison if convicted. Authorities seized assets worth about 100 million koruna ($4.5 million), including property, vehicles, jewelry, cash and crypto.

The agencies described it as the first Czech case in which suspected illegal gambling centered on a decentralized online casino using blockchain infrastructure, smart contracts and its own token.

BETFIN presented itself as a decentralized casino with its own token traded on decentralized exchanges. Investigators hired a specialist firm to examine its smart contracts on the Polygon network, and analyzed more than 2.3 million bets placed by about 28,500 players. The project was promoted on social media, Telegram and Discord, as well as in online videos and at conferences.

Users could join in two ways, Michal Šimůnek, director of the customs service's search and supervision section, told a press conference near Prague on Thursday, according to the Czech News Agency (ČTK). They could buy tokens to play casino games such as roulette, or buy them as investors and become co-owners of the casino. Despite its decentralized branding, the group ran a centrally controlled system, he said.

A large share of player accounts were created by automated programs to inflate activity, Šimůnek said. "This created the casino's supposed profit, which was then redistributed to investors," he said, in comments translated from Czech.

Developers issued billions of tokens and steered investors to buy them off-exchange, which let the group raise money without moving the token's market price, Šimůnek added. Customs officers also found that the declared value of all tokens in circulation, more than 45 billion koruna ($2 billion), far exceeded the roughly 11 million koruna ($500,000) of liquidity on exchanges.

Czech news site Seznam Zprávy reported the raids in October, saying those detained included YouTubers who had promoted BETFIN, and that the project's token had already fallen close to zero in 2025.

The token, BET, has fallen further since. It traded at about $0.000001 on Monday, more than 99.9% below its all-time high of $0.002906, and hit a new all-time low within the past week, according to CoinGecko data. Its 24-hour trading volume was about $1,100, with both CoinGecko and CoinMarketCap putting its total supply at around 777.8 billion tokens.

NCOZ deputy director Petr Danko said using blockchain technology is not illegal, but using it outside the law is. The Czech state is the injured party, he said, because the group was required to obtain authorization to run gambling. Whether investors suffered losses will depend on the ongoing investigation, he added.

The High Prosecutor's Office in Olomouc is overseeing the case. Criminal proceedings are continuing, and authorities said they could not share further details.

#4975. South Korea’s draft crypto bill sets path for domestic ICOs while capping exchange shareholder stakes at 20%

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The bill would allow initial coin offerings and the issuance of local stablecoins, while setting stricter licensing requirements and ownership caps for crypto exchanges.

South Korea's Financial Services Commission (FSC) published the government's draft of the country's upcoming regulatory framework for digital assets on Tuesday, covering previously unregulated areas such as initial coin offerings and stablecoins.

The country's top financial regulator described its version — titled the "Act on the Innovation of the Digital Asset Industry and Protection of Users" — as a single framework to promote industry growth, market fairness, and user safety.

Once enacted, it would mark South Korea's second major legal framework for crypto, following a user protection-focused law enacted in 2024.

"The bill we are announcing today is expected to raise the trust and transparency of Korea’s digital asset market and take the industry’s competitiveness to the next level," FSC Chairman Lee Eok-won said in a translated statement. "It should also help attract new capital, foster new industries, and create jobs. However, we must thoroughly prepare for new risks and build resilience so the market can recover even in a crisis."

Under the draft, South Korea would reclassify "virtual assets" as "digital assets" and establish clearer boundaries for crypto businesses. Firms would be categorized by function — trading, brokerage, or custody — each requiring a separate license. Operating an exchange would require all three. Another category of asset managers would face a lighter registration process with the FSC rather than full licensing.

The bill also seeks to establish a regulatory framework for Korean won-pegged stablecoins, formally termed "digital payment tokens." Initially, only consortiums in which banks collectively hold a majority stake would be authorized to issue them, though non-bank firms could participate as the largest single shareholder.

Stablecoin issuers would be required to hold 100% reserves at banks, redeem tokens at par on demand, while barred from paying interest. The draft also notes that Tether (USDT) and other offshore stablecoins could circulate domestically if they meet the FSC's standards.

Additionally, the legislation would allow domestic initial coin offerings (ICOs), provided issuers file a whitepaper with the FSC before launching the token.

The draft also opens the door for market-making activity. Firms licensed to trade digital assets for their own account would be allowed to act as liquidity providers, posting buy and sell quotes after signing a contract with an issuer or an exchange. Exchanges would then have to publicly report the quote history and performance of those liquidity providers.

Despite heavy pushback, the bill retains a 20% ownership cap per major shareholder for crypto exchanges, which can be extended up to 34% with FSC approval. Existing exchanges would have a three-year grace period to comply.

The FSC also wants to strengthen user protections by introducing internal control standards comparable to traditional finance, including continuous monitoring of customer assets and strict IT security requirements. In the event of a hack or system failure, firms would be held strictly liable to compensate users for losses, even without proven negligence.

"The government will do its utmost to ensure the bill is fully discussed in the National Assembly and enacted as soon as possible," Lee said. The draft will now head to the legislature, where lawmakers will debate it and potentially merge it with competing proposals.

Meanwhile, local news outlet Newsis reported that industry participants have raised concerns that the bill imposes heavy burdens on crypto firms, forcing existing platforms to undergo new licensing processes and restructure around the 20% ownership limit.

One anonymous industry source told Newsis that the bill, if enacted, could weaken domestic firms' footing in the market and drive more users toward overseas platforms.

#4974. Ethereum Foundation doubles protocol security team ahead of overhaul

The foundation said its next upgrade will be harder than the Merge, and the required pace of development will make it easier to ship bugs.

The Ethereum Foundation says it has doubled its protocol security team as it accelerates work to overhaul Ethereum’s defenses against threats posed by quantum computers and artificial intelligence.

In a Monday blog post, Ethereum Foundation researcher Kev Wedderburn said that while Ethereum has been planning ways to mitigate threats posed by quantum computers, AI has now emerged as a more urgent risk.

The planned overhaul would change how Ethereum validators sign messages, how users authorize transactions and how Ethereum verifies data posted by rollups.

“This upgrade is harder than the Merge, and we have less time,” said Wedderburn, while acknowledging that faster development “makes it easier to ship bugs.”

He said in response, the foundation has expanded its protocol security team to ensure formal verification and security work can keep pace with development.

“Every layer of the protocol and ecosystem will need to adapt to the reality that hardening the protocol is now our highest priority,” he added.

Earlier this month, Ethereum Foundation researcher Justin Drake outlined a December 2028 target for completing Ethereum’s post-quantum and post-AI transition, a year earlier than the foundation’s previously announced quantum-resistance deadline.
