#4973. Ethereum Foundation doubles protocol security team, calls AI a ‘more urgent risk’ than quantum

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Ethereum developers face deadlines for a post-quantum transition as the Foundation raises security staffing and AI advances reshape its threat assessment.

Artificial intelligence has emerged as a “more urgent risk” than quantum computing, according to the Ethereum Foundation, as models increasingly identify vulnerabilities in production software faster than people can review them.

In a blog post published Monday, the Ethereum Foundation said AI models are also beginning to improve on decades-old algorithms, adding to concerns about the mathematical assumptions underlying cryptography.

The Foundation said it has doubled the size of its protocol security team as it works to introduce hash-based cryptography across Ethereum’s consensus mechanism, user accounts, and data blobs. The transition has an internal deadline of 2029.

Under the plan, validators would use hash-based keys, wallets would support migration to hash-based signatures, and blobs would move to hash-based commitments.

The EF said researcher Justin Drake proposed a December 2028 target for the upgrade during Devcon in Mumbai, though it maintains its 2029 internal deadline ahead of a scheduled timetable review in January 2027.

Ethlabs, a nonprofit founded by former Ethereum Foundation researchers, has separately proposed moving the target to 2027 through an initiative it calls pqai27. Ethlabs cited two developments behind its proposed acceleration: the U.S. government's revised 2030 quantum risk target, brought forward from 2035, and Google's plan to reach post-quantum readiness by 2029.

Meanwhile, independent Ethereum core developer funding group Protocol Guild reported that its membership fell 9.5% to 172 in its quarterly audit. Organizer Trent Van Epps warned that a funding shortfall could slow upgrades and force individual development teams to shut down.

#4972. Strategy Buys $30M in Bitcoin, Moves $1B From USD Reserve to Cash Pot

Strategy Buys $30M in Bitcoin, Moves $1B From USD Reserve to Cash Pot

Strategy bought 457 BTC for $29.6 million last week and moved $1 billion out of the reserve that backs its dividend payments into a general-purpose cash account, the company said on Monday.

The Bitcoin treasury firm paid an average of $64,898 per coin between November 16 and November 22, according to an SEC filing. Its fourth straight weekly purchase lifted its holdings to 851,305 BTC, acquired for a total of $64.23 billion at an average of about $75,446 per coin.

Strategy has acquired 457 $BTC and repurchased $212M of $STRC. We increased our Digital Credit Securities Repurchase Program by $1B and reallocated $1B from our USD Reserve to USD Cash. As of 11/22/26, we hold 851,305 BTC and $4.8B of USD Assets. $MSTR https://t.co/Wklom6dzrm

— Michael Saylor (@saylor) November 23, 2026

At the current Bitcoin price of about $61,233, per Decrypt data, those holdings are worth about $52.1 billion, roughly $12.1 billion less than Strategy paid. Bitcoin fell as low as $55,298 over the weekend, its lowest level since September 2024.

Executive chairman Michael Saylor tweeted that, as of November 22, Strategy held 851,305 BTC and $4.8 billion in U.S. dollar assets.

Strategy's board authorized management to move up to $1 billion from its USD Reserve, which is meant to cover dividends on its preferred stock and interest on its debt, to its USD Cash account. Management can use that account for broader purposes, including buying Bitcoin, topping up the reserve and other capital management.

After the move, the USD Reserve stood at $3.73 billion and USD Cash at $1.09 billion. The reserve held $4.77 billion on November 8, according to an earlier filing. Strategy drew $17.6 million from it to pay preferred dividends last week.

Strategy repurchased 2,183,747 shares of STRC, its variable-rate preferred stock, for $211.8 million, down from $228 million the week before. It funded the Bitcoin purchase and $147 million of the buyback by selling 1,925,676 shares of MSTR stock for $176.7 million in net proceeds, and paid the remaining $64.8 million from USD Cash.

The board also raised the cap on its digital credit securities repurchase program, which covers its preferred stock, from $3 billion to $4 billion, leaving $1.12 billion available. Before the increase, about $120 million remained, after two weeks of STRC buybacks totaling about $440 million. Another $1 billion remains available to buy back MSTR stock.

The changes come a week before index provider MSCI removes MSTR from its MSCI USA Index after trading closes on November 30, which also takes it out of broader benchmarks such as the MSCI World index. Strategy has estimated that funds linked to MSCI indexes hold about 13 million of its shares.

#4970. The 15 Best Jeans for Men, Tested and Reviewed by Style Editors

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

FEW MENSWEAR PIECES nail durability and easy style in one package like a great pair of jeans. For me, it’s the starting point for most of my outfits. But once you start paying attention, you realize how wide the gap is between pairs that just look good and pairs that can go with a bunch of different looks. Some are so stiff like a straitjacket, others stretch out so much they feel like leggings rather than denim.

Over the past five years, I’ve tested more than 60 pairs across seasons—winter layering, dead-of-summer heat, and everything in between—alongside Men’s Health’s fashion team. We looked at different fits, washes, and cuts across a range of builds and style preferences. Skinny fits are out. Instead, go for relaxed, straight, or straight-slim cuts that fall at the widest part of your legs for a flattering drape.

The Levi’s 501 is the blueprint of jeans—the one that every other pair takes notes from. I’ve had the straight-leg style in my closet for some years now and it fits comfortably every time. It has an easy fit through the hip and thigh, with a slight taper below the knee that doesn’t stack over my shoes. The 501s is made with 100% cotton, that’s sturdy and substantial but still breathable, even in the subway where things feel stuffy.

Levi’s offers five different inseams, which is not standard for most retail denim brands. There’s also plenty of washes and different levels of stretch including low and high stretch as well none at all. In other words, whether you’re tall, short, or somewhere in between, there’s an option for everyone and you can skip wasting money at a tailor. I found that the low-stretch pair gives you enough flex to move around without any stiffness. The no-stretch version broke in nicely after a few wears, and the high-stretch ones felt more laid-back without losing shape, but just a tad less breathable.

I’ve washed the 501s a million times and the fit always held up. It hasn’t become too baggy or too tight. Though I’ve machine dried mine before and only saw minimal shrinkage, I still recommend air drying to keep the fit where it’s at. The button fly took a minute to get used to, but it adds to that vintage feel. Out of all the washes I tried, the medium stonewash was the standout. It’s soft straight out of the box, with just the right amount of fading. It works with everything in my closet: t-shirts and hoodies, oxford shirts and loafers, boots and sneakers.

This pair of travel jeans from Bonobos is the most flexible jean I’ve tested. Honestly, they wear more like athletic pants than traditional denim. The brand uses a stretch cotton blend with a generous dose of elastane, which gives you full range of motion to handle all kinds of airport chaos: twisting and turning through TSA, sprinting to your gate, or squeezing into a tight middle seat.

I usually fly in sweatpants because I can’t stand feeling restricted on long flights, but these jeans completely changed that. On a 13-hour flight back from Japan, I stayed comfortable the entire time. No pinching, no stiffness, and I wasn’t overheating, either. The fabric is super breathable and lightweight, so even during hot layovers and road trips, I didn’t feel like I was melting.

Another big win: they hold their shape. A lot of high-stretch jeans get that saggy, baggy-knee look after a few hours, but not these. I’ve worn and washed them multiple times, and they still bounce back. And despite the gym-pant-level flexibility, they still look like real jeans; clean, tailored, and polished.

They’re also extremely low-maintenance. I packed them tightly into a carry-on for hours, pulled them out, and didn’t see a single crease. That wrinkle resistance is impressive. They looked crisp and ready to wear, no steamer or iron needed. And in true Bonobos fashion: they come in more waist sizes and fits than just about any brand we’ve tested, so no matter your build, you’ll find a version that works for you.

Abercrombie has one of the strongest takes on the baggy jeans right now. This style of denim in general has fully made its comeback, showing up on runways all the way down to everyday streetwear. The brand’s offering is easy to wear: lightweight, super relaxed through the leg, and has that slight “puddle” at the shoe—meaning the hem stacks a bit over your sneakers instead of hitting right at the ankle.

I like that it comes in multiple inseams, so you can actually control how much stacking you want depending on your height or how you like your jeans to sit. And with 20-plus colorways, it’s easy to find a version that fits your vibe, whether you want something low-key and neutral or a little more standout. I personally love the vintage black wash that reads clean and modern, even with the looser shape. In terms of fabric, it’s made from 100% cotton that already feels broken-in, so you’re not dealing with anything overly structured out of the gate.

I put on the Ford Standard Jean from Buck Mason, and could instantly feel how durable this pair was. Not only is this denim as tough as they come, it’s built to last and looks damn good doing it. It’s sourced from Japan’s Kaihara mills, which is known for blending traditional shuttle loom techniques with modern innovation. In other words, this fabric has that rich, textured character you expect from premium Japanese denim, but with a level of consistency that ensures long-term durability. At 14 oz, these jeans sit in that sweet spot—not too heavy, not too light—so it feels sturdy but won’t take forever to break in.

Unlike classic 100% cotton, this pair has a hint of stretch that lets me move freely from the jump. The mid-rise sits perfectly at my waist and I noticed the cut is straight through the thighs, giving ample room to breathe and take big, confident strides. If you’ve got some serious quads, that’s not a problem. These jeans can handle your muscles without compromising your style. It tapers neatly down below the knee, laying right on your sneakers for a clean, sharp finish. Whether I’m keeping it casual with a tee or dressing it up with a crisp button-down and blazer, it has that versatile edge to switch things up.

Most selvedge denim is super stiff like cardboard, but that’s not the case with this pair from 3sixteen. Since the cotton fabric blend is infused with a hint of spandex, it's wearable out of the gate. I didn’t go through an annoying break-in period, so I could actually sit down, bike to the bodega, or bend down low without cutting off circulation. The straight fit gives me breathing room instead of feeling painted on. It’s tapered in all the right places so you get that clean, tailored silhouette without feeling like you borrowed your little cousin’s pants. And they’re not stacking weird at the ankle or drowning your sneakers; these sit just right, especially cuffed.

This is Japanese selvedge denim from Kuroki Mills: a denim mill famed for its high-quality fabrics and innovative production techniques like indigo dyeing. Yes, the pair is priced north of $300, but you get your cost per wear, it goes with a bunch of different looks, and is built to last for years to come.

If you’re the kind of guy who thinks $15 jeans have to feel like cardboard and fit like a punishment, let me introduce you to the Amazon special that exceeds my expectations.These budget jeans clock in at just $15, but they don’t feel like a throwaway buy. The denim is a cotton-elastane blend, so while it’s not that stiff, 100% raw denim people love, it looks like classic denim and feels way more forgiving. That hint of stretch allows me to sit, squat, and climb stairs without any restriction. Honestly, the pair is also comfortable enough to travel in, which is not something I say lightly about most cheap jeans.

The fabric has a decent weight to it, too. It’s not paper-thin, not overly rigid; just that sweet middle ground that feels broken-in without looking flimsy. Now, are the jeans perfect? No. The zipper jammed on me a couple of times during the first few wears. Not a dealbreaker, because it loosened up and zipped smoothly after a bit, but worth noting. They’re functional, but they don’t have that tank-like durability you’d expect from a $200 pair.

Uniqlo’s straight jeans from its JW Anderson collaboration are a really clean, easy take on everyday denim. It’s got that classic straight-leg silhouette that’s not too baggy or too slim, so it feels wearable right away. I don’t feel like I’m fighting the jeans, but I also don’t feel swallowed by them. One of the standout qualities is the pre-washed finish. You get a subtle, broken-in fade straight out of the box that doesn’t feel stiff or raw.

Another thing to note: the inseam is 32, and on me this pair runs a little long, so there’s some stacking at the ankle. I’ve found it looks better cuffed. For taller guys, it creates a nice break over the shoe. When it comes time to style, it pairs nicely with oversized tees, structured outerwear, and pretty much anything you’d wear year-round. This pair just sits well, doesn't feel restrictive, and holds up as a reliable “grab-and-go” pair. Plus, it’s more affordable than most options on the list, meaning you can stock up on the three colorways without blowing too much money.

When you’re spending good money on denim, it should be built to last and AGOLDE’s jeans fit the bill. The moment I pulled one leg through, I could tell it was rigid yet surprisingly smooth. It’s made from non-stretch sustainable cotton, which breathes well and softens over time without losing structure. It also has some heft, so I wouldn’t wear it in high-80- or 90-degree temps, but I’ll be reaching for it from fall straight through spring.

Style-wise, it’s a nice departure from your classic blue denim, with a washed-out grey hue that looks sharp with a plain tee or lightweight flannel and boots. My only gripe is the fit: the jeans are already baggy and only come in a 32-inch inseam, which creates more stacking than I’d like at 5 '8". I had mine hemmed slightly to clean up the break. If you’re 5 ‘11” or taller, though, it’ll drape over your shoes or boots the way it’s intended without looking overly bunched.

If you’re looking to lean into the Western workwear trend without going full cowboy, AG’s classic bootcut Jean is an easy place to start. I wore this pair to the Beyonce Cowboy Carter concert to coincide with the album’s theme, but it also works just as well for everyday wear when you want to keep things more understated. For the office, you could wear it with a crisp button-down, simple tee, and minimal sneakers. From a fabric standpoint, the cotton-elastane blend has enough give to move with you, while the premium stretch denim from Japan’s renowned Kaihara mill still feels substantial.

It tapered through the thigh before opening slightly below the knee and sits naturally over cowboy boots. Though it looks just as sharp with clean leather sneakers if boots aren’t your thing. AG only offers one color, but I love the medium-blue wash, which has a clean, lived-in look that adds character without looking overly faded, making it easy to dress up or down. I’d throw it on with a plain white tee and overshirt for the weekend or a denim shirt and leather boots when I want to lean into that classic Americana vibe. Like AGOLDE’s jeans, this pair also only comes in a 32-inch inseam. In this case, it works for guys who are closer to 5’11” or taller.

Wrangler makes one of the most iconic cowboy jeans out there for decades. These were originally made for working cowboys and riders, but this timeless silhouette works for a casual office day or weekend outings. The denim is heavyweight cotton, so right away you feel that structure with a broken-in-over-time feel.

Fit-wise, it sits higher on the waist and runs straight through the leg with room where you actually need it—seat and thigh especially. That’s why guys with thicker legs tend to gravitate toward them. They don’t squeeze you or taper too hard, so you actually get comfort without losing that classic straight-leg Western shape. It’s not chasing trends or trying to be “updated.” And if you’re looking for a more affordable alternative to the AG Jeans, this is your best bet.

Brooks Brothers may be known for Oxford shirts and chinos, but our fashion editors were thoroughly impressed by the Americana brand’s denim. Its slim fit pair is made with premium, mid-weight denim that’s super robust, but soft enough to wear for hours, with a hint of spandex for extra mobility. The fit is where the pair truly shine—a precise, tailored cut that contours through the hips and thighs for a clean silhouette without being super tight. Although the jeans fit closer to the body, you don’t feel like you’re suffocating.

Every stitch is strategically placed, reflecting Brooks Brothers’ heritage in classic menswear while still feeling fresh and contemporary. Style-wise, these jeans bridge the gap between smart-casual and laid-back cool, so we’ve worn them to a baseball game as well as an office meeting. These jeans are a dream for a lean to moderately athletic build, though if you have a muscular lower body, you might want to size up.

I’ve probably tested over 30 pairs of dark wash jeans in the past few years, and they all start to blur together. But I can confirm that this one from Tommy Hilfiger is an exception. The straight cut is what really sold me. Because it’s regular from the hip to the hem, it doesn’t pinch when I sit down. You know that awful feeling when you’re at your desk or on the train and your jeans dig into your hips like a vice grip? None of that here. I wore these straight through a long shoot day and a subway ride home without needing to unbutton them the second I walked in the door.

The fabric blend is another win: it’s mostly cotton with a hit of lyocell. That synthetic blend softens things up in a way pure cotton denim never does. It almost feels broken-in right off the rack but still has the structure of a good pair of jeans. And unlike stretch-heavy blends, it doesn’t bag out or sag. The deep rich indigo wash makes it easy to dress things up with a blazer, but casual enough that I can throw on sneakers and a simple tee without it looking too forced.

Our style editors did some research and it turns out that there is no rule saying you can only wear white denim during the summer. Every guy could use a pair in their wardrobe, and make good use of it all year around. The best option we found is this pair from Ralph Lauren that has a pearly white texture, while providing the rugged comfort of a pair of jeans. It’s a straight fit but not too loose, and there was just a hint of stretch for a little boost in comfort.

If you haven’t worn white denim in the past because you’re just not sure how to style it, the trick is to think of something you already know how to work with and just switch this jean in. If you wear chinos in any shade of khaki or brown, a pair of white jeans will give an edgier look without having to change anything else about the outfit. You get the opposite effect when it comes to bluejeans, where swapping them for white elevates the feel of, what is otherwise, the same outfit.

The fit of a black jean is crucial to pulling off dressed up fit, and that is exactly what Lee did here. For starters, I’m 5’8 and the slim-straight cut elongates my profile, giving a sleek, balanced silhouette. This pair is tailored to hug my frame just right without feeling like I squeezed into skinny jeans. When wearing them out on multiple occasions, I still felt like I had enough room to move, thanks to the comforting stretch fabric.

Adorned with branded hardware and the leather logo patch, the aesthetic feels like a nod to the past, while the modern cut still keeps you looking fresh. In terms of style, this pair is the ultimate wardrobe chameleon, working in both casual and semi-dressy settings. It looked just as great with a simple tee and sneakers at a backyard BBQ, as it did with a sport coat and dress shoes at a theater show.

Indigo is the dye that makes most jeans blue. When a brand uses a really rich indigo, like Mavi did here, you get a beautiful range from deep, dark navy and light sky blue. The lighter fades gradually show up and get more pronounced with each wear in a jean like this.

Mavi nailed the modern athletic fit in this jean, providing plenty of room in the hip and thigh, along with a taper in the lower leg that looks clean and stylish. This fit is assisted by a blend of cotton, tencel, polyester, and elastane that is lightweight and stretchy. It was one of the most comfortable jeans for our testers with big quads.

The right fit is the foundation of great jeans. A well-fitting pair should flatter your body type, feel comfortable, and align with your personal style. Here's a breakdown of common fits:

A slim fit jean is slightly tapered from the thigh to the ankle for a modern, streamlined silhouette. It's great for lean, athletic, or average builds who want a sleek, put-together look. Not to get them confused with skinny jeans, they're not nearly as tight, but they are more form-fitting than straight-leg jeans. Because slim fit jeans are so sleek, they pair well with everything—tees, button-downs, and even blazers. In terms of footwear, it can be worn with dress shoes or sneakers.

A straight fit has a uniform width from hip to hem, meaning no tapering at the ankle. It works for all body types, and offers a relaxed but structured look. Offering a balanced silhouette that isn't too baggy or too tight, this fit works great for everyday wear, whether you're pairing it with a flannel, sweatshirt, or polo shirt.

A relaxed cut has extra room in the seat, thighs, and leg, so it’s the most comfortable fit, but it also gives laid-back that’s come back in style. It's ideal for bigger guys, muscular legs, and more breathability. We found it looks great with oversized or relaxed tops to maintain proportion.

A tapered cut is slightly wider in the thighs and gradually narrow toward the ankle. It's a sweet spot between straight-leg and slim-fit jeans—giving you some comfort up top while keeping things clean and tailored below. Ideal for guys with athletic thighs or anyone who wants a streamlined look without going full-blown skinny.

Elastane and spandex (same thing, just different names) are synthetic fibers known for thor serious stretch and bounce-back ability. They’re the secret sauce in modern jeans that makes them way comfier than the stiff, unyielding denim of decades past. A bit of elastane—usually around 1-2%— gives jeans just enough flex to move with you, making them ideal for guys who plan to wear their jeans all day everyday, whether that’s sitting at a desk, running errands, or just kicking back. The trade-off? Stretchy jeans don’t always hold their shape as well as OG 100% cotton denim.

No stretch, no shortcuts. At first, these jeans are stiff. But over time, they mold to your body, creating a custom fit. If you love the idea of breaking in a pair like a well-worn leather jacket, go for this kind—but be ready for a couple uncomfortable weeks.

The holy grail for denim lovers. Selvedge denim is considered top-tier because of its durability, excellent craftsmanship, and the way it develops over time. It’s woven on old-school shuttle looms, which produces a fancy self-finished edge inside the cuff. This means the fabric is denser, less prone to fraying, and typically of higher-quality than mass-produced denim. The real magic? It molds to your body and fades in a way that’s totally unique to how you wear them—no fake distressing needed. The downside? It’s usually stiff at first. Since most selvedge denim is raw (aka unwashed), it starts out rigid, like a thick cotton canvas—but, in the end, it rewards you with some of the best-looking, long-lasting jeans out there.

Your inseam isn't just a number—it dictates whether your jeans stack, skim, or flood. If your denim is too short—you're in capri territory, unless you're purposely rocking cropped jeans. If they're too long, we hope you like puddles of denim at your ankles. For that just-right length, your jeans should either break (gently touch the top of your shoes) or stack naturally if that's your thing.

Most brands offer 30", 32", and 34" inseams, but if you're a shorter or taller guy, look for brands that offer extended sizing or hemming services (Uniqlo and Levi's are great for this). Pro tip: If you like cuffing, go a little longer. If you want a clean, straight-down look, hem them or buy your exact length.

When it comes to denim type in terms of wash and color, you’re choosing between raw vs. washed and light vs. dark vs. everything in between. The right pick depends on how much patience you have, how polished you want to look, and whether you mind a little indigo rub-off. Here’s the breakdown:

This is denim in its purest form: untouched, unwashed, and dark as midnight. It starts out stiff and gradually fades in all the right places based on how you wear them. The trade-off? It takes commitment—you’ll need to break them in, and they might stain your lighter clothes and furniture (hello, indigo bleed).

Pre-washed for softness and color variation, so you get that broken-in look without the break-in period. If you want instant comfort and zero dye transfer, this is your move. If personal fades is your jam, go raw. As for a consistent color and softness from day one, go washed.

The most versatile option—dress it up with a blazer or down with a hoodie. Darker washes are slimming and work in almost any setting. Raw or rinse denim will develop natural fades over time, adding character.

A casual, everyday wash that looks good with everything. Already has some fading, so there’s no awkward stiff phase. Think of it as the sweet spot between too dressy and too relaxed.

Laid-back, retro, and effortlessly cool—perfect for summer or a more relaxed vibe. Looks great with white tees, sneakers, and vintage-inspired fits. Not as dressy as dark denim, but adds character to your wardrobe.

Since 2021, myself and the Men's Health style team have gone full denim detective mode—testing more than 50 different pairs, each worn for at least two weeks at a time, and six hours a day. As someone who’s 5"8" and typically wears 28W/30L, I know what works and what doesn’t. I threw these jeans into every case scenario—casual brunches, city commutes, long-haul flights, running errands, and yes, even squatting for good measure. I took detailed notes on fit, comfort, wash, inseam, and performance (stretch, moisture-wicking, breathability).We checked if they were true to size, how the fabric held up through hours of movement, whether they needed a marathon break-in, and if they could work in a variety of settings. Our testers also include a diverse range of body types to help us ensure an unbiased result for selecting the best jeans for men. We made sure our picks weren’t trendy jeans, but true workhorses that will look great years from now.

As the most versatile article of clothing a man can own, jeans have the ability to traverse many different types of formality. Given that denim originates from workwear, it inherently embodies a casual nature. That's why I suggest throwing in some contrast, like rocking penny loafers with laid-back tailoring.

For a more laid-back look, pair your jeans with a well-fitted tee and canvas or leather sneakers. But when aiming for a smarter appearance, combining dark wash jeans with a crisp button-down shirt or sweater with a pair of boots is a great idea as well.

On the other hand, for a business casual look, throw on a pair of suede loafers with dark denim, an oxford button down, and timeless accessories. Take those same jeans and style your denim with a pair of low-top sneakers like common projects white sneakers, or a heavyweight crewneck sweatshirt and you've got a great weekend look for fall.

100% cotton denim will feel sturdier and often lasts longer—but it can be stiff at first. A cotton-elastane blend adds comfort and mobility. Neither is “better” universally; it depends on how you wear your jeans. If you’re commuting, traveling, or sitting all day, stretch will keep you comfortable. If you want that old-school fade-and-mold-to-your-body experience, go full cotton.

Look at the stitching. Tight, even seams with no loose threads means you have a pair of durable jeans. Check the rivets (those little metal reinforcements at pocket corners), the zipper glide, and the heft of the waist button. Quality jeans often have reinforced belt loops and sturdy pocket bags that don’t feel like tissue paper.

Think about silhouette. The rise—whether high, mid, or low—changes where the jeans sit on your torso and can subtly shift proportions. From there, it’s about the leg shape. Straight-leg jeans are timeless and work on nearly everyone. Tapered cuts offer more room in the thigh with a slimmer opening at the ankle, ideal if you lift or prefer a cleaner line. Slim fits narrow throughout for a sharper look, while flares and bootcuts widen at the hem and are designed to sit neatly over boots (and yes, they’re creeping back into the mainstream). The right combination of rise and fit can make an affordable pair look elevated—or make an expensive pair look completely wrong.

#4969. BlackRock opens tokenized money market fund with $100 million in assets to Hong Kong investors

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Standard Chartered and Ant International seeded the fund, which BlackRock said is the first Hong Kong-domiciled money market fund offering a constant net asset value.

BlackRock has opened its first tokenized money market fund in Hong Kong to retail and institutional investors, the asset manager said in a statement on Tuesday.

Dubbed BlackRock HKD Digital Liquidity Fund, the product offers conventional and tokenized units, allowing investors to access the same money market strategy through traditional or blockchain-based ownership records.

The fund had $100 million in assets under management as of Nov. 20, according to the statement. It invests in short-term instruments, including government debt, overnight and term deposits, and other eligible securities.

"As traditional and digital markets become more connected, investors want cash solutions that are simple, trusted and available when they need them," said Dan Maley, head of APAC digital assets at BlackRock.

BlackRock said its fund offers accumulating share classes allowing the NAV to change, as well as distributing share classes that seek to maintain a constant unit price and pay out income.

It is the first Hong Kong-domiciled money market fund to offer a constant net asset value to both institutional and retail investors in the local market, according to the statement. For distributing share classes, the CNAV structure targets a stable unit price, with investment income distributed to investors rather than added to the value of their units.

#4968. Stacks Opens Bond 4 With 1,050 BTC of Bitcoin Staking Capacity

Registration is open for the bond starting ~Dec. 5, after Bond 3 sold out at 1,005 BTC with 21shares and Bullish.

GRAND CAYMAN, Cayman Islands, Nov. 24, 2026. Stacks Labs today announced 1,050 BTC of capacity for Bond 4 of Bitcoin staking with Stacks, a Bitcoin layer where BTC holders earn BTC. The bond starts ~Dec. 5, and institutions can register at stacks.co/institutional-bitcoin-staking.

Bond 4 carries a 3% target annual yield, paid in BTC weekly over a six-month term. Each BTC staked is paired with STX worth 5% of its value, so filling Bond 4’s 1,050 BTC of capacity requires a corresponding STX commitment. Rewards come from Stacks miners, who commit BTC through Proof of Transfer (PoX), the consensus mechanism Stacks has run since January 2021.

Every bond since the Genesis Bond launched in September has filled. 21shares returned for Bond 3, and Bullish (NYSE: BLSH) joined as the first top-10 public Bitcoin treasury company to stake BTC.

“Opening more than 1,000 BTC of new capacity for institutions shows how strong the demand for Bitcoin yield has become,” said Muneeb Ali, CEO, Stacks Labs, and founder of Stacks.

Institutions can stake from qualified custody, including BitGo. During the bootstrap phase, self-custodial Bitcoin staking is whitelisted. Holders who are not on the whitelist can take part through pooled Bitcoin staking at stacks.co/bitcoin-staking.

Stacks is a Bitcoin layer where BTC holders earn BTC. With Bitcoin staking, holders stake BTC with STX, and their BTC stays on the Bitcoin L1 under their own custody. Stacks has distributed over 4,200 BTC in rewards to participants since 2021. Learn more at stacks.co.

Media contact: Shannon Voight, PR & Events, Stacks Labs, [email protected]

Institutional onboarding: https://www.stacks.co/institutional-bitcoin-staking

#4967. Neuberger Berman’s HINC fund brings high-yield credit to Aave’s institutional lending platform

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Neuberger Berman, with $230 billion in assets under management, partnered with Securitize to bring HINC onchain in August.

Neuberger Berman's tokenized High Income Fund (HINC) has launched on Aave Horizon, allowing eligible investors to use their holdings as collateral to borrow USDC, GHO and RLUSD.

The fund, which invests primarily in high-yield corporate bonds and collateralized loan obligation (CLO) tranches, brings a new category of institutional credit to Horizon, whose collateral offerings have largely centered on tokenized Treasuries and higher-rated assets.

"Tokenization has always been about more than putting traditional assets on a blockchain. The real opportunity is making those assets useful across the onchain financial ecosystem," Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo said in a release.

Neuberger Berman, a global investment manager with $230 billion in assets under management, partnered with Securitize to bring HINC onchain in August.

Aave Labs launched Horizon on Ethereum in August 2025 to enable institutional investors to borrow stablecoins against tokenized real-world assets. The platform has since surpassed $500 million in total deposits and supports other institutional funds, including VanEck's tokenized VBILL Treasury fund, which entered DeFi lending markets earlier this year.

"Tokenized assets become much more powerful when they can participate directly in onchain markets," said Stani Kulechov, founder and CEO of Aave Labs. "As more asset classes move onchain, integrations like this can help create deeper and more useful financial markets."

Securitize's tokenized AUM topped $5 billion earlier this year, with about $3.1 billion coming from tokenized Treasuries.

#4966. Google wants to make you the star of your own AI slop movies

Viewers could find their AI-doppelgangers inserted into the crowd during the Cricket World Cup, or take a leading role in their very own romantic comedy.

Google has filed for a patent on a system that uses information it has on you to create a customized AI-generated TV channel, full of shows that star you as the protagonist.

The patent, submitted May 13, 2025, and published by the US Patent and Trademark Office on Thursday, describes a system called “My Channel,” filled with AI-generated content tailored to a user’s watch history and demographic data. The system could use photos or videos of the viewer to create a digital likeness and generate a synthetic voice for their character’s dialogue.

“You are a customized video generating model. Generate a customized video for a user where the user will play the protagonist,” the example AI prompt reads.

“The generated customized video includes characteristics based on the . The generated customized video follows the style and format of the of the user. The generated customized video sticks to the of the user, but the narrative for the generated customized video is customized.”

Many companies file patents that never become products. However, Google’s proposal isn’t too far off from reality. In China, AI-powered filmmakers are generating hundreds of one-minute TV microdramas, called “duanju,” each day.

Related: Nvidia reinstates Jensen Huang as CEO amid ongoing intelligence probe

Shortical, an Israeli-based streaming service available in the US, produces fully AI-generated microdramas among conventional titles. It even allows users to audition their face to be potentially added as characters in future AI-generated microseries.

Google envisions the kinds of genres a user could find themselves starring in. Source: USPTO

In another example, Google describes a user who has recently watched a video about a cricket player in the Cricket World Cup.

“The video should incorporate elements of the Cricket World Cup and Player 1’s recent performance. Perhaps [user_name] could be cheering for India in the stands or interacting with Player 1 in some way,” it wrote.

Google’s proposal also makes room for commercials. In one version described in the filing, when a viewer picks an AI-generated show, an ad server retrieves the video, inserts ads and streams the resulting version to the viewer’s TV.

The application names Aravind Reddy Ayuluri, a software engineer and Prameela Kavya Thummuru, a machine learning engineer at Google as the inventors.

Cointelegraph reached out to Google for comment.

#4964. Morning Minute: Crypto Rebounds after NVDA Drives Friday Selloff

Morning Minute: Crypto Rebounds after NVDA Drives Friday Selloff

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

Nvidia closed Friday at $125.53, down 47.5% from its October high, which works out to roughly $2.7 trillion of market value gone in seven weeks. The company beat earnings last week and guided revenue higher, and the stock still fell 23% across the five sessions.

The Nasdaq dropped 10% on the week, its worst since 2020, erasing its entire 2026 gain and shedding about $8.7 trillion since October 6. The S&P and Dow both closed in correction, AMD lost 20.5%, and CoreWeave now trades below its IPO price. Bank of America’s fund manager survey showed investors cutting tech exposure by the most since 2006, with 52% expecting at least one major hyperscaler to announce a capex cut inside six months.

Polymarket traders put 54% odds that the AI bubble will burst in 2026.

Crypto rode the same elevator down. Bitcoin hit roughly $55,100 Friday night, its lowest level since 2024 and marking a new cycle low, 56% off last October’s peak. Fear and Greed printed 9 and ETFs bled $1.79 billion on the week. It’s now tracking toward two consecutive down years, which has never happened before.

Then came the weekend bounce. Bitcoin reclaimed $60,000 and sits over $61,000 this morning; ETH is back above $1,710, and even Anthropic, down 9% Friday, quotes near $90 on Hyperliquid’s perp, still above its $83 offer.

Two explanations landed over the weekend.

U.S. President Donald Trump posted Sunday that the market is down $6 trillion since the Democrats’ “rather strange” win on November 3, that the Nasdaq is down 20%, and that it’s “100% due to” Democratic policy. Looks like no Trump bailout is coming anytime soon.

Elon Musk’s version is more useful. He warned Saturday that the cash shortage next year is “going to be nuts,” because even a few months of lag between paying for compute and earning revenue off it means hundreds of billions in incremental working capital. Then the question: where will that come from?

That’s a funding problem rather than a valuation one, and it lands harder coming from him. SpaceX reportedly sold $32 billion of bonds last week to buy Nvidia chips, so he’s describing a squeeze he’s already borrowing into.

So where does this leave us? The Fed meets December 8th and 9th with the market pricing a hike into all of this. Gas prices just hit an eight-month low ahead of Thanksgiving, down about 15% in a month, which should take some pressure off the headline print. The catch is that pump prices are still running 18% above last year, and core CPI, the number Waller actually cited when he floated fifty basis points, strips energy out entirely. Any relief has to come from shelter and services cooling instead.

Perhaps the Fed provides some. Whether it’s enough to stop the bleeding, only time will tell.

#4963. Ledn brings US business onshore with account to hold, swap and borrow against bitcoin

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Assets are held by an independent qualified custodian and segregated from Ledn's own holdings.

Crypto lender Ledn has launched a U.S.-based custody account that allows eligible customers to hold bitcoin, gold, stablecoins and U.S. dollars, trade between them with no trading fees, and borrow against bitcoin without selling it.

The move brings Ledn's U.S. operations onshore and expands its lending availability into additional states, reaching markets with a combined population of roughly 55 million people.

Assets are held by an independent qualified custodian and segregated from Ledn's own holdings, while bitcoin purchases carry a 0.5% spread that the company says is about half the cost of purchases on major U.S. apps.

"What our clients want is to stay in bitcoin," Ledn Co-Founder and Chief Strategy Officer Mauricio Di Bartolomeo said in a release. "Most people buy it in one place, keep it in another, and when they need cash, they sell it. Now they can do all three in one account."

In a June interview with The Block, Di Bartolomeo said the bitcoin-backed lending market could reach $1 trillion within the next five to 10 years. Of note, LendingUSA partnered with Cross River Bank this week to launch a bitcoin-secured lending program.

Since 2018, Ledn has serviced more than $11 billion in loans. The firm jumped into a fully custodied bitcoin-only model in early 2025. Tether made a strategic investment in the firm last November.

"Now Americans can buy, sell, swap and borrow in one account that’s theirs alone," said Co-Founder and CEO Adam Reeds. "If you're going to be a financial services company, boring is a good thing. We want to be the reliable, stable place where people build their wealth, not a platform with a million features built for trading."

#4962. Coldcard Postmortem Says Weak Seeds, Not Hacked Devices, Drove Bitcoin Thefts

Coinkite on Tuesday published its full postmortem on the Coldcard seed-generation flaw that let thieves drain bitcoin from customer wallets starting in July, saying no device was hacked and that the stolen keys were rebuilt offline from weak seeds.

The 18-page report, dated Nov. 23 and modeled on a National Transportation Safety Board accident report, lays out how the defect shipped in Coldcard firmware from March 29, 2021, through July 30, 2026, and why years of outside reviews missed it.

“The losses are real and, for some customers, life-changing. No report or firmware update can undo that. We are sorry,” Coinkite wrote.

A seed is the list of recovery words that controls a bitcoin wallet, and it is only as safe as the randomness used to create it. Coldcard devices were supposed to draw that randomness from the hardware random number generator built into their STM32 chip.

Instead, a firmware setting switched that hardware source off, and a safeguard in Coinkite’s libNgU code library failed to catch it. Seed generation quietly fell back to Yasmarang, a predictable software generator built into MicroPython, the programming environment the firmware runs on.

Nothing crashed, and the devices produced normal-looking seed words. Coinkite estimates affected seeds carried roughly 40 bits of effective security on the older Mk2 and Mk3 models and about 72 bits on the Mk4, Mk5 and Q, against an intended 128 bits. That shrinks the pool of possible seeds enough for attackers to search it offline.

The report says Coinkite’s release checks confirmed the hardware code was present, but never confirmed that seed generation actually reached it. At least 15 independent review efforts since 2021 also missed the flaw, including two paid third-party reviews and an AI-assisted firmware review this year that produced 85 candidate findings.

Unexpected transactions from affected seeds began July 29. Coinkite said it identified the defect the next day, after users discovered thefts, and shipped fixed firmware for every supported release track on July 31.

As of Nov. 18, Coinkite had received about 300 loss-related support tickets tied to 242 customer accounts, or about 0.21% of its estimated Coldcard user base. The company’s blog post cites 236 accounts. Because Coldcard is self-custodial, Coinkite can only count losses that customers report, and it says the true number is likely higher.

The report cites Galaxy Research figures showing 1,778.84 BTC stolen from more than 8,600 addresses, worth about $107 million at today’s price. Galaxy pegged losses above $115 million in August, based on prices when the coins were taken, and white hats later moved some exposed coins into a recovery trust.

Who found and exploited the flaw is still unknown, and law enforcement is investigating. The report calls AI-assisted discovery “a plausible hypothesis, not an attribution,” noting that the first thefts came two days after the full model weights of the AI model Kimi K3 were released. Bitcoin Core developers ended long embargoes last month for bugs that AI tools can easily rediscover.

Coinkite said an independent review by Keylabs, published Oct. 15, found the defect “fully closed” in Mk5 firmware 5.6.2 and 5.6.3 and Q firmware 1.5.2Q and 1.5.3Q. That review did not cover Coldcard’s Edge releases, a preview track for developers and early adopters.

Newer Coldcard firmware now requires users to add their own randomness, through key presses, dice rolls or coin flips, and mixes it with three hardware sources. The device also checks at startup that seed generation reaches the hardware generator, and stops if it does not.

But a firmware update does not repair a seed that was already created on affected firmware. Coinkite told owners whose seeds were made on affected firmware to install verified fixed firmware, create a new seed and move their coins. Seeds made with at least 50 private dice rolls were not weakened.

Three of the report’s 15 recommendations to Coinkite remain open, including whether to compensate affected customers. The company said it has “no decision” on a plan yet and asked whoever holds the stolen bitcoin to return it through [email protected].

#4960. Anchorage Digital Sets Up Asset Manager to Offer Bitcoin Yield to Clients

Anchorage Digital, the first federally chartered crypto bank in the U.S., is moving into asset management with a new subsidiary that will offer custody clients a way to earn yield on their bitcoin.

The unit, called 61st Parallel Asset Management, is expected to start operating by early December, CEO Nathan McCauley said in an interview to CoinDesk. Its first product will be a bitcoin-denominated fund, meaning returns are measured in BTC rather than dollars. The name nods to the city of Anchorage, Alaska, which sits at 61 degrees north latitude.

“We have increasingly heard clients say they want to earn on top of the assets they hold at Anchorage — something that’s very common among the large financial institutions of the world,” McCauley said.

Anchorage has filed a Form ADV, the disclosure document investment advisers use to register with the Securities and Exchange Commission or state regulators, or to report as exempt advisers. The form covers a firm’s ownership, business, assets under management, fees and conflicts of interest. As of Tuesday afternoon, the SEC’s public adviser database showed no record for 61st Parallel, so the filing’s details were not yet public.

The entity itself is not new. New York state records show that 61st Parallel Asset Management LLC, a Delaware company, registered to do business in New York on July 8, listing an office on Park Avenue South in Manhattan.

Anchorage already lets clients earn on some holdings. Its website lists staking, which pays rewards for helping secure proof-of-stake networks, and a separate rewards product for eligible assets. Bitcoin runs on proof-of-work and cannot be staked natively.

Anchorage has not said publicly how the fund will generate yield, what it will charge or which clients will qualify. Those details matter to bitcoin holders, because bitcoin pays no interest on its own. Common ways to earn on it include lending coins out, posting them as collateral or selling options against them, and each carries its own risk.

Public companies have already tested some of those approaches. Trump Media disclosed this month that it had pledged about 73% of its bitcoin to an options program in which the counterparty can rehypothecate, or reuse, the coins.

Founded in 2017, Anchorage received a national trust bank charter from the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency in January 2021, the first crypto firm to win one. The OCC lifted a 2022 consent order over the bank’s anti-money laundering controls in August 2025.

The bank no longer stands alone. Last December, the OCC granted conditional approvals to five more digital asset firms, including Circle, Ripple, BitGo, Paxos and Fidelity Digital Assets, to become national trust banks.

The asset manager is the latest in a run of expansions. On Nov. 5, Anchorage Digital Swap Dealer LLC applied to the National Futures Association to register as a swap dealer with the Commodity Futures Trading Commission. The company said much of its options growth has come from public companies holding bitcoin and ether on their balance sheets.

In February, Tether made a $100 million strategic investment in Anchorage at a $4.2 billion valuation, after picking Anchorage Digital Bank as the issuer of its U.S. stablecoin. Anchorage also ran its first employee tender offer at that valuation instead of raising new primary capital.

In June, the company rolled out off-exchange settlement with Binance, letting institutions trade on the exchange while their assets stay in segregated custody at the bank. Anchorage also offers trading, staking and settlement to institutional clients.

The swap dealer application remains under review by the National Futures Association, and McCauley said at the time that the company was not in a position to speculate on timing.

#4959. XRP Ledger DEX volume hits 4.51 million XRP a day in Q3 as big traders take over: Evernorth

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Trading activity became more concentrated, according to an Evernorth report, with the 10 largest accounts accounting for 65% of order-book volume.

The XRP Ledger's (XRPL) decentralized exchange order-book volume averaged 4.51 million XRP per day in the third quarter of 2026, up 26% from the prior period and 2.8 times the level a year earlier, according to a quarterly report from Evernorth shared with The Block.

Trading activity became more concentrated, with the 10 largest accounts accounting for 65% of order-book volume. Tokenized assets on XRPL, excluding stablecoins, averaged roughly $5 billion, up 34% from the second quarter, while U.S. spot XRP ETFs recorded $308 million in quarterly net inflows.

Evernorth's report shows that XRPL trading is shifting toward larger participants. Average swap size nearly doubled quarter over quarter to about 35 XRP, while daily swaps fell 37%. Automated pools accounted for 13% of exchange volume, down from 38% a year earlier, with more trading moving to the order book.

"That's consistent with professional trading, and market makers are part of that picture, not separate from it," Evernorth CIO Hugo Jeandesboz told The Block. "The real test is whether more participants join them, and that's what our report is there to measure."

So, what are the biggest catalysts that could sustain XRPL's institutional growth into the next year? Evernorth said it comes down to utility.

"People want to use XRP, and the way to give them a reason is real, production-grade products built natively on the ledger, so institutions don't have to leave it to get things done," Jeandesboz said. "The Q3 numbers point that way."

Ripple USD (RLUSD) on XRPL averaged $937 million, up 74%, and tokenized assets averaged about $5 billion, including Aviva Investors' tokenized fund.

"Native lending is the next example the market is watching," Jeandesboz said. "It's still pending validator approval, and it would sit on top of upgrades that already went live this year. We built Evernorth to put XRP to work, and that's the shift we track."

Evernorth completed a SPAC merger last month, holding roughly 473 million XRP at the time. The company planned to deploy its treasury holdings through "institutional strategies" in the fourth quarter.

#4958. Nvidia Reinstates Jensen Huang as CEO Days After Board Ruled Out His Return

Nvidia Reinstates Jensen Huang as CEO Days After Board Ruled Out His Return

Nvidia's board has reinstated Jensen Huang as president and CEO, six days after its chair said directors did not intend to bring him back.

The board voted unanimously to reinstate Huang as president, CEO and a board member, effective immediately, the company said on Monday. It followed "a series of discussions and meetings" with Huang, after which the board was satisfied with his "candor and ability to lead" the firm.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Nvidia with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

In reaching its decision, the board said it received input from "senior levels of the United States government," and based on that input, it is confident that Huang's reinstatement "poses no national security risk." The statement did not name the officials involved.

"The Board believes Jensen is the right person to lead NVIDIA, and that his reinstatement is in the best interests of our stockholders, employees, partners and customers," board chair Harvey Jones said. Jones will remain chair, and Colette Kress, who served as interim CEO, continues as chief financial officer.

NVIDIA Board reinstates Jensen Huang as President, CEO and Board Director.

Read the release: https://t.co/x4bnjiSVil

— NVIDIA Newsroom (@nvidianewsroom) November 23, 2026

"I take this responsibility with the greatest humility and renewed commitment," Huang said in the statement. "I can't wait to get back to work."

The decision overturns the board's position from last week. "The Nvidia board does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO," Jones said on the company's November 17 earnings call, as Nvidia hired executive search firm Heidrick & Struggles to find a permanent replacement.

Hours earlier, Huang had offered to return if the board decided it was in the best interest of shareholders, employees, the company "and America." His comeback had drawn support from President Donald Trump and Elon Musk, even as CNBC reported that directors were not considering it.

Huang resigned on November 12 after reports of extramarital relationships, including one with Alice Huang, a former executive at Singapore-based Nvidia customer Megaspeed whom U.S. officials suspected of being a Chinese intelligence asset. China has denied that she is a spy.

The independent directors' committee investigating whether Huang was the target of a foreign intelligence effort "will proceed to conclusion," Nvidia said, and Huang has said he will cooperate with it.

The company also pointed to its new National Security and Export Control Committee, made up of independent directors, and said it is searching for a chief compliance officer to oversee compliance across the company. Nvidia said it will keep cooperating with government investigations and inquiries.

Huang still faces scrutiny in Washington. House committees have asked him to sit for a closed-door interview on December 1, and Senate Banking Committee Chairman Tim Scott (R-SC) has asked him to testify at a public hearing on December 3.

Nvidia shares rose to over $160 in premarket trading on Monday, up about 28% from Friday's close of $125.53, according to Yahoo! Finance. That would add roughly $780 billion to the company's market value, but still leave the stock below its $166.19 close on the day Huang resigned.

#4956. Why 42 Macro says it’s waiting to buy the Bitcoin dip

The independent macro research company warns the Bitcoin sell-off may not have yet run its course.

Darius Dale, founder and CEO of 42 Macro, says his firm has trimmed its Bitcoin exposure and is waiting for signs that the sell-off has run its course before it considers buying again.

“We’ve already de-risked out of Bitcoin,” Dale said in an interview with Cointelegraph last week. “Our stress levels are low. We’re just waiting to buy.”

Dale’s comments came just days before Bitcoin fell to a two-year low around $55,100 on Saturday, raising questions about whether Bitcoin has finally found its bottom this cycle.

However, Dale warned that persistently high inflation could prompt further Fed tightening that could drain the liquidity that supports Bitcoin. The US Federal Reserve is widely expected to increase rates by 25 basis points at its next meeting on Dec. 9.

“Bitcoin doesn’t generate earnings. Bitcoin needs liquidity to go up,” he said.

Bitcoin was trading at around $64,000 at the time of the interview. Dale said it could fall another 30% to 40% if the S&P 500’s decline from its Oct. 6 peak reaches 20% by Christmas, which would put it at around $38,400. The S&P500 is currently down 7.4% from that peak.

Related: Bitcoin rebounds 17% from $55K lows as Nvidia rally sparks $500M crypto liquidations

Asked when 42 Macro may consider buying Bitcoin again, Dale said the firm will be looking at its own market signals.

“And once we pivot to a risk on market regime, once Bitcoin, the bottoming process concludes and our signals go from bearish right now to neutral or maybe even back to bullish. That’s when you want to buy.”

Despite near-term caution, Dale expects the conditions to become more favorable for Bitcoin around the middle of 2027, when easing inflation could give the Fed room to shift away from tighter monetary policy.

“So we think 2027 will be a phenomenal year for stocks, gold, Bitcoin,” he said.

Dale said the Fed may need to see second-quarter inflation data before changing course, putting the potential shift in late June or in the first half of the third quarter.

#4955. Here’s what happened in crypto today

Need to know what happened in crypto today? Here is the latest news on daily trends and events impacting Bitcoin price, blockchain, DeFi, Web3 and crypto regulation.

Today in crypto, South Korea unveiled a draft bill that would initially limit won stablecoin issuance to bank-controlled groups, 42 Macro CEO Darius Dale said in an interview last week his firm is waiting for signs that Bitcoin’s sell-off has run its course before buying again and Nvidia reinstated Jensen Huang as CEO despite an ongoing intelligence probe.

South Korea’s Financial Services Commission (FSC) unveiled a draft bill Tuesday that would initially limit issuance of won-denominated stablecoins to groups majority-owned by banks and generally cap shareholders’ stakes in crypto exchanges at 20%.

Banks would have to own more than half of a stablecoin issuer collectively, though a fintech or technology company could be its largest individual shareholder, the FSC said in its announcement.

Domestic stablecoin issuers would need FSC authorization and at least 5 billion won (about $3.7 million) in equity capital. They would also have to hold reserves covering at least 100% of their outstanding tokens and redeem them at face value without delay.

Exchanges would need dealing, brokerage and custody licenses and would have to meet capital and operating requirements. Shareholders’ stakes would be capped at 20%, or 34% with FSC approval.

The draft also seeks to lift South Korea’s 2017 ban on domestic initial coin offerings.

Darius Dale, founder and CEO of 42 Macro, says his firm has trimmed its Bitcoin exposure and is waiting for signs that the sell-off has run its course before it considers buying again.

“We’ve already de-risked out of Bitcoin,” Dale said in an interview with Cointelegraph last week. “Our stress levels are low. We’re just waiting to buy.”

Dale’s comments came just days before Bitcoin fell to a two-year low around $55,100 on Saturday, raising questions about whether Bitcoin has finally found its bottom this cycle.

However, Dale warned that persistently high inflation could prompt further Fed tightening that could drain the liquidity that supports Bitcoin. The US Federal Reserve is widely expected to increase rates by 25 basis points at its next meeting on Dec. 9.

“Bitcoin doesn’t generate earnings. Bitcoin needs liquidity to go up,” he said.

Bitcoin was trading at around $64,000 at the time of the interview. Dale said it could fall another 30% to 40% if the S&P 500’s decline from its Oct. 6 peak reaches 20% by Christmas, which would put it at around $38,400. The S&P500 is currently down 7.4% from that peak.

Nvidia’s board unanimously voted to reinstate Jensen Huang as president and CEO after consultations with senior US government officials, concluding that his return posed no national security risk despite an ongoing investigation into an alleged foreign intelligence operation targeting him.

In a Monday announcement, Nvidia said its directors were satisfied with Huang’s candor and ability to lead following a series of discussions. The independent investigation will continue despite his reinstatement.

Nvidia shares surged 28.91% to $161.82 shortly after US markets opened on Monday, according to Yahoo Finance data.

Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress, who served as interim CEO, will return to her previous role, while Harvey Jones will remain chair of the board.

The chipmaker also said it had recently formed a National Security and Export Control Committee comprising independent directors to oversee compliance and security risks. Nvidia plans to appoint a chief compliance officer and said it would continue cooperating with government investigations.

#4953. Bitcoin ETFs see another $570 million in net outflows as November total nears $5 billion and BTC dips below $60K

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs saw more than half a billion dollars in net outflows on Monday as bitcoin surrendered an Nvidia-fueled rally and slipped back below $60,000.

U.S. spot bitcoin exchange-traded funds saw another $569.7 million in net outflows on Monday as bitcoin surrendered an initial rally following the reinstatement of Jensen Huang as CEO of Nvidia.

BlackRock's IBIT bitcoin ETF again led Monday's net outflows with $509.3 million exiting the fund, according to data compiled by The Block. IBIT's dominant bitcoin fund has recorded just one day of net inflows so far this month, with its cumulative November net outflows approaching $4 billion.

Grayscale's BTC and Bitwise's BITB also witnessed net outflows of $61.1 million and $12.6 million, respectively, while Fidelity's FBTC saw the only net inflows of $13.3 million. The remaining ETFs posted zero flows for the day, with the combined bitcoin ETFs nearing $5 billion in net outflows for the month.

Meanwhile, Ethereum ETFs also generated net outflows of $84.9 million on Monday, led by BlackRock's ETHA. U.S. Solana ETF saw a combined $3.8 million in net outflows for the day, while spot XRP ETFs recorded $2.5 million in net outflows, according to SoSoValue data.

Nicolai Sondergaard, senior research analyst at Nansen, argued that the selling represented a cyclical reduction in risk rather than a structural exit from bitcoin ETFs. Despite nearly $5 billion exiting the funds so far in November, cumulative net inflows remain around $52 billion, or about 83% of their October 2025 peak, he said.

However, Nansen data also shows little evidence that large holders are absorbing the ETF selling so far. Large wallets that rarely move coins received a net 10,000 BTC from exchanges over the past month, compared with approximately 70,000 BTC sold by ETFs during November, according to Sondergaard.

Bitcoin rose as high as $64,500 at one point on Monday amid a broad market rally following Nvidia's confirmation that it had reinstated Jensen Huang as president, CEO, and board director. Nvidia (NVDA) shares themselves closed up 31.5% at $165.06 on Monday, adding nearly $1 trillion in market cap, and are currently down 4.8% in pre-market trading on Tuesday, per TradingView.

However, Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) gave up its initial gains to finish Monday roughly flat before slipping back below $60,000 earlier on Tuesday morning. Bitcoin is currently trading for around $60,300, according to The Block's prices page.

Ether (ETH) witnessed a similar spike, but ended up trading down 0.8% for the day, while the GMCI 30 index, representing a selection of the top 30 cryptocurrencies, fell around 1.1%.

Joel Kruger, market strategist at LMAX Group, told The Block that elevated Treasury yields and continued expectations of an aggressive Federal Reserve policy path were keeping the dollar supported and making it harder for crypto to sustain a rebound.

#4951. Bitcoin Rallies, Anthropic Sinks as Nvidia Surges 35% on Jensen Huang’s Return

Bitcoin Rallies, Anthropic Sinks as Nvidia Surges 35% on Jensen Huang’s Return

Wall Street welcomed Jensen Huang back to Nvidia with lots of green confetti today by sending the chipmaker’s shares up nearly 35%, helping drag battered technology stocks and crypto prices out of last week’s rout.

Nvidia traded around $169.83 at 10:28 a.m. ET, up 34.97% from Friday’s close. It is the biggest bullish jump in the history of the company.

And the FOMO contagion spread quickly to other tech stocks. Rival AMD jumped 8.91% to $462.09, while the iShares Semiconductor ETF (SOXX) surged 15% to $141.77.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Nvidia with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

Bitcoin joined the party, trading near $63,500 after dipping below $60,000 during Friday’s selloff. Ethereum climbed to about $1,790, and cash CFDs tracking the Nasdaq 100 and S&P 500 rebounded to roughly 26,950 and 7,285, respectively.

The catalyst was Nvidia’s announcement that its founder Jensen Huang had been reinstated as president, CEO and director, effective immediately. The board voted unanimously just six days after publicly ruling out his return—and even doubling down on their decision several times.

Huang resigned November 12 amid reports of extramarital relationships, including one with a businesswoman U.S. officials suspected of ties to Chinese intelligence. Beijing has denied the espionage allegation, and no public evidence has established that Huang knowingly passed company secrets to China.

Colette Kress, Nvidia’s finance chief, took over as interim CEO while the company launched a search for a permanent replacement. On November 17, board chair Harvey Jones said directors did not intend to reinstate Huang, despite a campaign for his return backed by Silicon Valley executives and President Donald Trump.

Monday’s reversal followed meetings with Huang and input from senior U.S. government officials. The board said it was satisfied with his candor and leadership ability and was confident his return posed “no national security risk,” though its independent investigation will continue.

“I can’t wait to get back to work,” Huang said in the statement. Kress will remain chief financial officer, and Jones will stay on as board chair.

“It tells us to some extent that Jensen Huang was too big to be fired,” Interactive Brokers strategist Steve Sosnick told Reuters.

Prediction markets were caught off guard, too. A Polymarket contract on Huang rejoining Nvidia by November 30 traded near 99% after the announcement, compared with roughly 3% shortly beforehand.

His return also tanked the probabilities of the AI bubble bursting. After his outsting, Polymarket odds skyrocketed to more than 70%, minutes after his reinstatement, the odds dropped to 14%.

The rally followed an ugly stretch for AI stocks. Nvidia closed Friday at $125.53, about 47.5% below its October high, while the Nasdaq lost roughly 10% over the previous week. Bitcoin fell toward $55,100 Friday night before staging a weekend recovery.

Monday’s bounce pushed Nvidia above the $166.19 closing price recorded on the day Huang resigned. Bitcoin, meanwhile, recovered the $63,000 area, but its daily chart still shows a bearish moving-average configuration and a relative strength index near 34, indicating that the recent relief rally has not erased the broader downtrend.

Overall, crypto traders are still timid but evidently don’t think this is the end of the world.

On the chart, Bitcoin faces initial resistance around $65,030, followed by $68,090. On the downside, $61,240 and the psychological $60,000 mark are immediate levels to watch; another break lower would put the recent $55,100 area back in focus.

One of the few notable losers was Anthropic, whose stock slipped 3.16% to $85.81. That left the Claude developer about 25% below its post-IPO peak near $115, although still slightly above the $83 offering price set for its $81.34 billion public offering last Thursday. Nvidia itself committed about $10 billion to a separate, conditional private investment in Anthropic alongside the IPO.

The decline looks more like investors continuing to digest a pricey, volatile listing than a reaction to Huang’s reinstatement. But the internet has another theory: memes and posts have cast Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei as playing a role in Huang’s ouster because of his support towards open-weight models and his decision to buy Hugging Face, the world’s largest repository of open source AI models, to protect the ecosystem.

Huang championed broad access to open models, while Amodei has argued for tighter safeguards around powerful AI systems, especially warning against open source AI competing against close models like the ones his company develops, a position that has been widely criticized by influential figures in the AI scene like Yan Lecun who called his mindset “a new form of medieval obscurantism.”

Dario's spiel since GPT-2 has always been about hyping the dangers so that open models would be made illegal and *he* would stay in control.He left OpenAI because he wasn't going to have control.It's like the Ottoman Empire banning the printing press for Arabic so they would…

Nvidia’s independent inquiry into the reported foreign intelligence effort remains open. Huang has also been asked to meet House investigators on December 1 and testify before a Senate panel on December 3.
