#4882. The 5 Best Laser Hair Growth Devices for Men in 2026, According to Doctors

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

You don’t need to fly overseas to regrow hair.

THERE COMES A time in every man’s life—sans the few genetically gifted—when he must face hair loss. It hits sooner than many expect, as the American Hair Loss Association notes that 85 percent of men will face hair loss by age 50. There are then two options: lean into it and join the Stanley Tucci Gang, or invest in equipment to regrow hair. For the latter, the best tools to reverse balding at home are laser hair growth devices.

The majority of men lose their hair due to male pattern baldness (also known as androgenetic alopecia), a condition caused by genetic predisposition and hormonal influences, wherein testosterone is declining and dihydrotestosterone (aka DHT) is surging. This process is characterized by the classic receding hairline look, along with thinning around the crown.

Laser hair growth devices are best at treating this cause of hair loss, explains dermatologist and founder of Remedy, Dr. Muneeb Shah, MD. Other common factors like stress, illness, medications, nutritional deficiencies, and autoimmune diseases will be less responsive to laser therapies, as hair follicles are often damaged or inactive from these issues and less willing to be rebooted from light treatment.

Laser hair growth devices work by using low-level laser therapy, or LLLT, to stimulate weakened or dormant hair follicles, says hair restoration physician and creator of the Bauman Turbo LaserCap, Dr. Alan Bauman, MD. The process is known as photobiomodulation, and it involves exposing tissues to light in the red and near-infrared spectrum, which can improve cell metabolism and blood flow, reduce inflammation, and stimulate hair follicles to re-enter and maintain the growth phase.

Generally, LLLT does not include LED; instead, it uses laser diodes. However, some devices will combine both for added effectiveness or to reduce the intensity of laser diodes. “In simpler terms, laser therapy supercharges the environment for healthy hair growth by restoring energy to sluggish follicles and optimizing scalp conditions,” Dr. Bauman says.

Laser hair growth devices are rarely cheap (you won’t find an effective one below $400), so you want to make sure you’re getting the right option for you before making the investment. After chatting with the pros, these are the five laser hair growth devices worth considering.

The biggest dilemma when buying a laser hair growth device is choosing between LED and laser diodes, but with the iRestore Laser Therapy Hair Growth System, you don’t have to. This device uses a hybrid of laser diodes and LEDs to get the best of both worlds, pairing the gentle nature of LEDs with the power of laser diodes. Dr. Shah backs it, noting that iRestore is reputable and the device is FDA-cleared.

The brand even offers a six-month money-back guarantee if no results are seen. However, I’d imagine the brand doesn’t receive many sendbacks, as this device gives full coverage with serious results. That being said, Dr. Bauman warns that the combo of LED and laser diodes may dilute benefits for some due to mixed light sources. But hard to argue with a six-month guarantee.

Read More: iRestore Laser Face Mask Pro Review

Dr. Shah also recommends this offering from Current Body. This futuristic helmet offers a spectrum of red light LED from 620 to 660 nanometers. The flexible intensity allows for a customized experience, which is ideal for those new to these tools or with sensitive skin to ease into LED treatment and see what your ideal nanometer level is.

While results will be stronger closer to 650 or 660 nanometers, this range is powerful enough to reduce inflammation, decrease DHT levels (to maintain follicle strength), and improve blood flow on the scalp (to provide hair follicles with more oxygen and nutrients needed for growth).

Sure, it may look like a VR set from a scrapped Black Mirror episode, but it’s quite comfortable, and deceptively adjustable for a quality fit. Use for 10 minutes daily, and results can be seen quickly in as little as 16 weeks. The only reasons this helmet missed out on the top spot is that it relies solely on LED, and is roughly $300 more expensive than iRestore’s helmet.

Not everybody has the budget (or desire) to drop several thousand on a hair growth device. Maybe you’re on the fence about these tools, or the prospect of regrowing your hair in general. For these folks, Hairmax will be your best option, as it’s the least expensive tool on the market that’s still effective.

Featuring 12 medical-grade laser diodes in a comb-style tool, you gently brush this device through your hair and across your dome to stimulate hair growth. The sessions are listed at eight minutes; however, it often takes longer for the best results, given the manual nature of the device and the smaller surface it covers at once. Still, you won’t find a cheaper FDA-approved model.

Read More: Is Your Hair Loss From Thinning or Breaking

At first glance, the Hairmax LaserBand 82 looks like a nuisance to use. Rather than a cap design like HigherDose and Capillus, helmet like iRestore and Current Body, or Hairmax’s comb, the LaserBand resembles a mini MRI machine. But unlike those devices, this tool only needs to be used for a couple of minutes to see results, three times a week.

The brand boasts that sessions need only be 90 seconds, but Dr. Bauman warns it’ll take a bit longer for full coverage—like two or three minutes. Still, that’s by far the quickest treatment on this list. Plus, Dr. Bauman says it features a quality diode count (246 lasers), even if you may need to reposition after 90 seconds for full coverage.

Read More: Best Hair Thickening Shampoos for Men

Men’s Health writer Bob Gearing wore the HigherDose Red Light Therapy Hat every day for the past year, and swears by the results. He notes that this nifty cap is both more effective than Rogaine and less invasive, leaving no irritation or redness. It’s super easy to use, pop it on like any hat, and wear for 10 minutes a day while the 650 nanometer LED red light penetrates the scalp to promote a healthier environment for hair growth.

The brand lists 16 months as the target, but the best results will appear after six months. Unlike the Capillus cap, whose red light peaks through the sides during use, this is the only hair growth device you could get away with wearing in public without anyone noticing. It simply looks like a black hat, nothing more. This makes it both easy to use and even easier to incorporate into your daily routine.

As Dr. Bauman puts it, not all laser hair growth devices are created equal. The first thing to consider is what light is being used in your device to stimulate growth. The two primary options are LEDs (Light Emitting Diodes) and laser diodes. He recommends zeroing in on laser diodes as they’re more powerful and penetrate deeper into the scalp, targeting the hair follicle root more effectively.

From a strict regrowth perspective, laser diodes will be more effective; however, these also present more potential side effects given their strength, such as hyperpigmentation, burns, redness, and swelling. LEDs present fewer side effects, so they’re better for sensitive scalps, but they won’t penetrate the scalp and hair follicles as deeply. This means LED is best suited for early shedding and will struggle to help more severe hair loss.

For both LED and laser diode options, the therapeutic wavelength is crucial. Dr. Shah recommends wavelengths between 630 and 680 nanometers to successfully penetrate the scalp and stimulate hair follicle growth. If you have sensitive skin or are new to these tools, take it slow and work your way to the top level to limit irritation.

Dr. Bauman says that the 650-nanometer range is key, as it is the optimal wavelength absorbed by mitochondria for energy production to support hair follicle function. This process boosts cellular energy, as hair follicles are highly active structures that need a lot of energy to support the hair growth cycle. The end result is reduced inflammation, improved blood flow, and extended growth.

To be clear, no laser hair growth device is going to give you Thor-level flow overnight—these tools take time. Dr. Shah says that men will need at least six months of consistent use before seeing results after regular use. But not every device’s session will be the same. Some, you’ll need to wear or place near the dome for five minutes, while others for half an hour. Consider the comfort of your device, along with your schedule, before investing.

Most importantly, however, Dr. Shah says consistency is crucial because missing sessions, no matter how long they are, can reduce the device’s effectiveness. If executed properly, early results will appear around six months, such as reduced shedding or slight thickening, before fuller regrowth occurs near the one-year mark, according to Dr. Shah.

Whenever you’re dealing with new technology, rely on reputable organizations to first approve it for use. Both Dr. Shah and Dr. Bauman advise only looking to laser hair growth devices that have been accepted by the FDA for the safest, most effective experience. However, there are different levels of FDA acceptance. FDA-listed or FDA-registered simply means that the manufacturer has informed the FDA, but doesn’t imply approval or authorization.

FDA clearance, on the other hand, means the device is substantially equivalent to a product already on the market.

FDA approved is the ideal, as this means the device has successfully been through extensive testing and clinical trials to demonstrate safety and effectiveness. But to date, there have been no LED or laser diode devices for hair growth that are FDA-approved, so look for FDA-cleared options.

As someone whose hairline is still going strong after 33 years (and a ton of preventive hair thinning products), I leaned heavily on the counsel of hair loss professionals for these selections. I conferred with dermatologist Dr. Shah and hair restoration physician Dr. Bauman on what to look for in an effective laser hair growth device and which they recommend.

I also consulted with fellow Men’s Health writers and editors who have battled hair loss on their experience, based on ease of use, effectiveness, and long-term results. Following this research and testing, we nailed down these tools as the five best laser hair growth devices for men in 2026.

#4881. Trump Says He 'Made the Deal' to Reinstate Jensen Huang, Sparking Bipartisan Backlash

Trump Says He 'Made the Deal' to Reinstate Jensen Huang, Sparking Bipartisan Backlash

U.S. President Donald Trump has taken credit for Jensen Huang's return to Nvidia, drawing criticism from lawmakers in both parties who say the administration has yet to explain what assurances it gave the company's board.

"I convinced them," Trump told CNBC in a phone interview on Monday, shortly after Nvidia reinstated Huang as president and CEO. "I made the deal."

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Nvidia with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

Trump said the board had asked for assurances that Huang was not a national security threat, and that the administration provided them based on "everything we know." He said a government investigation had found that Huang himself was exonerated. Asked whether last week's stock losses had shaped his view, Trump said "probably so."

The board's decision was sealed by a personal promise from Trump on Sunday, CNBC reported, citing three people familiar with its thinking. Senior administration officials had contacted directors overnight on Friday, and Huang then joined meetings with the board over the weekend, the outlet said. The board's statement cited input from "senior levels of the United States government" without naming anyone.

Attorney General Todd Blanche told Trump and Vice President JD Vance on Saturday that Huang was not the target or subject of any Justice Department investigation, and that there was no evidence he had broken laws, The Wall Street Journal reported, citing U.S. officials.

During a marathon board session on Sunday, Huang disclosed additional affairs beyond the two that had been widely reported, but was contrite, the newspaper said, citing people familiar with the discussion.

Trump told The New York Times that he had consulted Blanche and Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton before recommending Huang's return. "I had to go to them before I recommended this," he said. A day earlier, Clayton had said that the U.S. investigation into Alice Huang and her former company, Megaspeed, was ongoing.

The reversal came six days after board chair Harvey Jones said directors did not intend to bring Huang back. Trump later posted an AI-generated image on Truth Social showing Huang holding a red bra aloft in his raised fist, in a pose modeled on the photo of Trump after the 2024 assassination attempt on his life.

Huang's reinstatement drew the ire of lawmakers on both sides of the aisle.

"The reckless decision to bring Jensen Huang back as CEO does not change the fact that Mr. Huang, NVIDIA executives, and the company's board still owe Congress answers," Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-MA), ranking member of the Senate Banking Committee, said in a statement. She called on the administration to explain what input was given, and by which officials.

Republicans also pushed back. Sen. Tom Cotton (R-AR), chair of the Senate Intelligence Committee, tweeted that the controversy over Nvidia and Megaspeed was "not resolved because Jensen Huang has his job back," and pledged to pass chip export-control bills including his Chip Security Act.

I have warned the administration from the start about Communist China obtaining our most advanced chips through both smuggling and remote access. The controversy with Nvidia and Megaspeed is just the tip of the iceberg, and it’s not resolved because Jensen Huang has his job back.…

— Tom Cotton (@SenTomCotton) November 23, 2026

"No national security risk? Based on what?" Sen. John Cornyn (R-TX) tweeted, quoting the board's statement. Sen. John Curtis (R-UT) asked who in government had weighed in and what they told the board, noting that it had ruled out Huang's return only a week earlier. "I'd like to know what changed," he wrote.

Sen. Pete Ricketts (R-NE) tweeted that Huang's behavior "does not support reinstatement," adding that Nvidia still had to answer for national security concerns and "unaccounted-for chips."

Rep. Hakeem Jeffries (D-NY), who is expected to become House Speaker in January, called the board's decision "indefensible, irresponsible and incomprehensible" in a statement. He promised an investigation into Nvidia and into what he called "corrupt abuse of power" by Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick and his department.

Trump's own Nvidia holdings may have gained as much as $15 million in value on Monday, according to the Financial Times, which calculated that he could have held up to 370,000 shares as of September 30, based on his latest ethics disclosure. At the low end of the disclosure's ranges, he would have held about 32,000 shares and gained $1.3 million. The White House did not respond to the newspaper's request for comment.

Sen. Ruben Gallego (D-AZ) tweeted that the president was "selling out our national security to keep the stock market high," calling for an investigation into stock trades made before the announcement.

Nvidia shares closed up 31.5% at $165.05 on Monday, adding about $950 billion to its market value, before slipping 5% to $156.82 in premarket trading on Tuesday, according to Yahoo! Finance.

Huang is due to sit for a closed-door interview with three House committees on December 1, and to testify before the Senate Banking Committee on December 3. On Monday, the Commerce Department also added Megaspeed to its Entity List trade blacklist.

#4876. $14.7B in ‘Lost’ Bitcoin Has Moved Since 2018: Report

$14.7B in ‘Lost’ Bitcoin Has Moved Since 2018: Report

Bitcoin that hasn't been moved in years is sometimes considered "lost" by industry onlookers, and one lawsuit is arguing that they've been "abandoned." But since 2018, roughly 10,000 supposedly lost Bitcoin wallets have sprung back to life.

Pseudonymous developer Quickben22 built a list in 2018 of 92,076 Bitcoin wallets that hadn't moved funds in more than six years, assuming the owners had lost their keys. Eight years later, the developer revisited the list to discover that 9,990 of those wallets have since moved a total of approximately 245,000 BTC, or just over $14.7 billion at Bitcoin's current price.

"Dormant is not the same as lost," Quickben22 told Decrypt. "I expected nearly all of the 92,076 addresses to still be silent after 8 years. About 89% are, but 9,990 have moved, and 14+ year old addresses are still waking up."

That distinction matters, as the findings come as a New York lawsuit brought by a pseudonymous plaintiff known as 'Noah Doe' tries to claim 39,069 dormant Bitcoin addresses as abandoned property, including wallets linked to Bitcoin creator Satoshi Nakamoto.

The plaintiffs argue the coins are "abandoned" under New York's lost property law. They say they found the wallets, reported them to the NYPD, and sent the owners notice by attaching on-chain messages to tiny Bitcoin transactions.

"It's like writing down the addresses of every locked house on a street, telling the police, and claiming whatever is inside because nobody answered the door," Quickben22 said.

Some wallets linked to the suit have since woken up, with several moving funds earlier this year—including one first funded in 2013 that shifted 411.45 BTC in October, then worth $33.4 million. One pseudonymous holder has also filed a motion to dismiss the case, claiming to still control a named wallet.

Quickben22's list was originally built for an app called Bitcoin Key Hunter, which guesses private keys at random and checks them against the dormant addresses in the hope of landing on one.

"It's like playing the lottery, but with much, much smaller odds," he said, before breaking down the math: roughly 1 in 10^43 across the whole list. "Those are about the odds of winning five Powerballs in a row," he added.

Quickben22 did not respond to Decrypt's query on whether he has successfully guessed the keys to any of the listed wallets.

It is impossible to know who exactly moved the funds from the supposedly lost wallets—it might not even be the original owner. An original owner tidying up old funds looks the same on-chain as someone who found a seed phrase in a drawer, or a hacker who got hold of a key that was never theirs.

"The blockchain shows that someone could sign, but not who," Quickben22 wrote.

Even when a wallet really is lost, the owner doesn't always let go.

Welshman James Howells has spent more than a decade trying to recover a hard drive holding around 8,000 BTC, which his then-girlfriend accidentally threw out during an office clear-out in 2013. He traced it to a landfill in Newport, Wales, and spent years seeking permission to dig it up, until a judge dismissed his case on environmental grounds. Howells called the ruling a "stitch-up."

Last year, he offered the local council £2.5 million to buy the landfill outright, Decrypt reported. The council didn't respond. He has since given up the search and is instead launching Ceiniog, a Bitcoin layer-2 network with a token he says is backed by the buried coins he can't access.

“MY view is this: As long as YOU (or anyone else in the world) can view the balance [and can] verify for yourself that it still contains 8,000 Bitcoin, then [the] Ceiniog coin is fully backed with its value,” he told Decrypt last year. “If those coins ever moved, Ceiniog instantly loses its backing and value. I am guaranteeing that those coins will never move—because the private key is on a [hard drive] in a landfill.”

Howells' 8,000 BTC haven't moved since 2013. On paper, they look exactly like the coins on Quickben22's list, and the kind the Noah Doe lawsuit wants declared abandoned.

#4874. The 50 Best Thanksgiving Movies of All Time

Grab a bowl of popcorn and gobble up these picks.

OKAY, LET'S UNPACK just what the heck makes a Thanksgiving movie a "Thanksgiving movie" besides, just like, something set in November around a gluttonous dinner table. Sure, you’ve got your traditional gather-around-the-stuffing affairs like the classic A Charlie Brown Thanksgiving. But there’s gotta be something a little bit more to this genre than turkey aesthetics. There has to be more than just theatrics.

Ultimately, Thanksgiving is about colonial hegemony and fictional parley feasts used to sugarcoat genocidal invasion and ... whoa, sorry about that; you caught us reading from the Book of Uncomfortable Truths. While the historicity of the holiday—particularly that involving Pilgrims and Native Americans—is hogwash, the spirit of the celebration—gratitude found at the beginning of the harvest season, when food isn’t just a superfluous decoration but a means of winter survival—can be found in all cultures.

Which is why a “Thanksgiving movie” doesn’t have to be an American movie. (And why the most American Thanksgiving movie is probably The New World, if only for showing what those first harvest seasons were really like: violence, starvation, lots of sinning.)

That’s not to say we won’t throw on some fun movies to watch during Thanksgiving; we’re not all about historicity and jagged truth pills. We want to have fun as well. And, if you can’t decide what to watch, there’s always football. What’s the name of the team the Cowboys usually play, now?

Here are the best Thanksgiving movies to watch this season.

Halloween has Halloween. Christmas has Black Christmas. Now, horror director Eli Roth has given Thanksgiving its very own terrifying, fun, slasher film: Thanksgiving. Based on his own fake trailer of the same name (from the theatrical version of 2006's Quentin Tarantino/Robert Rodriguez double feature Grindhouse), this movie is just about everything horror fans could want: a whodunit mystery, very well made, solid acting (from Patrick Dempsey and Big Shot star Nell Verlaque), and some absolutely brutal violence. Do not watch if you have a weak stomach!

Sam Raimi's Spider-Man may have reinvented the superhero movie genre as we know it, but it's also a covert Thanksgiving movie where one of the movie's most vital scenes takes place when Norman Osborn (Willem Dafoe) starts to carve a turkey. A timeless film for many reasons!

If you’re still feeling some type of way after watching You Can See Everything, look no further for your next watch than 1999’s American Movie, which similarly played with the format, drama, and intention of what a documentary (and a movie in general) really is, and is trying to say. This portrait of the American dream follows a regular guy obsessed with making a low-budget horror movie, and brings everyone along for the ride. It also features a vital Thanksgiving sequence that marks one of the only points in the film where characters aren’t working on the aforementioned film.

This historical adventure film (okay, maybe historical is a stretch) just feels like a Thanksgiving movie. Does it not? And Nic Cage is in top form.

In the beautifully-animated Fantastic Mr. Fox, Wes Anderson made a film that's fantastic viewing (ba dum tsss) for all ages—with an orange-brown aesthetic that feels just right for Thanksgiving. The voices of George Clooney, Meryl Streep, Bill Murray, and more make this a ton of fun.

This family-focused dramedy about a family of writers stagnant in various stages of life is set around a couple particularly dramatic Thanksgiving dinners. With a cast led by Greg Kinnear, Jennifer Connelly, and more (and a very brief glimpse of a young Glen Powell!), you're certain to be entertained around Thanksgiving time with this one.

Judd Apatow's follow-up to Knocked Up was Funny People, a movie about an aging comedy superstar (Adam Sandler) who, when facing potentially bad medical news, decides to take on a young new writer (Seth Rogen) to jump-start his career and get some joy back into his life. In one particularly notable scene, the older comedian spends Thanksgiving with his younger employees’ friends—and it's a funny, unnerving, and super memorable moment.

While The Big Chill doesn't explicitly take place during Thanksgiving, it does feel like Thanksgiving, given that it's all about loved ones gathering and hashing out some long-simmering arguments (and features a memorable Thanksgiving flashback as well). The movie has a very simple premise: a group of former college friends come back together after 15 years following the suicide of their friend. Fun fact? The unseen friend marked one of the very first roles for Kevin Costner, who had his scenes cut. The movie's actual cast is great nonetheless, including Tom Berenger, Glenn Close, William Hurt, Kevin Kline, and more.

She's Gotta Have It is Spike Lee's very first movie, and, despite his impressive resume through the next 30+ years, still one of his best. The movie follows a woman named Nola Darling who's choosing between her three gentleman suitors, and in one of the most memorable scenes in the film, all four have Thanksgiving dinner together.

Ridley Scott and Denzel Washington made fireworks together in 2024 with Gladiator II, but their first time working together came in 2007 with the crime epic American Gangster. Telling the saga of Frank Lucas, Scott and Washington both do some of their great work to bring this portrait of a violent but altruistic man to life. The movie spans a long period of time, but one particularly memorable (and shocking) moment comes during the Thanksgiving holiday.

Everyone knows the silly premise of Robin Williams's star vehicle Mrs. Doubtfire—where a man creates a new persona as a British nanny to spend more time with his kids after a divorce—but it's also a great Thanksgiving movie. What's better for pre- or post-turkey viewing than a movie about going to absurd lengths for family?

This Denis Villeneuve movie starts with a pair of families coming together for Thanksgiving—but things take a stark turn from there when a daughter from each family goes missing after dinner. This is one of the most interesting and gripping mystery thrillers in recent memory, with a cast—Hugh Jackman, Jake Gyllenhaal, Viola Davis, Paul Dano—that all bring their A-games.

Little Women is a timeless Thanksgiving text (both the actual novel and the many adaptations), but we lean toward the most recent version, directed by Barbie's Greta Gerwig, because it's just so warm and cozy. With a great cast led by Saoirse Ronan and Florence Pugh, this should be an annual Thanksgiving staple.

This family-focused film follows four adopted brothers (Mark Wahlberg, Andre 3000, Garrett Hedlund, and Tyrese Gibson) as they return home for their mother's funeral—only to discover that she's been murdered. The revenge tour then begins. The late great director John Singleton was behind this one.

This existential horror (adapted from a play of the same name) finds Richard Jenkins and Steven Yeun as members of a family all coming together for a Thanksgiving in Manhattan. If you want some dread and discomfort for Thanksgiving dinner, this is the movie for you.

For Your Consideration is one of the very funny Christopher Guest films (a group that also includes This Is Spinal Tap, A Mighty Wind, and Best in Show, among others) that features the famed actor/comedian and his many usual collaborators, including Catherine O'Hara, Eugene Levy, Fred Willard, and more. O'Hara, here, plays an actress who gets swept up in the annual mania of movie awards season—and Thanksgiving winds up playing a major part in her potentially award-winning role.

As just about everyone remembers on Thanksgiving... families can be tough. Perhaps to put it even more bluntly, families can be annoying—and messy. That's on perfect display in Dan in Real Life, where Steve Carell plays a widower who returns to his family home for Thanksgiving to find that he's got a little romantic thing going with a woman (Juliette Binoche) who just happens to be his brother's (Dane Cook) girlfriend. Whoops!

Because, well, no one comforts us during the holidays quite like Peanuts, and this short special is almost as magical as the Christmas one.

Controversial opinion: The New World is the best Thanksgiving movie. The origins of Thanksgiving aren’t exactly warm and fuzzy, and Terrence Malick’s (Days of Heaven, Tree of Life) underappreciated 2005 masterpiece wrestles with the costs of the settling of Jamestown, Virginia, on all sides (while still being gorgeous to look at, this being Malick). Even the deeply romantic moments between John Smith and Pocahontas are rooted in a real sense of place, and backgrounded by historical peril. Just let everyone know this isn’t exactly a feel-good Turkey Day experience.

True to its title, The Ice Storm couldn’t really be icier. One of Ang Lee’s best movies, it transports us to the mind of young, moody Elijah Wood as Paul Hood and then from there to the tragic and sometimes kinky ’70s messiness of two suburban families set over a Thanksgiving weekend. There’s a key party and Sigourney Weaver, so the film kind of sells itself.

Paul Schrodt is a freelance writer and editor covering pop culture and the entertainment industry. He has contributed to The New York Times, The Wall Street Journal, GQ, Men's Health, The Hollywood Reporter, Los Angeles magazine, and others.

Joshua St Clair is an Assistant Editor at Men's Health Magazine.

#4872. USDT buyers face 22% VAT when Moscow Exchange opens crypto trading

Buyers will pay VAT on top of the trade price in December because Russia’s tax code has no explicit exemption for foreign digital instruments.

Buyers of Tether’s USDt (USDT) stablecoin will pay a 22% value-added tax (VAT) on top of the trade price when Moscow Exchange (MOEX) opens crypto trading on Dec. 1, according to the exchange’s clearinghouse.

The National Clearing Centre (NCC) said it would apply VAT to USDT purchases, sales and swaps through Dec. 31. A buyer purchasing 100,000 rubles worth of USDT would pay an additional 22,000 rubles in VAT.

Unlike Bitcoin (BTC) and Ether (ETH), which Russia classifies as digital currencies, USDT falls under a separate legal category known as foreign digital instruments. Russia’s tax code excludes sales of digital currencies from VAT, but it provides no explicit exemption for foreign digital instruments, according to the NCC.

The NCC said it had asked Russia’s Federal Tax Service and Finance Ministry for guidance. It said it would update market participants if it received clarification or if lawmakers exempted trades conducted before Jan. 1, 2027.

Bank of Russia rules that took effect Nov. 17 allow non-qualified investors to buy Bitcoin, Ether and USDT through intermediaries, with annual purchases capped at 300,000 rubles (about $3,650) per intermediary.

Related: Sanctions fears loom over Russia’s regulated crypto trading launch

#4870. Tennis is Aces for Healthy Aging

There’s good reason why the United States Tennis Association calls it “the world’s healthiest sport.”

If we told you tennis has been shown to boost heart health, balance, strength, mental acuity, and mood plus add nearly 10 years to your life, you might think we also had a bridge to sell you. But it’s true: Research shows that even recreational tennis can have a seriously impressive impact on your health—now and for years to come.

“Tennis is a whole-body sport,” says sports medicine surgeon Alexis Colvin, MD, chief medical officer of the US Open. “It offers cardiovascular benefits, muscle strengthening, flexibility—all the things you need to deal with any physical challenges later in life.”

You also don’t have to be a star tennis player to reap the longevity benefits, Dr. Colvin adds. In a 2020 study she co-authored, researchers surveyed over 10,000 tennis players, most of them recreational; about 70 percent played just one to three days a week. The players scored higher than the general population on measures such as physical strength, mental and emotional health, and social engagement. “You don't have to be playing at a very high level to get those benefits,” Dr. Colvin says.

And those benefits appear to extend one’s life: When researchers followed 8,577 people for up to 25 years, they found that those who played tennis an average of 103 minutes a week lived 9.7 years longer than their sedentary counterparts. They also lived significantly longer than cyclists (who get an extra 3.7 years), swimmers (3.4 years), and joggers (3.2 years).

Even if you’ve never played tennis before, it’s never too late to start. Keep reading for five reasons to pick up a racket, hit the court, and do your future self a favor.

Tennis involves bursts of high intensity movement—sprinting to the net, running backwards to lob a volley, stopping and then lunging to the opposite side of the court to hit the ball—all of which can do wonders for your cardiovascular health. It boosts heart rate, maximizes your body’s use of oxygen, strengthens heart muscle, improves circulation, and can lower blood pressure and cholesterol levels. As a result, women who play tennis an average two to three hours a week have a 44 percent lower risk of developing cardiovascular disease and a 66 percent lower chance of dying from it.

When you sprint across the court, swing a racquet, or come to a sudden stop, you’re working your leg muscles, core, shoulders, back, and arms—in other words, strengthening nearly every part of the body. A recent study of more than 5,000 women between the ages 63 and 99 found that those who had greater muscle strength lived the longest. And because you’re also supporting your own body weight as you run and land, the sport also helps increase bone density, Dr. Colvin says. Stronger bones mean a lower risk of fractures and osteoporosis in the future, which is what you need if you want to age well.

Tennis requires coordinated movement and muscle control to maintain fluid footwork and smooth, continuous swings. Your core helps make that possible and is key to good balance. Both core strength and balance can help you avoid falls and fractures as you get older. Another part of balance is visuospatial skill—the ability to recognize environmental cues that help you stay upright. Tennis may help sharpen that, too. In a 2022 study, older tennis players who faced a disruption that could throw them off balance were able to react and steady themselves as well as younger players.

Tennis demands intense focus and decision making. “There’s a lot of active thinking going on,” Dr. Colvin says. “You’re making a lot of split-second decisions while you’re playing, plus thinking about the overall strategy of the game. That kind of mental engagement is important as we get older.” According to research, engaging in that kind of quick and strategic thinking while being physically active may be particularly good at promoting neuroplasticity and boosting cognitive function—both of which can stave off the cognitive decline that can come with age.

Studies have found that people who play tennis regularly tend to be less stressed or anxious than people who do lower-intensity exercise. They also report feeling more optimistic, less depressed, and a greater sense of well-being. Like other high-energy sports, tennis may boost mood by prompting the release of brain chemicals such as endorphins, dopamine, and serotonin. It’s also a fun game to play with friends. “The social aspect of tennis is one of its key benefits,” Dr. Colvin says. Numerous studies show that those kinds of community ties are essential for a long, healthy, happier life. With tennis, that’s built into the game.

#4868. GTA 6 PC Ports ‘Nuked’ Just Days Before Devs Expected It to Be Playable

GTA 6 PC Ports ‘Nuked’ Just Days Before Devs Expected It to Be Playable

Several major projects attempting to port Grand Theft Auto 6 to PC have been "nuked," according to community insiders. It comes as developers thought a playable port was just days away, after notorious Rockstar hacker Cyberleek released the game's files.

Both the Leonida GitHub page and the GTA6Modding Discord server—two of the most serious attempts at porting the latest Grand Theft Auto title to PC—have been removed. Other servers also involved in the AI-powered porting scene, such as the Chasm Server, have also locked down.

"Leonida and GTA6Modding have both been nuked," Freakman, a Chasm Server mod, wrote on Discord. "We have no affiliation with Cyberleek," they added. "Multiple servers have been struck, we're staying low for tonight."

Cyberleek released an Xbox build of GTA 6 on November 19, the day the game launched on consoles. However, they held back the main executable needed to run it and promised they'd release it if the CYBERLEEK meme coin hit a $15 million market cap.

Porting projects had been preparing for that file, expecting it'd be the key to making the port playable. The scene leans heavily on AI coding tools to speed up the reverse engineering needed to get console games running on PC, with developers describing themselves as "vibe coders."

The meme coin missed its $15 million target, but Cyberleek released the executable on Monday anyway. The next day, several GTA 6 porting projects were shut down.

"They're taking out servers left and right that are mentioning the leak," Chasm community member Weedgoose told Decrypt. "Some servers take the hit while others are going dark and are waiting for the flames to extinguish."

Some of the impacted community members have moved to alternative messaging platforms that are more private than Discord, Weedgoose said.

It appears the Leonida GitHub was removed by its creator, an apparent high school student, as it shows a 404 error page rather than a DMCA takedown page. That said, there's no way of knowing if the creator received a cease and desist (or similar correspondence) that led to this decision.

Chasm mod Freakman did not respond to Decrypt's request for comment regarding why the server has gone dark.

Take-Two Interactive, Rockstar's parent company, has been going after the leaks for months. In August, it filed subpoenas asking Discord and Microsoft, which owns GitHub, to hand over data that could help identify Cyberleek.

Rockstar has yet to announce an official PC version of GTA 6 yet. GamesBeat reported in October that one could arrive in Q1 of 2027—and the prospect of an unofficial port releasing in advance of that would likely dent sales.

Rockstar did not respond to Decrypt's request for comment.

#4865. Morning Minute: Jensen Returns, Markets Soar (Except Crypto)

Morning Minute: Jensen Returns, Markets Soar (Except Crypto)

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

Six days after Nvidia’s board chair told investors “the Nvidia board does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO,” the board voted unanimously to reinstate him as president, CEO and director, effective immediately.

Nvidia shares rose 33% to around $167, adding roughly a trillion dollars of market value in a session. Huang’s personal net worth climbed about $36.6 billion on his 3.5% stake, moving him to 13th on the Forbes global list. Short sellers took roughly $12.2 billion in paper losses, their worst single day since at least 2019, which wiped out the $10.5 billion they had booked the prior week.

The stock market absolutely ripped on the news. The Nasdaq closed up 6.51% at 24,089, its best day since April 2025. The S&P gained 4.7%, the Dow was +3%, and the VIX collapsed 25.8% to 26.62.

Bitcoin went along with the ride—for a few hours. It touched $63,929 intraday, up 5.1%, then spent the afternoon handing it back and finished near $61,000, barely up on the day. The ETFs used the pump to exit more, with BTC ETFs seeing another $570M in outflows on the day.

ETH and other majors also closed red. Alts gave back their gains. And the CMC top 20 index managed just 0.20% gain.

That’s a 19 to 1 ratio between what the Nasdaq captured and what Bitcoin kept, on the same catalyst, on the same day. Pain for the crypto bulls.

What makes it even more painful, is that crypto fell with AI stocks the whole way down, and the standard explanation was that both trade on the same liquidity and the same risk appetite. Monday tested that and the correlation fell through. The AI trade got a catalyst and markets bounced. Crypto didn’t bounce with it.

As for Nvidia’s reversal decision itself, questions remain to say the least. The board says it was satisfied with Huang’s “candor and ability to lead,” and that input from senior U.S. officials left it confident his return “poses no national security risk.” Trump publicly pushed for it and said the board had sought assurance on the espionage allegations. What actually changed between November 17 and November 23 hasn’t been explained. The independent directors’ investigation is still open, and Nvidia is now standing up a board committee for national security and export-control oversight plus recruiting a chief compliance officer.

Perhaps answers will trickle out over time. The AI trade may not need them anyway. And crypto, well, it's in its own world right now, waiting for its own catalysts (likely quantum-related). But it’s very unclear that catalyst is coming in 2026, and it’s becoming clear that many are not interested in waiting for the answers…

#4863. How South Korea’s proposed 20% cap could change who runs Upbit

Naver Financial agreed to buy every share in Upbit’s operator, but a proposed ownership cap could strip away its voting rights on four-fifths of them.

When Naver struck its deal for Dunamu, the company behind crypto exchange Upbit, it had already worked out how to retain control of its financial arm.

Agreements with two Dunamu executives would give Naver 46.5% of the votes in Naver Financial despite owning just 17%. Control of Dunamu itself looked simpler, and Naver Financial would own it outright.

However, South Korea’s proposed ownership cap puts that second part of the deal in doubt. If lawmakers approve the bill, shares above the 20% cap would lose their votes.

Related: South Korea to permit crypto withdrawal freezes during hacking threats

With regulatory approval, a shareholder could vote up to 34%. The draft gives regulators no explicit power to order a sale, so Naver Financial could still hold every Dunamu share while losing the vote on most of them. That leaves investors in the $11.2 billion deal with a question the purchase price alone can’t answer.

Who would have the right to appoint the directors who oversee Upbit? The board and contractual rights Naver Financial retains could determine whether its parent can count Dunamu’s earnings in its accounts.

#4862. There Isn’t Just One Way to Have Oral Sex—These 12 Positions Will Boost Intimacy and Pleasure

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

If you’ve ever given or received oral sex (or done both at the same time), you probably have a position or two that usually work for you. And while it can be easy, convenient, and efficient to end up in the same configuration time after time, there are just as many ways to have oral sex as there are to have intercourse—and there’s a solid argument for shaking up your routine.

“The thing that happens, especially in long-term relationships, is people tend to revert to a default. And it tends to be the [position] that works most reliably and most quickly,” says Rachel Zar, PhD, LMFT, a sex therapist in Chicago. Now, there’s nothing wrong with a position that consistently helps you orgasm. But “our nervous systems adapt and become less excited by something that feels routine, or like the same thing over and over again,” she explains. “Sometimes, excitement can come from something not working most effectively—or getting to orgasm—but from something being new.”

While trying out new sex positions can certainly help you climax, novelty also produces feel-good hormones that benefit your overall health. Specifically, “it gives a dopamine release,” says Debra Laino, DHS, a sex therapist and clinical sexologist in Wilmington, Delaware. (This is only one of several health benefits of sex, btw.)

And novelty isn’t just good for your physical health—it can also boost your emotional connection with your partner. Trying new positions and angles allows you to keep learning more about each other’s bodies, which can benefit your relationship and sex life as you grow older together, says Jenn Gunsaullus, PhD, a sociologist and sexologist in San Diego.

First, consider who wants to be in control. Unequal power dynamics tend to play a role in most oral sex positions, since one partner (the giver) is naturally in service to the other (the receiver). But that’s part of the fun. In fact, “one of the things people enjoy about oral sex is the power dynamic,” says Zar. “That can be power from the person who’s giving or receiving, depending on how you frame it.”

She recommends thinking about what makes receiving oral so appealing to you, specifically: Do you enjoy the feeling of relaxing and letting go, or do you prefer feeling empowered and in control? And when you’re on the giving side, do you like the idea of your partner surrendering to you—or do you prefer being told what to do? Your answer might help you pinpoint the kind of position you’ll find hot, whether that’s face-sitting or the butterfly technique.

While the positions ahead can work for all couples and all kinds of oral sex, you may also want to choose one based on your and your partner’s specific body parts. If you’re giving a blow job, for instance, you might want to feel like you’re approaching the penis “from below” so you can control the depth and pace at which they’re thrusting into your mouth, says Zar. On the other hand, someone performing cunnilingus (or oral sex on a vulva) might want to make sure their partner’s legs are spread enough to give them access to the clitoris. (After all, over 36 percent of women with vulvas say that they need clitoral stimulation to orgasm.)

At Women’s Health, we aim to describe sexual experiences as inclusively as possible. To avoid making assumptions about gender or anatomy, we use non-gendered language throughout this article. The “giving partner” or “giver” refers to the partner performing oral sex, whether on a penis, vulva, or anus. The “receiving partner” or “receiver” refers to the partner receiving oral sex. We encourage readers to modify any position to suit their bodies, preferences, and needs.

If the receiving partner has a penis, the giver may choose to kneel or lie beside them for maximum comfort.

Benefits: No matter how the giver situates their body, this beginner-friendly position is great for people who are new to having sex with each other, says Gunsaullus: There’s a lot of opportunity for eye contact, which can help the receiver provide verbal and nonverbal feedback (and a sexy visual). Plus, the giver has total control over what they’re doing here, which can be especially useful during fellatio (i.e., oral sex on a penis). For example, if the giving partner has a sensitive gag reflex, they can choose the depth and pace that allows them to perform oral comfortably.

Some receivers might prefer the way this configuration feels, while others enjoy keeping their legs spread out wide (more on that in a sec). Benefits: Receivers with vulvas and penises can try this move, but for givers performing cunnilingus, this position grants you access to even more parts of your partner’s vulva.
